[F10] [math:lem-c-one-diffeomorphisms-map-lebesgue-measurable-sets-to-lebesgue-measurable-sets](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-c-one-diffeomorphisms-map-lebesgue-measurable-sets-to-lebesgue-measurable-sets) says, under Countable Choice, that a $C^1$ diffeomorphism between open subsets of $\mathbb R^n$ maps Lebesgue measurable sets to Lebesgue measurable sets. Applying it to the inverse diffeomorphism gives measurable preimages and hence measurability of compositions of real or complex Lebesgue measurable functions with the chart maps.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish the two bounded endpoint maps and their restriction identity.

1.1 The given graph formulas make $\Phi$ and $\Psi$ smooth inverses on the ambient open sets $W,V$, with $|\det D\Phi|=|\det D\Psi|=1$. Smoothness on neighborhoods of $\overline U$ and $\overline C$ bounds both first derivatives there. The exact one-sided identity gives $\Omega\cap U=\Psi(C_+)$. Thus $\Psi:C_+\to\Omega\cap U$ and $\Phi:U\to C$ are $C^1$ diffeomorphisms between open sets, as are their inverses. AC supplies Countable Choice for [F8]–[F10]. For any such restriction $S:D_1\to D_2$, [F10] applied to $S^{-1}$ makes preimages of Lebesgue measurable sets measurable; in particular $w\circ S$ is measurable for every real or complex measurable $w$ on $D_2$. If two representatives differ on a null set $N\subset D_2$, their compositions differ only on $S^{-1}(N)$, which is null by [F9] applied to $S^{-1}$. Finally [F8] applied to the nonnegative measurable function $|w|^q$ gives $\|w\circ S\|_{L^q(D_1)}^q=\|w\|_{L^q(D_2)}^q$, since $|\det DS|=1$. This proves the chart composition assertions for arbitrary $L^q$ classes in both directions on the positive and doubled patches. [F1, F6, F7, F8, F9, F10, given]

2.1 Apply [F2] with $k=1$, $p=q$ on matched compactly contained subpatches of $C_+$ and $\Omega\cap U$. Every compact weak test support is contained in such a pair, so the weak chain rule holds throughout $C_+$, and in the reverse direction throughout $\Omega\cap U$. In particular $\partial_i(w\circ\Psi)=\sum_{j=1}^n((\partial_j w)\circ\Psi)\partial_i\Psi_j$ there. Integrating this finite sum using the fixed first-derivative bounds and the $L^q$ identity in step 1.1 for each $\partial_jw$ gives $\|w\circ\Psi\|_{W^{1,q}(C_+)}\le C\|w\|_{W^{1,q}(\Omega\cap U)}$. The reverse composition bound follows in the same way. The same reasoning on $C,U$ gives the doubled-chart Sobolev composition bounds. These are restrictions of the same well-defined $L^q$ compositions from step 1.1. [F1, F2, F6, F7, F8, step 1.1]

3.1 Multiplication by $\rho$ and steps 1.1 and 2.1 bound $g=(\rho u)\circ\Psi$ on $C_+$ at both endpoints. It vanishes almost everywhere outside $K\cap C_+$, so it misses the artificial lateral and top edges. Here is the weak test argument for its zero extension $f=L_\rho u$ to $H_+$. Choose $\theta\in C_c^\infty(C)$ equal to $1$ near $K$. For any $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(H_+)$, $\theta\varphi\in C_c^\infty(C_+)$, because $\varphi$ stays away from $t=0$. The weak derivatives of $g$ vanish on the open set $C_+\setminus K$: there $g=0$ and the weak test identity and uniqueness of its derivative classes give zero derivatives. Consequently the terms containing derivatives of $\theta$ vanish, and the weak identity for $g$ tested against $\theta\varphi$ becomes $\int_{H_+}f\,\partial_j\varphi=-\int_{H_+}E_+(\partial_jg)\varphi$, where $E_+$ denotes zero extension from $C_+$ to $H_+$. This proves the partial extension with unchanged component norms. No test crosses the actual boundary $t=0$ and no zero boundary condition is assumed. Thus $L_\rho$ has the asserted $L^q$ and $W^{1,q}$ bounds. [F3, F5, F6, step 1.1, step 2.1]

4.1 By [F4], $Rf$ is bounded at both endpoints and vanishes almost everywhere outside $K$, since the positive support lies in $\overline{B_0}\times[0,b]$. In particular its reflected support remains compactly inside $C$. Pull it back to $U$ by $\Phi$, multiply by $\psi$, and extend by zero outside $U$. Steps 1.1 and 2.1, [F3] and [F5] give the asserted bounds for this same $T_{\rho,\psi}$ formula, because the multiplied support is compactly inside $U$. On $\Omega\cap U$ the coordinate lies in $C_+$, so reflection is the identity and $T_{\rho,\psi}u=\psi\rho u$ there. Outside $U$ both sides vanish. If $\psi=1$ on $\operatorname{supp}\rho$, this is $\rho u$ throughout $\Omega$; [F3] supplies such a $\psi$. All operations are linear on almost-everywhere classes, and the $W^{1,q}$ maps are restrictions of the same $L^q$ formulas. The geometric data and formulas do not involve $q$, proving the final assertion. [F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 3.1] ∎



## A compactly supported common L²/H² extension from a bounded smooth domain

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-bounded-common-endpoint-extension) · level 1


### Statement

Assume AC. Let $G\subset\mathbb R^n$ be bounded with smooth boundary, and let $V$ be a fixed open neighborhood of $\overline G$. There is one linear map $E_G:L^2(G)\to L^2(\mathbb R^n)$, independent of an endpoint choice, with $r_G E_G=I$, bounded also $W^{2,2}(G)\to W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n)$, such that $\operatorname{supp}E_Gu\subset K\Subset V$ for one fixed compact set $K$. Moreover the weak space $W^{2,2}(G)$ equals the restriction quotient of whole-space $H^2(\mathbb R^n)$ with equivalent norms; at order zero the restriction quotient is isometrically $L^2(G)$.

Here $n\ge1$, $G$ is nonempty and open, and smooth boundary means the one-sided graph convention at every finite order, including the finite-union-of-intervals convention when $n=1$. Fix $\mathbb K\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$; all maps and weak spaces are over $\mathbb K$. The weak spaces and their derivative-sum norms are those of the integer Sobolev definition. Whole-space $H^j(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$, $j\in\{0,2\}$, is the Bessel-potential completion, identified by the integer Fourier theorem with its unique $L^2$ class; its norm is $\|\langle\xi\rangle^j\mathcal F_2F\|_2$ with $\langle\xi\rangle=(1+|\xi|^2)^{1/2}$. For real scalars, take its real-valued $L^2$ subspace with this inherited norm. Thus $H^0(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb K)=L^2(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb K)$ isometrically.

Precisely, $r_GF=F|_G$ is restriction of the $L^2$ class, and the endpoint restriction space and its infimum norm mean
$$\mathcal R_j(G;\mathbb K)=\{r_GF:F\in H^j(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb K)\}\subseteq L^2(G;\mathbb K),\qquad \|u\|_{\mathcal R_j}=\inf_{\substack{F\in H^j(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb K)\\r_GF=u\ {\rm a.e.}}}\|F\|_{H^j},\quad j\in\{0,2\}.$$
Equality of restrictions is always equality almost everywhere on $G$. Equivalently, this is the quotient by the kernel of $r_G$. The assertions above are $\mathcal R_2(G;\mathbb K)=W^{2,2}(G;\mathbb K)$ with equivalent norms and $\mathcal R_0(G;\mathbb K)=L^2(G;\mathbb K)$ isometrically.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available. AC selects a graph chart at each boundary point before the finite subcover is taken; its countable instance is inherited through the Sobolev, weak-chain and Fourier interfaces. The subsequent finite cutoffs and the explicit reflection require no further choice principle.

[F2] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-flat-second-order-reflection](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-flat-second-order-reflection) supplies one flat formula bounded at both the zeroth and second weak-derivative endpoints and preserving the smaller-chart support.

[F3] [Bounded $C^k$ domains and boundary charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-c-k-domain-and-boundary-charts) supplies the one-sided smooth graph charts and their compact-patch derivative bounds. [math:thm-heine-borel-rn](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-heine-borel-rn) makes $\overline G$ and $\partial G$ compact. Step 1.1 below derives the finite nested chart cover from these inputs. [math:lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization) supplies cutoffs and subordinate partitions. The construction and matched-half-patch exhaustion in proof steps 1.1, 2.1 and 2.2 of [Bounded $C^k$ domains admit integer-order Sobolev extension](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-extension-theorem-for-bounded-smooth-domains) are applicable with $k=p=2$; we use their localization with the explicit common reflection, without asserting that separate endpoint extension operators agree.

[F4] [$C^k$ boundary flattening preserves local $W^{k,p}$](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-c-k-boundary-flattening-preserves-wkp-locally) gives the weak chain formula for $k=2$ and change of variables on fixed compact patches, [math:lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor) gives cutoff derivative bounds through order two, and [math:lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp) gives zero extension of the compact interior and compact ambient-chart pieces.

[F5] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies $W^{j,2}(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$ with the Bessel-potential $H^j$ via the unique regular-distribution $L^2$ representative, for $j=0,2$. Its norms are equivalent at $j=2$ and equal at $j=0$, with the prescribed $2\pi$ Fourier normalization. Restricting this identification to real-valued classes gives the stated real convention with the same bounds.

[F6] [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm) defines the weak spaces over $\mathbb R$ and $\mathbb C$ as almost-everywhere $L^2$ classes with all weak derivatives through the stated integer order in $L^2$, with the derivative-sum norm and $W^{0,2}=L^2$. [math:def-real-order-bessel-potential-sobolev-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-real-order-bessel-potential-sobolev-space) defines whole-space $H^j$ as the weighted-Schwartz completion; F5 supplies the $L^2$ identification used in the local definition of $\mathcal R_j$.

[F7] [math:lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces) gives restriction to every open subset as a contraction on $W^{k,p}$ for every admitted order and exponent, over either scalar field, and identifies the restricted weak derivatives. We use $k=0,2$ and $p=2$.

### Proof

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement under the stated hypotheses.

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

1.1 At each $x\in\partial G$, choose a graph chart in the convention of F3, reversing its normal coordinate so that $G$ maps to $t>0$. Since its flattened image is open and contains the image of $x$, choose three nested doubled cylinders centered there, with successively larger positive tangential and normal radii, whose outer closure lies inside that image and whose inverse image lies compactly in $V$. For $n=1$ only the normal intervals occur. The inverse images of the innermost cylinders form a boundary cover; compactness of $\partial G$ gives finitely many. The compact set left in $\overline G$ after removing these innermost patches misses $\partial G$ and lies in $G$, so finitely many balls with closures in $G$ give a relatively compact interior open set covering it. Apply the partition supplier to the resulting finite cover of a neighborhood $N$ of $\overline G$. Choose a compact neighborhood of $\overline G$ in $N$; local finiteness leaves only finitely many partition pieces meeting it. Group those by cover member and extend their compactly supported sums by zero outside $N$. The resulting ambient smooth $\rho_0,\ldots,\rho_m$ sum to $1$ on $G$, with $\operatorname{supp}\rho_0\Subset G$ and the other supports compactly in the innermost patches. The intervening and outer cylinders provide the positive margins required by the common reflection. Choose $\psi_i$ compactly supported in each outer chart and equal to $1$ near $\operatorname{supp}\rho_i$. All these choices precede $u$, and their finitely many supports lie compactly in $V$. [F1, F2, F3, given]

2.1 For $i>0$ flatten $\rho_i u$ to the positive half-cylinder and extend it by zero across only the lateral and top artificial edges, as a function $f_i$ on $H_+$. At order zero, change of variables bounds this map on $L^2$. At order two the weak chain rule has first derivatives bounded by a constant times the first input derivatives, and second derivatives bounded by a constant times the first and second input derivatives; these constants depend on the bounded first and second derivatives of the chart and its inverse on the larger closed patch. To handle the half-patch reaching $t=0$, apply the local chain formula on an exhaustion of matched interior subpatches. The same ambient bounds apply on every subpatch; all tests are covered and increasing the integrals gives the norm bound on the whole half-patch. The localized function vanishes near the artificial edges, so tests in $H_+$ prove that its zero extension across those edges has the corresponding weak derivatives extended by zero. No test in $H_+$ crosses the true boundary $t=0$. [F3, F4, F6, F7, step 1.1]

3.1 Apply the common reflection to $f_i$, pull the result back to the larger doubled chart, multiply by $\psi_i$ and extend the product by zero outside the ambient chart. Call this $E_i u$. The support assertion of the flat lemma and the chosen chart margins keep the reflected support in the larger chart. Chart, reflection and cutoff bounds give $\|E_i u\|_2\le C_i\|u\|_{L^2(G)}$ and $\|E_i u\|_{W^{2,2}}\le C_i^{\prime}\|u\|_{W^{2,2}(G)}$. The same operations act on $L^2$ representatives and on their weakly differentiable subclass; all coordinate maps preserve null sets. On $G$, the reflected positive side is unchanged and $\psi_i\rho_i=\rho_i$, so $r_GE_i u=\rho_i u$. [F2, F3, F4, step 2.1]

4.1 Let $E_0u$ be the zero extension of $\rho_0u$ and define $E_Gu=E_0u+\sum_{i=1}^mE_i u$. This is one linear formula on all of $L^2(G)$; the preceding estimates give both endpoint bounds for its restriction to $W^{2,2}(G)$. Restriction gives $r_GE_Gu=u$. Its support is contained in the fixed union of the compact interior and ambient-chart cutoffs, so the same compact $K\Subset V$ works for every input. [F3, F4, step 1.1, step 3.1, algebra]

5.1 If $u=r_GF\in\mathcal R_2(G;\mathbb K)$, F5 identifies $F$ with its weak $W^{2,2}$ class and F7 restricts that class and each of its derivatives to $G$, giving $\|u\|_{W^{2,2}(G)}\le C\|F\|_{H^2}$. Conversely, if $u\in W^{2,2}(G;\mathbb K)$, step 4.1 and F5 give the whole-space extension $E_Gu\in H^2$ with $\|E_Gu\|_{H^2}\le C'\|u\|_{W^{2,2}(G)}$. In the real case the same construction preserves real values, so this extension also lies in the specified real subspace. Infimizing the first bound over exactly the extensions in the definition of $\mathcal R_2$ and using $E_Gu$ for the second gives $C^{-1}\|u\|_{W^{2,2}(G)}\le\|u\|_{\mathcal R_2}\le C'\|u\|_{W^{2,2}(G)}$. This proves equality of the spaces and equivalence of norms; definiteness of the infimum follows also from $\|u\|_{L^2(G)}\le\|F\|_{H^2}$, while homogeneity and the triangle inequality follow by scaling and adding extensions and then taking infima. At order zero F5 and F7 give $\|u\|_2\le\|F\|_{H^0}$ for every extension. Extension by zero of any $L^2(G)$ class has exactly the same $L^2=H^0$ norm, since its squared integral outside $G$ is zero. Thus every such class belongs to $\mathcal R_0$ and its infimum norm is exactly $\|u\|_2$. [F5, F6, F7, step 4.1, algebra] ∎


## Non-trapping obstacle

[Open item](item-def-se-nontrapping) · level 2


### Definition

Let $n\ge2$, and let $\Theta\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a nonempty compact obstacle with smooth boundary and connected exterior $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$. A complete generalized ray is the spatial projection $x:\mathbb R\to\overline\Omega$, parametrized by physical time at unit speed, of a complete admissible generalized bicharacteristic in the exterior, including reflected and glancing boundary behavior.

The obstacle is **non-trapping** if every such complete ray leaves every compact subset of $\mathbb R^n$ in finite forward time: for each ray $x(t)$ and each compact set $K\subset\mathbb R^n$, there is a finite $t\ge0$ with $x(t)\notin K$. The time may depend on both the ray and $K$. This is the non-trapping part of the article's standing hypothesis (H).

### Definition references

[def-se-geometry](item-def-se-geometry)

[prop-se-generalized-ray-contract](item-prop-se-generalized-ray-contract)

### Justification

The geometry and the meanings of compactness, smooth boundary and connected exterior are fixed by [def-se-geometry](item-def-se-geometry). The generalized-ray convention is fixed independently by [prop-se-generalized-ray-contract](item-prop-se-generalized-ray-contract): it includes complete compressed reflected and glancing curves and identifies their spatial projections. The quantified curves have time domain $\mathbb R$; the escape test uses their forward restrictions. A curve defined only on a finite interval or on $[0,\infty)$ is not automatically a complete ray in this predicate: applying the predicate to that curve requires a complete extension. The condition above is therefore a definite predicate on the obstacle and those admissible rays. It asks for a finite escape time separately for each ray and compact set; this definition does not assert a common escape time, uniqueness at infinite-order contact, or a geometric control theorem.

### Source notes

The article's footnote on page 2 describes reflected geometric-optics rays leaving compact sets in finite time, and Remark 1 uses generalized bicharacteristics. The article is cited for attribution; the ray convention used here is supplied by the independent prerequisite.


## General Banach-triple Aubin–Lions–Simon compactness

[Open item](item-thm-se-general-aubin-lions-simon-input) · level 2


### Statement

Assume Countable Choice. Let $X,B,Y$ be Banach spaces over the same scalar field $\mathbb K\in\{\mathbb R,\mathbb C\}$, with $X$ compactly embedded into $B$ and $B$ continuously embedded into $Y$, and let $0<T<\infty$ and $1\le p<\infty$. A family bounded in $L^p(0,T;X)$ whose distributional time derivatives are bounded in $L^1(0,T;Y)$ is relatively compact in $L^p(0,T;B)$.

Relative compactness means that the closure of the family in the displayed $L^p(0,T;B)$ norm is compact. The embeddings are compatible injective linear maps; the time spaces consist of strongly measurable almost-everywhere classes for Lebesgue measure on $(0,T)$, with Bochner norms. The derivative is represented by a strongly measurable $Y$-valued integrable function satisfying the Bochner distributional identity against scalar smooth compactly supported time tests.

### Statement references

[math:def-countable-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-countable-choice)

[def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple](item-def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple)

[lem-author-ab209e1f475e-bochner-lp-inclusion](item-lem-author-ab209e1f475e-bochner-lp-inclusion)

[def-author-ab209e1f475e-banach-valued-time-derivative](item-def-author-ab209e1f475e-banach-valued-time-derivative)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** Countable Choice, the three Banach spaces over the same scalar field, $0<T<\infty$ and the finite exponent in the Statement; boundedness means a common finite supremum of the displayed Bochner norms. The localized complex Sobolev example in [F2] and [F4] additionally assumes the Axiom of Choice.

**Goal:** Record the independently established general compactness input and its exact applicability.

[F1] Under Countable Choice ([math:def-countable-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-countable-choice)) and $0<T<\infty$, the compatibility maps in [def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple](item-def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple) identify every $u\in L^p(0,T;X)$ as a $B$-valued and $Y$-valued class by [lem-author-ab209e1f475e-bochner-lp-inclusion](item-lem-author-ab209e1f475e-bochner-lp-inclusion). On this finite interval it is also in $L^1(0,T;Y)$. Thus the identity defining its derivative in [def-author-ab209e1f475e-banach-valued-time-derivative](item-def-author-ab209e1f475e-banach-valued-time-derivative) is meaningful. Countable Choice is retained to meet these helper contracts; no necessity assertion for Simon's theorem is made.

[F2] For the localized Sobolev application assume the Axiom of Choice ([math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)), which includes Countable Choice. Let $G\subset\mathbb R^n$, $n\ge1$, be a nonempty bounded open set with smooth boundary, $B=L^2(G;\mathbb C)$ and $Y=(H_0^2(G))^{\prime}$, with the full weak-derivative $H^2$ norm and conjugate-dual convention of [lem-author-ab209e1f475e-local-negative-pivot-injection](item-lem-author-ab209e1f475e-local-negative-pivot-injection). That supplier makes $Y$ Banach and the $L^2$ pivot an injective continuous linear map $B\to Y$. The space $B$ is Banach by [math:lem-l-two-with-the-integral-pairing-is-a-hilbert-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-l-two-with-the-integral-pairing-is-a-hilbert-space). If a complex Banach space $X$ embeds compactly into $B$, these maps give the triple in [def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple](item-def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple).

[F3] At $p=2$, a bound $\sup_u\|u\|_{L^2(0,T;X)}<\infty$ and strongly measurable distributional derivative representatives with $\sup_u\|\partial_tu\|_{L^\infty(0,T;Y)}\le M<\infty$ give the exact derivative hypothesis $\sup_u\|\partial_tu\|_{L^1(0,T;Y)}\le TM$ by [lem-author-ab209e1f475e-finite-time-derivative-conversion](item-lem-author-ab209e1f475e-finite-time-derivative-conversion), under the Countable Choice and positive finite interval already assumed. The derivative remains valued in the local negative space; it is not identified with the exterior Dirichlet spectral negative scale.

[F4] In [F2] define $X=H^{1/2}(G)$ by the restriction convention of [math:def-nitsche-restriction-bessel-potential-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-nitsche-restriction-bessel-potential-sobolev-spaces), applicable to this nonempty open set with $s=1/2$. Thus its elements are restrictions of complex whole-space Bessel-potential completion classes, with norm $\|u\|_X=\inf_{U|_G=u}\|U\|_{H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^n)}$. This $X$ is Banach: the whole-space space is complete by [math:cor-bessel-potential-spaces-are-hilbert-and-complete](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-bessel-potential-spaces-are-hilbert-and-complete), and restriction $R:H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^n)\to L^2(G)$ is linear with $\|RU\|_2\le\|U\|_{H^{1/2}}$ by the restriction definition's $L^2$ realization. Its kernel $N$ is a linear subspace and is closed: if $U_j\in N$ and $U_j\to U$ in the whole-space norm, the bound gives $\|RU\|_2\le\|U-U_j\|_{H^{1/2}}\to0$. The quotient by $N$ is Banach by [math:thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach), and $U+N\mapsto RU$ is a linear bijection onto $X$ with exactly the displayed quotient norm. Completeness therefore transfers to $X$. All three cited inputs require only Countable Choice, available under [F2]. Consequently one may take this $X$ once its bounded-domain compact embedding into $L^2(G)$ has independently been supplied; no global exterior compact embedding is asserted here.

### External input

**Imported result:** Simon, §8, assumption (8.1), printed p.84, and Corollary 4, first clause, printed p.85 (scanned PDF leaf 11). For Banach spaces with compatible compact $X\hookrightarrow B$ and continuous $B\hookrightarrow Y$ embeddings, a family bounded in $L^p(0,T;X)$ whose derivatives are bounded in $L^1(0,T;Y)$ is relatively compact in $L^p(0,T;B)$, for $0<T<\infty$ and $1\le p<\infty$. This is an external mathematical input, not a locally reconstructed proof of the general theorem.

**Application of the contract:** Under the stated choice and interval hypotheses, [F1] matches Simon's Banach-triple and distributional-derivative hypotheses using [def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple](item-def-author-ab209e1f475e-compact-banach-triple), [lem-author-ab209e1f475e-bochner-lp-inclusion](item-lem-author-ab209e1f475e-bochner-lp-inclusion) and [def-author-ab209e1f475e-banach-valued-time-derivative](item-def-author-ab209e1f475e-banach-valued-time-derivative). In the local example, [F2] supplies the Banach space $B$ by [math:lem-l-two-with-the-integral-pairing-is-a-hilbert-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-l-two-with-the-integral-pairing-is-a-hilbert-space) and the Banach space $Y$ and pivot injection by [lem-author-ab209e1f475e-local-negative-pivot-injection](item-lem-author-ab209e1f475e-local-negative-pivot-injection), under the additional Axiom of Choice ([math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)). For the specific fractional choice, [F4] supplies the definition and Banach property of $X$ using [math:def-nitsche-restriction-bessel-potential-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-nitsche-restriction-bessel-potential-sobolev-spaces), [math:cor-bessel-potential-spaces-are-hilbert-and-complete](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-bessel-potential-spaces-are-hilbert-and-complete) and [math:thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach). Its compact embedding is still a separate condition. With that condition, the $L^2(X)$ bound and the derivative bound in [F3], [lem-author-ab209e1f475e-finite-time-derivative-conversion](item-lem-author-ab209e1f475e-finite-time-derivative-conversion) gives the required $L^1(Y)$ bound. Applying the imported clause then yields relative compactness in $L^2(0,T;L^2(G))$. Countable Choice ([math:def-countable-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-countable-choice)) meets the general helper hypotheses. The general Statement itself requires exactly the displayed $L^1(Y)$ derivative norm, including $p=1$, and is not restricted to an evolution triple.

**Actual source reading:** In this adjudication the independent original PDF was opened online and its retained scanned leaf 11, printed pp.84–85, was visually read for (8.1) and the finite-$p$ clause of Corollary 4. The general compactness proof, surrounding intermediate proofs and earlier theorems were not reconstructed or fully checked. The separate $p=\infty$ clause is not used. The elementary closed-kernel application in [F4] is local reasoning from the declared library inputs, rather than part of the imported Simon statement.


## Collar and tail assembly of the exterior graph domain

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-exterior-graph-domain) · level 2


### Statement

Assume AC and let $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$ be a smooth compact-obstacle exterior, $n\ge2$. Then
$$\{u\in H^1_0(\Omega):\Delta u\in L^2(\Omega)\}=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega).$$
For the shifted form operator $A=I-\Delta_D$ on this domain,
$$C^{-1}\|u\|_{H^2(\Omega)}\le\|Au\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\le C\|u\|_{H^2(\Omega)}.$$
The constants may depend on the fixed exterior geometry, but no non-trapping hypothesis is needed.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-weak-dirichlet-form](item-lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-weak-dirichlet-form) identifies $D(A)$ with the weak Dirichlet domain and gives $\|u\|_{H^1}\le\|Au\|_2$.

[F3] [lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-bounded-collar-h2](item-lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-bounded-collar-h2) gives $\eta u\in H^2(G)$ with a bound by $\|\Delta u\|_2+\|u\|_{H^1}$.

[F4] [lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-tail-distribution-extension](item-lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-tail-distribution-extension) extends $(1-\eta)u$ with its distributional Laplacian, and [lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-whole-space-h2](item-lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-whole-space-h2) then gives its global $H^2$ regularity.

[F5] Write $H^k(U)=W^{k,2}(U;\mathbb C)$, $k=1,2$, using the weak derivatives and sum-of-squares norms of [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm). The space $H^1_0(U)=W^{1,2}_0(U;\mathbb C)$ is the $H^1$ norm closure of $C_c^\infty(U;\mathbb C)$ by [math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure). Their Countable Choice hypothesis is supplied by the AC assumption in [F1].

[F6] [math:lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization) supplies a smooth compactly supported cutoff equal to one near a compact subset of an open Euclidean set, and a locally finite smooth partition subordinate to any open cover. On a compact test support only finitely many members of that partition contribute.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove the conclusions in the statement under its stated hypotheses.

1.1 Choose $L>0$ with $\Theta\Subset B_L$ and, by [F6] with compact set $\Theta\subset B_L$, choose $\eta\in C_c^\infty(B_L)$ equal to $1$ near $\Theta$. Put $G=B_L\setminus\Theta$, exactly as in [F3]. Let $u$ belong to the weak domain and put $g=\Delta u$. The collar lemma gives $w=\eta u\in H^2(G)$ and $\|w\|_{H^2(G)}\le C(\|g\|_2+\|u\|_{H^1})$. Define $\widetilde w=w$ on $G$ and zero on $\Omega\setminus G$. The two open sets $G$ and $\Omega\setminus\operatorname{supp}\eta$ cover $\Omega$. On the second set $\widetilde w$ is identically zero, and on the overlap $w$ and all its weak derivatives through order two vanish. For each compactly supported test on $\Omega$, a subordinate smooth partition from [F6] has only finitely many terms meeting its support. Applying the weak derivative identities on each open piece and summing proves the global weak identities; thus $\widetilde w\in H^2(\Omega)$, with each derivative given by zero extension of the corresponding derivative on $G$ and with unchanged component norms. This extends only across the artificial sphere, not across the obstacle. [F3, F5, F6, given, construct]

2.1 The tail lemma and whole-space Fourier lemma give a function $v\in H^2(\mathbb R^n)$ with $v|_\Omega=(1-\eta)u$ and $\|v\|_{H^2}\le C(\|g\|_2+\|u\|_{H^1})$. Adding the extended collar part to $v|_\Omega$ gives $u\in H^2(\Omega)$ and $\|u\|_{H^2}\le C(\|g\|_2+\|u\|_{H^1})$. Conversely $u\in H^2\cap H^1_0$ has $\Delta u\in L^2$ by its weak derivatives, so the two domains coincide. [F3, F4, F5, step 1.1]

3.1 For $u\in D(A)$, the weak form bound gives $\|u\|_2\le\|u\|_{H^1}\le\|Au\|_2$, while $g=u-Au$ gives $\|g\|_2\le2\|Au\|_2$. Substitution in the preceding estimate proves $\|u\|_{H^2}\le C\|Au\|_2$. In the other direction $\|Au\|_2=\|u-\Delta u\|_2\le C_n\|u\|_{H^2}$. These are the stated graph-norm comparisons. [F2, F5, step 2.1, algebra] ∎


## Spectral cutoff dual tests and the exact interpolation lower bound

[Open item](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-strip-duality) · level 2


### Statement

Assume AC. Let $T\ge I$ be self-adjoint on a nonzero complex Hilbert space $K$, with first-variable-linear inner product, and use the admissible strip class for $(K,D(T))$ and the exact domain norm $\|Tv\|_K$. If $F$ is admissible and $M_j=\sup_t\|F(j+it)\|_{X_j}$, then $F(\theta)\in D(T^\theta)$ and $\|T^\theta F(\theta)\|\le M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta$, for $0<\theta<1$. Thus $[K,D(T)]_\theta=D(T^\theta)$ isometrically, including for the strip convention with vanishing boundary values.

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] The strip class, sum norm, complex-power convention and exact upper inequality are as defined in the spectral-profile lemma [lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-analytic-strip-profiles](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-analytic-strip-profiles).

[F2] Spectral cutoffs, multiplier norm integrals and monotone convergence characterize the domain of T to the power theta [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus).

[F3] Scalar three-lines applies to bounded continuous scalar strip functions holomorphic in the interior, including zero endpoint bounds [math:thm-hadamard-three-lines](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-hadamard-three-lines).

[F4] AC is retained for the spectral suppliers [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

[F5] A complex Hilbert space is complete for its inner-product norm [math:def-hilbert-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-hilbert-space). Under the assumed AC, self-adjoint means that the densely defined operator equals its adjoint, with equal domains and values [math:def-symmetric-self-adjoint-and-essentially-self-adjoint](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-symmetric-self-adjoint-and-essentially-self-adjoint).

[F6] For the first-variable-linear inner product, Cauchy–Schwarz gives $|\langle u,v\rangle|\le\|u\|\|v\|$ for all $u,v\in K$ [math:thm-cauchy-schwarz-for-real-and-complex-inner-product-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-cauchy-schwarz-for-real-and-complex-inner-product-spaces).

### Proof

**Given:** The data and AC in the statement.

**Goal:** All asserted identifications and bounds, with the stated exact norms.

1.1 Let $P_N=E_T([1,N])$, $h\in K$, and $\phi_N(z)=\langle F(z),T^{\overline z}P_Nh\rangle$. Since $T^{\overline z}P_Nh$ is norm-antiholomorphic (the logarithm is bounded on the cutoff), this is scalar holomorphic inside and continuous on $S$. By Cauchy–Schwarz its modulus is bounded on all $S$ by $N\|h\|\sup_S\|F(z)\|_K$. At $z=it$ its modulus is at most $M_0\|h\|$, since imaginary powers are unitary. At $z=1+it$, the self-adjoint multiplier product gives $\phi_N(1+it)=\langle TF(1+it),T^{-it}P_Nh\rangle$, so the bound is $M_1\|h\|$. All scalar three-lines hypotheses, including boundedness throughout the strip rather than just the boundary, have now been verified. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6]

2.1 Three-lines yields $|\langle T^\theta P_NF(\theta),h\rangle|\le M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta\|h\|$. Taking $h$ to be the normalized vector on the left when it is nonzero gives $\|T^\theta P_NF(\theta)\|\le M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta$. The squares of these norms increase to $\int_{[1,\infty)}\lambda^{2\theta}\,d(E_T)_{F(\theta)}$. Their uniform bound makes this integral finite; the exact Borel-calculus domain formula proves the membership and inequality. Taking the infimum over $F$ gives the lower inequality; the upper inequality from the profile lemma proves equality, and hence definiteness and completeness of this interpolation norm. No density assertion about analytic functions and no preexisting operator interpolation theorem is used. [F1, F2, F3, F5, step 1.1] ∎


## A single collar-reflection and free-tail exterior extension

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-single-exterior-extension) · level 2


### Statement

Assume AC and let $n\ge1$. Let $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb R^n$ be open with compact complement $C=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Omega$. Here a smooth exterior domain has the following one-sided local graph property. If $n\ge2$, at every $a\in\partial\Omega$ there are an open neighborhood $W$ of $a$, rigid coordinates $R(x)=(y,z)$, an open ball $B\subset\mathbb R^{n-1}$ and $h\in C^\infty(B)$ such that $R(W)\subseteq B\times\mathbb R$ and
$$R(\Omega\cap W)=R(W)\cap\{(y,z):z>h(y)\}.$$
For $n=1$, every boundary point $a$ has an interval neighborhood $I$ and a sign $\varepsilon\in\{-1,1\}$ such that $\Omega\cap I=\{x\in I:\varepsilon(x-a)>0\}$. These conditions specify the local one-sided graph convention for the unbounded open set; $C=\varnothing$ is allowed.

For each $1<q<\infty$ there is one bounded linear map
$$E:L^q(\Omega)\longrightarrow L^q(\mathbb R^n)$$
whose restriction to $W^{1,q}(\Omega)$ is bounded into $W^{1,q}(\mathbb R^n)$ and such that $PE=I$ on $L^q(\Omega)$, where $P$ is restriction to $\Omega$. Its formula uses finitely many fixed boundary-chart reflections, a fixed compact interior piece and the zero extension of an identity tail vanishing near the obstacle. The same geometric formula can be chosen for all $q$ in this range; its norm constants may depend on $q$.

### Statement references

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[Bounded $C^k$ domains and boundary charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-c-k-domain-and-boundary-charts)

[math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm)

[lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-collar-chart-localization](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-collar-chart-localization)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement. The $L^q$ spaces use Lebesgue measure and may have real or complex scalars.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available for the Sobolev suppliers. It also permits selecting local chart data at the boundary points in step 1.1.

[F2] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-collar-chart-localization](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-collar-chart-localization) gives each fixed boundary piece $T_i$, with one formula on $L^q$ and $W^{1,q}$ and $P T_i u=\rho_i u$.

[F3] [math:lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization) supplies a fixed ambient cutoff $\eta=1$ near the compact complement, compactly supported in a large ball, and final chart cutoffs equal to one near specified compact supports. For a finite open cover of a compact Euclidean set, [math:lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration) supplies ambient smooth functions compactly supported in the respective cover members whose sum is one near that set.

[F4] [math:lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor) gives bounded cutoff multiplication. [math:lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp) extends a compact interior piece, while [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm) supplies the test definition of derivatives for the unbounded tail.

[F5] The local one-sided graph and flattening convention of [Bounded $C^k$ domains and boundary charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-c-k-domain-and-boundary-charts) is used at every finite order, with the vertical orientation chosen so that the exterior is $t>0$. Only its local chart convention is used here. The exterior-domain hypotheses themselves are explicitly stated above; the nested chart construction is given in step 1.1.

[F6] [math:thm-heine-borel-rn](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-heine-borel-rn) identifies compact Euclidean sets with closed bounded sets and supplies the finite-subcover property, including for the boundary and the compact sets used in steps 1.1 and 2.1.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove the conclusions in the statement under its stated hypotheses.

1.1 If $C=\varnothing$, take $E=I$; henceforth let $C\ne\varnothing$. Choose $\eta\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ equal to $1$ near $C$. Both $\partial\Omega\subset C$ and $K=\operatorname{supp}\eta\cap\overline\Omega$ are closed and bounded, hence compact, and $\partial\Omega\subset K$. At each boundary point use the stated graph data and put $\Phi(x)=(y,z-h(y))$. This is a smooth coordinate map with inverse $\Phi^{-1}(y,t)=R^{-1}(y,h(y)+t)$, taking $\Omega\cap W$ exactly to $\Phi(W)\cap\{t>0\}$. Since $\Phi(W)$ is open and contains $\Phi(a)=(y_a,0)$, choose a bounded ball $B_a$ centered at $y_a$ and $b_a>0$ so that $\overline B_a\times[-b_a,b_a]\subset\Phi(W)$, with $\overline B_a\subset B$. Choose a concentric $B'_a$ with $\overline{B'_a}\subset B_a$ and $0<b'_a<b_a$. The smaller chart $U'_a=\Phi^{-1}(B'_a\times(-b'_a,b'_a))$ has compact closure inside $U_a=\Phi^{-1}(B_a\times(-b_a,b_a))$. Reflection $(y,t)\mapsto(y,-t)$ preserves the smaller closed cylinder, giving the required reflected-support margin. The first derivatives of these chart maps and their inverses are bounded on the larger closed cylinder because the derivatives of $h$ are continuous on $\overline B_a$; their Jacobians have absolute determinant one. For $n=1$, use $\Phi(x)=\varepsilon(x-a)$ and two nested symmetric intervals, which have the same properties. [F1, F3, F5, F6, given]

2.1 The sets $U'_a$ cover the compact boundary, so retain finitely many, say $U'_1,\ldots,U'_m$. The set $K\setminus\bigcup_iU'_i$ is closed and bounded, hence compact, and lies in $\Omega$: every point of $\overline\Omega$ outside $\Omega$ is a boundary point and was covered. Cover this remainder by finitely many balls $V_j$ with $\overline V_j\subset\Omega$. Apply the finite ambient partition supplier to the resulting finite open cover of $K$, obtaining $\chi_i\in C_c^\infty(U'_i)$ and $\chi_{0j}\in C_c^\infty(V_j)$ with total sum one near $K$. Put $\rho_i=\eta\chi_i$ and $\rho_0=\eta\sum_j\chi_{0j}$. Then $\rho_0$ has compact support in $\Omega$ and $\rho_0+\sum_i\rho_i=\eta$ on $\Omega$: wherever $\eta$ is nonzero in $\Omega$ the point lies in $K$, and elsewhere both sides are zero. Each $\rho_i$ has compact support in the smaller symmetric chart from step 1.1, so its reflected support stays compactly inside $U_i$. Choose a final smooth cutoff compactly supported in $U_i$ and equal to one near both these supports. These are precisely the chart and cutoff data needed by the localization supplier. All this data is fixed independently of $q$. [F2, F3, F6, step 1.1]

3.1 For $u\in L^q(\Omega)$ define $T_\infty u$ as the zero extension of $(1-\eta)u$. It is $L^q$ bounded. If $u\in W^{1,q}$, fix $\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$; then $(1-\eta)\phi\in C_c^\infty(\Omega)$, so the global weak derivative of $T_\infty u$ is the zero extension of $(1-\eta)\partial_j u-(\partial_j\eta)u$. Thus $\|T_\infty u\|_{W^{1,q}}\le C_\eta\|u\|_{W^{1,q}}$. This test argument does not require bounded support of the tail. The zero extension $T_0u$ of $\rho_0u$ has both endpoint bounds by its compact interior support. [F3, F4, step 2.1]

4.1 Define $Eu=T_\infty u+T_0u+\sum_{i=1}^mT_i u$, using the chart operators of the localization supplier with the data in step 2.1. This is one formula for every $L^q$ input; for a $W^{1,q}$ input it is exactly the same formula. The finite sum of the $L^q$ bounds gives $\|Eu\|_q\le C_q\|u\|_q$, and the finite sum of the first-derivative bounds gives $\|Eu\|_{W^{1,q}}\le\widetilde C_q\|u\|_{W^{1,q}}$. On $\Omega$ the sum equals $((1-\eta)+\rho_0+\sum_i\rho_i)u=u$. This proves $PE=I$, the norm bounds and the common-operator assertion. All choices depend only on the geometry and cutoffs; reflection is the same even formula for every $q$, which proves the final claim. [F2, F3, F4, step 2.1, step 3.1, algebra] ∎

### Source notes

The one-sided local graph convention is supplied by [Bounded $C^k$ domains and boundary charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-c-k-domain-and-boundary-charts) and specified for the exterior in the Statement. The finite nested chart cover is constructed locally using [math:thm-heine-borel-rn](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-heine-borel-rn), followed by the cutoff and finite ambient partition suppliers in [F3]. The endpoint assembly uses [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-collar-chart-localization](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-collar-chart-localization); the unbounded tail and simultaneous $L^q$ endpoint are proved explicitly above.


## Exterior Dirichlet realization, H² domain and form domain

[Open item](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization) · level 3


### Statement

Assume the Axiom of Choice (AC). Let $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$, $n\ge2$, where $\Theta$ is compact with smooth boundary and $\Omega$ is open. Work in $L^2(\Omega;\mathbb C)$ with inner product $\langle u,v\rangle=\int_\Omega u\overline v$, linear in the first variable. For $k=1,2$, write $H^k(\Omega)=W^{k,2}(\Omega;\mathbb C)$ with its weak-derivative sum-of-squares norm, and let $H^1_0(\Omega)=W^{1,2}_0(\Omega;\mathbb C)$ be the $H^1$ norm closure of $C_c^\infty(\Omega;\mathbb C)$. Self-adjointness means equality of a densely defined operator with its Hilbert-space adjoint, including equality of domains. The form
$$q(u,v)=\int_\Omega(u\overline v+\nabla u\cdot\overline{\nabla v})$$
on $H^1_0(\Omega)$ represents a self-adjoint $A\ge I$. Its domain is $H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$, $Au=u-\Delta u$, with $\|Au\|_2$ equivalent to $\|u\|_{H^2}$. Moreover $D(A^{1/2})=H^1_0(\Omega)$ with $\|A^{1/2}u\|_2^2=\|u\|_2^2+\|\nabla u\|_2^2$. Thus $\Delta_D=I-A$ is self-adjoint and nonpositive.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice). It is the choice hypothesis of [F2] and [F3] and supplies the Countable Choice hypothesis of [F4] and [F5].

[F2] [lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-weak-dirichlet-form](item-lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-weak-dirichlet-form) supplies the self-adjoint shifted weak realization, its distributional domain, the energy bound and the exact square-root form norm on any nonempty open set.

[F3] [lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-exterior-graph-domain](item-lem-se-author-88d8498c76d6-exterior-graph-domain) proves the exterior $H^2$ domain and graph norm by bounded-collar regularity and the separately justified unbounded Fourier tail.

[F4] The weak-derivative spaces and norms are supplied by [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm) with $p=2$, complex scalars and $k=1,2$. The closure convention for $H^1_0=W^{1,2}_0$ is supplied by [math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure). The open-set and dimensional hypotheses hold here; their Countable Choice hypothesis follows from [F1].

[F5] Self-adjointness on the ambient complex Hilbert space means equality with the densely defined Hilbert-space adjoint, including the domain, as in [math:def-symmetric-self-adjoint-and-essentially-self-adjoint](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-symmetric-self-adjoint-and-essentially-self-adjoint). Its Countable Choice hypothesis follows from [F1]; the ambient $L^2$ Hilbert space and pairing are those in [F2].

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove the conclusions in the statement under its stated hypotheses.

1.1 The exterior of a compact subset of $\mathbb R^n$ is nonempty. Apply the weak-form lemma with $U=\Omega$ and the stated Dirichlet form. It gives a self-adjoint $A\ge I$, $D(A)=\{u\in H^1_0:\Delta u\in L^2\}$, $Au=u-\Delta u$, and $D(A^{1/2})=H^1_0$ with the exact form identity. [F1, F2, F4, F5, given]

2.1 The graph-domain lemma applies to this same weak domain and gives $D(A)=H^2\cap H^1_0$ and $\|Au\|_2\asymp\|u\|_{H^2}$. Since $A$ is self-adjoint, $I-A$ is self-adjoint on $D(A)$, and $\langle(I-A)u,u\rangle=-\|\nabla u\|_2^2\le0$. This identifies the nonpositive Dirichlet Laplacian and proves every assertion. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1, algebra] ∎


### Source notes

This is a local collar/free-tail composition of the named library results. The article under study is not a prerequisite source. The five intermediate items supply the substantial local argument; no independent proof-review credit is claimed.


## Exact scale interpolation and endpoint operator transport

[Open item](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-exact-scale-operator-interpolation) · level 3


### Statement

Assume AC. Let $H,K$ be complex Hilbert spaces, possibly zero, with inner products linear in the first variable. Let $A\ge I$ and $B\ge I$ be positive self-adjoint operators on $H$ and $K$, respectively. Form the completed spectral scales $H_A^s$ and $K_B^s$, $s\in\mathbb R$, by completing $D(A^\infty)=\bigcap_{n\ge1}D(A^n)$ and $D(B^\infty)=\bigcap_{n\ge1}D(B^n)$ in the norms $\|A^{s/2}u\|_H$ and $\|B^{s/2}v\|_K$, respectively, and use the admissible complex strip interpolation norm. For any real $a,b$ and $0<\theta<1$ there is the canonical isometric identity $[H_A^a,H_A^b]_\theta=H_A^{(1-\theta)a+\theta b}$. For real $a_0,a_1,b_0,b_1$, a consistent complex-linear operator $L$ on $H_A^{a_0}+H_A^{a_1}$ whose restrictions map $H_A^{a_j}$ boundedly to $K_B^{b_j}$ with bounds $M_j\ge0$ satisfies $\|Lu\|_{K_B^{(1-\theta)b_0+\theta b_1}}\le M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta\|u\|_{H_A^{(1-\theta)a_0+\theta a_1}}$ for every $u\in H_A^{(1-\theta)a_0+\theta a_1}$. Consistency means the restrictions agree on the intersection and define one map into the target sum. The zero-bound convention is $0^t=0$ for $t>0$.

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] Unitary scale shifts identify each ordered scale couple with a pivot and a power domain, with exact endpoint norms [lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-scale-identifications](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-scale-identifications).

[F2] The admissible strip convention and its explicit analytic profiles define the interpolation space [lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-analytic-strip-profiles](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-analytic-strip-profiles).

[F3] The cutoff dual argument supplies the isometric identity for a pivot and a positive self-adjoint operator domain [lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-strip-duality](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-strip-duality).

[F4] Positive real powers of a positive self-adjoint operator are self-adjoint and satisfy the exact spectral multiplier domains. On the zero Hilbert space all spectral multipliers are the unique full-domain operator [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus).

[F5] AC is assumed for the underlying spectral calculus [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

[F6] A complex Hilbert space is a complex inner-product space complete in its induced norm [math:def-hilbert-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-hilbert-space).

[F7] A completion of a normed space is a Banach space with a dense linear isometric embedding of that space [math:def-completion-of-a-normed-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-completion-of-a-normed-space).

[F8] A densely defined operator is self-adjoint when it equals its adjoint, with the same domain and values [math:def-symmetric-self-adjoint-and-essentially-self-adjoint](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-symmetric-self-adjoint-and-essentially-self-adjoint). The countable-choice hypothesis of this definition follows from the assumed AC: apply AC to the family of values of a sequence of nonempty sets and compose the choice function with that sequence [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

### Proof

**Given:** The data and AC in the statement.

**Goal:** All asserted identifications and bounds, with the stated exact norms.

1.1 On the zero Hilbert space the unique full-domain operator is self-adjoint, and all its powers act on $\{0\}$ by [F4]. Thus its smooth core is $\{0\}$. Its completion at any real scale index is again $\{0\}$: the dense isometric image of the zero space consists only of zero, and a nonzero vector of norm $r>0$ would have a ball of radius $r/2$ disjoint from that image. For the zero endpoint couple the only strip function is zero, so its interpolation space is $\{0\}$ with its unique norm. This proves the scale identity whenever $H=\{0\}$, and the same reasoning applies to $K$. If either $H=\{0\}$ or $K=\{0\}$, the operator $L$ has zero image and the claimed estimate is $0\le0$ or $0\le M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta\|u\|$, respectively. Hence the operator estimate is also established in every such case. For the remaining arguments assume $H,K\ne\{0\}$. [given, F2, F4, F6, F7, F8]

1.2 Suppose $a\le b$, and write $d=(b-a)/2$. The unitary $J_a$ maps the endpoint couple isometrically and consistently to $(H,D(T))$ for $T=A^d\ge I$ with norm $\|Tv\|$. The Borel calculus gives self-adjointness of $T$ and $T^\theta=A^{d\theta}$. Applying this common map and its inverse to strip functions preserves holomorphy, continuity, decay and both endpoint norms. The pivot/domain identity therefore gives $[H_A^a,H_A^b]_\theta=H_A^{a+2d\theta}$ with identical norms. If $a=b$, use $T=I$. If $a>b$, $F(z)\mapsto F(1-z)$ is an isometry between the admissible classes with swapped endpoints, and evaluates at $1-\theta$, proving the same formula. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F8]

2.1 Endpoint boundedness makes $L$ bounded between the sum spaces: for each decomposition $u=u_0+u_1$, its target sum norm is at most $M_0\|u_0\|+M_1\|u_1\|$, so at most $\max(M_0,M_1)\|u\|_{\mathrm{sum}}$ after infimizing. Thus composition with $L$ preserves sum-valued holomorphy and continuity; endpoint boundedness preserves boundary continuity and decay. For $\delta>0$ put $m_j=M_j+\delta>0$. If $F(\theta)=u$, the strip function $G(z)=(m_0/m_1)^{z-\theta}LF(z)$ is admissible in the target, has $G(\theta)=Lu$, and has boundary norms at most $m_0^{1-\theta}m_1^\theta\|F\|_{\mathcal F}$: on boundary zero multiply $M_0$ by $(m_0/m_1)^{-\theta}$ and on boundary one multiply $M_1$ by $(m_0/m_1)^{1-\theta}$. The scalar factor is bounded on the strip, since the ratio is positive real. Taking the infimum in $F$ and then $\delta\downarrow0$ gives the asserted norm bound, including $M_j=0$. The target and source norms in this calculation are exactly those in step 1.2. [F1, F2, F3, step 1.2] ∎


## Restriction quotient and Bochner interpolation for the exterior extension

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-sobolev-quotient-bochner-retraction) · level 3


### Statement

Assume AC, $1<q<\infty$, $0<s<1$, $0<T<\infty$ and $1\le p<\infty$. Let $X_0=L^q(\Omega)$ and $X_1=W^{1,q}(\Omega)$ for a smooth exterior domain, and let $E$ be the single extension in [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-single-exterior-extension](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-single-exterior-extension). Put $X_s=[X_0,X_1]_s$ and
$$H^{s,q}(\mathbb R^n)=\{f\in\mathcal S':\mathcal F^{-1}((1+4\pi^2|\xi|^2)^{s/2}\widehat f)\in L^q\}.$$
Then $X_s$ equals the restriction quotient $P H^{s,q}(\mathbb R^n)$ with equivalent norms. Its quotient kernel is closed and the quotient is Banach. Moreover
$$[L^p(0,T;X_0),L^p(0,T;X_1)]_s=L^p(0,T;X_s)$$
with the Bochner convention of strongly measurable almost-everywhere classes. The pointwise maps $E$ and $P$ are well-defined bounded maps on the corresponding Bochner endpoint and interpolated spaces, and $PE=I$ there.

### External input

Reference 0, Definition 3.1 (PDF p.15), defines $\Lambda^z=\mathcal F^{-1}(1+4\pi^2|\xi|^2)^{-z/2}\mathcal F$. Remark 3.2 (PDF pp.15–16) states that this is a well-defined linear map on tempered distributions: the multiplier is smooth with polynomially bounded derivatives, and the Fourier transform and its inverse act on $\mathcal S\prime$. Definition 3.3 and Theorem 3.7 (PDF pp.16–17) give $H^{s,q}(\mathbb R^n)=\{f:\Lambda^{-s}f\in L^q\}=[L^q,W^{1,q}]_s$ with equivalent norms when $1<q<\infty$, $0<s<1$. Example 2.7(i), PDF p.11, gives $[L^{p_0}(X_0),L^{p_1}(X_1)]_s=L^p([X_0,X_1]_s)$ for finite endpoint exponents with $1/p=(1-s)/p_0+s/p_1$. Only the equal exponent case $p_0=p_1=p$ is used below.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses of the Statement and the independently cited external contracts.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [math:thm-sobolev-spaces-are-banach-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sobolev-spaces-are-banach-spaces) gives the complex Banach endpoints and weak-derivative norms on any open set. Since $W^{1,q}\hookrightarrow L^q$ continuously, both form a compatible couple in $L^q$.

[F3] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-single-exterior-extension](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-single-exterior-extension) gives one $E$ on the endpoint sum with $PE=I$ and bounds at both endpoints. Restriction $P$ is contractive on both weak-derivative endpoints.

[F4] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined) interpolates these same maps, and [math:thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach) makes a quotient by a closed kernel Banach.

[F5] Reference 0, Definition 3.1 (PDF p.15), defines $\Lambda^z=\mathcal F^{-1}(1+4\pi^2|\xi|^2)^{-z/2}\mathcal F$. Remark 3.2 (PDF pp.15–16) states that this is a well-defined linear map on tempered distributions: the multiplier is smooth with polynomially bounded derivatives, and the Fourier transform and its inverse act on $\mathcal S\prime$. Definition 3.3 and Theorem 3.7 (PDF pp.16–17) give $H^{s,q}(\mathbb R^n)=\{f:\Lambda^{-s}f\in L^q\}=[L^q,W^{1,q}]_s$ with equivalent norms when $1<q<\infty$, $0<s<1$. Example 2.7(i), PDF p.11, gives $[L^{p_0}(X_0),L^{p_1}(X_1)]_s=L^p([X_0,X_1]_s)$ for finite endpoint exponents with $1/p=(1-s)/p_0+s/p_1$. Only the equal exponent case $p_0=p_1=p$ is used below.

[F6] [math:def-strongly-measurable-banach-valued-function](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-strongly-measurable-banach-valued-function) defines strong measurability for real or complex Banach values by almost-everywhere norm limits of measurable simple functions, unchanged by modifying a representative on a null set.

[F7] [math:thm-pe12-bochner-lp-completeness](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-pe12-bochner-lp-completeness) defines the Bochner $L^p$ norm on strongly measurable almost-everywhere classes and proves completeness on $(0,T)$ for real Banach values, $0<T<\infty$, $1\le p<\infty$, under Countable Choice. [math:lem-complex-norm-realification-is-a-real-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-complex-norm-realification-is-a-real-norm) preserves the norm and metric upon forgetting complex scalars; thus the realification of a complex Banach space is real Banach. AC supplies the Countable Choice used by this completeness theorem.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove the conclusions in the statement under its stated hypotheses.

1.1 Let $Y_j$ be the whole-space endpoints $L^q(\mathbb R^n),W^{1,q}(\mathbb R^n)$. Apply the strip operator theorem to $P:Y_j\to X_j$ and the same $E:X_j\to Y_j$. The whole-space Bessel input identifies $Y_s=[Y_0,Y_1]_s$ with $H^{s,q}$, so $P:Y_s\to X_s$ and $E:X_s\to Y_s$ are bounded, $PE=I$, and $P Y_s=X_s$. This proves the set equality with restriction classes rather than choosing an independent fractional extension. [F2, F3, F4, F5, given]

1.2 For a bounded linear spatial map $A:Z\to Z\prime$ between the Banach spaces in question and a strongly measurable $f$, F6 supplies measurable simple approximants converging almost everywhere in norm. Their images under $A$ are measurable simple functions converging to $Af$. Thus $Af$ is strongly measurable, independent of null-set representatives, and $\|Af\|_{L^p(Z\prime)}\le\|A\|\|f\|_{L^p(Z)}$. This applies to the common endpoint maps $E,P$ and to the spatial inclusions $X_1\hookrightarrow X_0$, $Y_1\hookrightarrow Y_0$. [F2, F3, F6, algebra]

1.3 Apply F7 to the realification of each $X_j,Y_j$. Its simple functions, null-set classes, norm and metric are unchanged by realification, so its strong measurability and Bochner $L^p$ space are the same sets with the same norm as for complex values. The real completeness theorem therefore proves metric completeness of the complex Bochner spaces. Complex scalar multiplication on classes obeys $\|\lambda f\|_{L^p}=|\lambda|\|f\|_{L^p}$, directly from the spatial norm and integral, so these are complex Banach spaces. AC supplies the theorem's Countable Choice hypothesis. [F1, F2, F6, F7, algebra]

2.1 In the interpolation norm let $C_P,C_E$ be these bounds, including the Bessel norm equivalences. If $Pf=u$, then $\|u\|_{X_s}\le C_P\|f\|_{H^{s,q}}$. Conversely $E u$ extends $u$ and $\|Eu\|_{H^{s,q}}\le C_E\|u\|_{X_s}$. Taking the infimum over extensions gives $C_P^{-1}\|u\|_{X_s}\le\|u\|_{\mathrm{quot}}\le C_E\|u\|_{X_s}$. Since $X_s$ continuously injects into $X_0=L^q(\Omega)$, $P:H^{s,q}\to L^q(\Omega)$ is continuous. Its kernel is closed. The quotient theorem makes the quotient norm definite and complete, proving its well-definedness. [F2, F4, F5, step 1.1, algebra]

3.1 The spatial inequalities $\|u\|_{X_0}\le\|u\|_{X_1}$ and $\|v\|_{Y_0}\le\|v\|_{Y_1}$ integrate to continuous injective embeddings $L^p(0,T;X_1)\hookrightarrow L^p(0,T;X_0)$ and $L^p(0,T;Y_1)\hookrightarrow L^p(0,T;Y_0)$. Injectivity follows because zero in the larger spatial space is zero in the smaller one at each time outside a null set. Each pair is consequently a compatible complex Banach couple in its zeroth endpoint, a Hausdorff normed space. The time-valued $E,P$ are single complex-linear maps on these endpoint sums, with the endpoint bounds from step 1.2 and $PE=I$ inherited almost everywhere from the spatial identity. The strip theorem therefore applies. Example 2.7(i), with the same finite exponent $p$ at both endpoints on $(0,T)$, identifies the two interpolated time spaces with $L^p(0,T;X_s)$ and $L^p(0,T;Y_s)$. Together with step 1.1 this gives the displayed Bochner identity and the claimed bounded retraction maps, including $PE=I$, on the interpolated time spaces. [F2, F3, F4, F5, step 1.1, step 1.2, step 1.3, algebra] ∎

### Source notes

All Bochner assertions use Example2.7(i), PDF p.11, not the later occurrence in a different proof on p.23. There is no identification with Slobodeckij spaces for q unequal to2 and no p=∞ time assertion.


## Dirichlet Laplacian and positive shift

[Open item](item-def-se-dirichlet-laplacian) · level 4


### Definition

Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $n\ge2$, and let $\Theta\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a nonempty compact set with smooth boundary and connected exterior $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$. Work in the complex Hilbert space $L^2(\Omega)$ of square-integrable functions modulo equality almost everywhere, with inner product $\langle u,v\rangle=\int_\Omega u\overline v\,dx$.

For $k=1,2$, $H^k(\Omega)$ consists of the $L^2$ classes whose weak derivatives of order at most $k$ are in $L^2$; $H^1_0(\Omega)$ is the closure of $C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ in the $H^1$ norm. Define the Dirichlet Laplacian $\Delta_D$ by $D(\Delta_D)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$ and $\Delta_Du=\sum_{j=1}^n\partial_j^2u$, where the derivatives are weak derivatives. Define $A=I-\Delta_D$ on the same domain.

The operator $\Delta_D$ is self-adjoint and nonpositive, and $A$ is self-adjoint with $A\ge I$ in the quadratic-form sense: $\langle Au,u\rangle\ge\|u\|_2^2$ for $u\in D(A)$. Here self-adjoint means that the densely defined operator equals its Hilbert-space adjoint, including equality of domains. Let $E_A$ be the spectral projection-valued measure of $A$. For real $s$, write $A^s$ for the spectral operator associated with the Borel function $\lambda\mapsto\max(1,\lambda)^s$. For real $t$, write $e^{it\Delta_D}$ for the bounded spectral operator associated with $\lambda\mapsto e^{it(1-\lambda)}$. These operators form the strongly continuous unitary Schrödinger group on $L^2(\Omega)$: for every $u\in L^2(\Omega)$ and $t_0\in\mathbb R$, $\lim_{t\to t_0}\|e^{it\Delta_D}u-e^{it_0\Delta_D}u\|_2=0$.

### Definition references

[def-se-geometry](item-def-se-geometry)

[def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation](item-def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation)

[math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm)

[math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization)

[math:thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators)

[math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus)

[math:def-strongly-continuous-one-parameter-unitary-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-strongly-continuous-one-parameter-unitary-group)

[math:lem-self-adjoint-operator-generates-a-strongly-continuous-unitary-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-self-adjoint-operator-generates-a-strongly-continuous-unitary-group)

[math:lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator)

### Justification

The geometry and smooth-boundary convention are those of [def-se-geometry](item-def-se-geometry). The complex weak Sobolev spaces and their norms are fixed by [def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation](item-def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation) and [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm); [math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure) supplies the test-function closure. In particular, the definition of $H^1_0$ does not itself assert a trace characterization.

Under the stated Choice assumption, [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization) applies to this smooth exterior and identifies the displayed domain and action with the self-adjoint Dirichlet realization. It supplies $A\ge I$ and the nonpositivity of $\Delta_D$. Choice includes the countable-choice assumption used in the Sobolev definitions and is also assumed by the realization and spectral suppliers.

The exterior is nonempty and open, so $L^2(\Omega)$ is nonzero. The spectral theorem [math:thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators) therefore supplies the unique spectral measure of $A$. The spectral calculus [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus) defines the stated operators, with the domain of $A^s$ consisting of those $u$ for which $\int\max(1,\lambda)^{2s}\,d(E_A)_u(\lambda)$ is finite. Applying [math:lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator) with the lower bound $c=1$ shows that $E_A$ is carried on $[1,\infty)$. Thus the Borel multiplier $\max(1,\lambda)^s$ agrees with the usual positive power $\lambda^s$ on the spectral support. The bounded calculus in that supplier gives the group law and adjoint formula for the exponential functions; their product with their conjugates is one, so each $e^{it\Delta_D}$ is unitary.

Applying [math:lem-self-adjoint-operator-generates-a-strongly-continuous-unitary-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-self-adjoint-operator-generates-a-strongly-continuous-unitary-group) to the self-adjoint $A$ on $L^2(\Omega)$ under the stated Choice assumption gives strong continuity of $t\mapsto e^{itA}u$ for every $u\in L^2(\Omega)$. The bounded spectral calculus identifies $e^{it\Delta_D}=e^{it}e^{-itA}$, because $e^{it(1-\lambda)}=e^{it}e^{-it\lambda}$. Thus for $u\in L^2(\Omega)$ and $t_0\in\mathbb R$,
$$\|e^{it\Delta_D}u-e^{it_0\Delta_D}u\|_2\le\|(e^{-itA}-e^{-it_0A})u\|_2+|e^{it}-e^{it_0}|\|u\|_2\longrightarrow0\quad(t\to t_0).$$
Together with the group law and unitarity already established, this gives a strongly continuous one-parameter unitary group in the sense of [math:def-strongly-continuous-one-parameter-unitary-group](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-strongly-continuous-one-parameter-unitary-group).

The article is cited for its notation and the stated Dirichlet domain. The realization and spectral conclusions above use the independently supplied mathematical items, not the article as proof.


## An explicit common interpolation model for the fractional Strichartz targets

[Open item](item-lem-se-interpolation-sobolev-model) · level 4


### Statement

Assume AC. For $1<q<\infty$, $0<s<1$ and a smooth exterior domain $\Omega$, fix the convention $\mathcal W^{s,q}(\Omega)=[L^q(\Omega),W^{1,q}(\Omega)]_s$ by complex strip interpolation. For a compatible couple $E_0,E_1$ embedded in a common Hausdorff vector space, its sum has norm $\inf_{x=x_0+x_1}(\|x_0\|_{E_0}+\|x_1\|_{E_1})$; the strip functions are bounded and continuous in this sum, norm-holomorphic inside, and have continuous $E_j$ boundary values tending to zero at imaginary infinity. The interpolation norm is the infimum of their maximum boundary supremum norm over functions taking value $x$ at $s$. A single collar-reflection/free-tail extension, bounded at both $L^q$ and $W^{1,q}$ endpoints and right-inverse to restriction, identifies this space with the restriction quotient of whole-space $H^{s,q}$, with equivalent norms. For each $0<T<\infty$ and $1\le p<\infty$, let $L^p(0,T;X)$ denote the Bochner space of strongly measurable almost-everywhere classes with norm $(\int_0^T\|f(t)\|_X^p\,dt)^{1/p}$. Then the corresponding target interpolation is $[L^p(0,T;L^q(\Omega)),L^p(0,T;W^{1,q}(\Omega))]_s=L^p(0,T;\mathcal W^{s,q}(\Omega))$. This is an explicit model chosen for the proposed applications; it is not an assertion that BGT specified this unique model, nor an identification with Slobodeckij spaces for $q\ne2$.

### External input

The abstract strip-space Banach property and exact operator theorem are the independent contract in reference 0, §2.2, PDF pp.9–10. Its whole-space Bessel identity is Definition 3.6 and Theorem 3.7, PDF p.17, using Definitions 3.1 and 3.3 on pp.15–16. The finite-exponent Bochner identity is Example 2.7(i), PDF p.11. These imported inputs have the hypotheses and reading limits in the source record. The exterior extension, its simultaneous endpoint bounds and the restriction quotient application are local arguments in the five named intermediate items.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses of the Statement and the independently cited external contracts.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [math:def-banach-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-banach-space) and [math:def-bounded-linear-operator](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-linear-operator) define complex Banach spaces and common bounded linear endpoint operators; [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm) fixes the $L^q,W^{1,q}$ weak-derivative endpoints. Under AC, [math:thm-sobolev-spaces-are-banach-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sobolev-spaces-are-banach-spaces) proves their completeness on the open set $\Omega$, using $k=0,1$, exponent $q$ and scalar field $\mathbb C$.

[F3] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined) verifies the stated sum/strip norms, their Banach well-definedness and exact interpolation of a common endpoint map.

[F4] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-single-exterior-extension](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-single-exterior-extension) constructs the one collar/reflection plus tail map, and [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-sobolev-quotient-bochner-retraction](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-sobolev-quotient-bochner-retraction) proves the quotient-norm comparison and finite-time Bochner identity for that map.

[F5] The abstract strip-space Banach property and exact operator theorem are the independent contract in reference 0, §2.2, PDF pp.9–10. Its whole-space Bessel identity is Definition 3.6 and Theorem 3.7, PDF p.17, using Definitions 3.1 and 3.3 on pp.15–16. The finite-exponent Bochner identity is Example 2.7(i), PDF p.11. These imported inputs have the hypotheses and reading limits in the source record. The exterior extension, its simultaneous endpoint bounds and the restriction quotient application are local arguments in the five named intermediate items.

[F6] [math:def-strongly-measurable-banach-valued-function](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-strongly-measurable-banach-valued-function) defines strong measurability by almost-everywhere norm limits of measurable simple functions, for complex as well as real Banach values. The Bochner norm and null-set class convention in the Statement are those of [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-sobolev-quotient-bochner-retraction](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-sobolev-quotient-bochner-retraction).

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove the conclusions in the statement under its stated hypotheses.

1.1 By the Sobolev completeness theorem with $k=0,1$, exponent $q$ and scalar field $\mathbb C$, the endpoints $L^q(\Omega)$ and $W^{1,q}(\Omega)$ are complex Banach spaces under AC. Both inject into $L^q(\Omega)$; indeed their sum is $L^q(\Omega)$ and the displayed sum norm is its $L^q$ norm because $\|u\|_q\le\|u\|_{W^{1,q}}$. The strip-model helper therefore makes the stated interpolation convention a definite complete normed space, with precisely the boundary continuity and decay specified in the Statement. All spaces are spaces of almost-everywhere classes, so there is no representative-dependent boundary value of a spatial function in this definition. [F1, F2, F3, given]

2.1 The exterior extension helper supplies one map at both endpoints. The quotient/retraction helper applies the strip theorem to this map and restriction, identifies the whole-space target using the cited Bessel convention, and proves both inequalities comparing the interpolation and restriction-quotient norms. Thus $\mathcal W^{s,q}(\Omega)$ has exactly the asserted quotient realization without importing a bounded-domain extension as an exterior theorem. [F3, F4, F5, step 1.1]

3.1 Let $0<T<\infty$ and $1\le p<\infty$, the finite Bochner range in the Statement. Example2.7(i) and the quotient/retraction helper give $[L^p(0,T;L^q(\Omega)),L^p(0,T;W^{1,q}(\Omega))]_s=L^p(0,T;\mathcal W^{s,q}(\Omega))$. The maps are consistent on sums and on strongly measurable classes as proved there. This supplies exactly the chosen fractional target model; it adds no identification with a different fractional convention and no claim about how BGT named its spaces. [F4, F5, F6, step 2.1] ∎

## Localized multiplier estimate for the exterior Dirichlet normal trace

[Open item](item-lem-se-localized-boundary-normal-trace) · level 4


### Statement

Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$, $n\ge2$, be an open exterior domain with compact smooth boundary, where $\Theta$ is compact, and let $T>0$. Let $\Delta_D$ be the nonpositive self-adjoint Dirichlet Laplacian of [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization). For $v_0\in H^1_0(\Omega)$ put $v(t)=e^{it\Delta_D}v_0$. There is a unique normal trace $\partial_\nu v\in L^2((0,T)\times\partial\Omega)$ obtained as the limit of the normal derivative traces of spectral approximants to $v_0$, and it satisfies $\|\partial_\nu v\|_{L^2((0,T)\times\partial\Omega)}\le C_{\Omega,T}\|v_0\|_{H^1(\Omega)}$. Here $\nu$ is the outward unit normal of $\Omega$, and surface measure is used on $\partial\Omega$.

There is a real smooth vector field $Q$ supported in an arbitrarily prescribed bounded neighborhood of $\partial\Omega$ and equal to $\nu$ on the boundary. For sufficiently regular solutions, the multiplier identity in the proof contains the boundary term $\frac12\int_0^T\int_{\partial\Omega}(Q\cdot\nu)|\partial_\nu v|^2\,dS\,dt$; its other terms are bounded by a constant depending on $Q$ and $T$ times $\|v_0\|_{H^1}^2$. Neither non-trapping nor star-shapedness is assumed.

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization) gives $A=I-\Delta_D\ge I$, $D(A)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$ and $D(A^{1/2})=H^1_0(\Omega)$ with $\|A^{1/2}u\|_2=\|u\|_{H^1}$. Sobolev spaces and the closure defining $H^1_0$ are as in [def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation](item-def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation).

[F2] The mathematical self-adjoint flow calculus of [physics:lem-nrqm-autonomous-dynamics-flow-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/physics/item/lem-nrqm-autonomous-dynamics-flow-calculus), the spectral theorem [math:thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators), and the form-domain identity and core of [math:lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator) apply under [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice). They give spectral truncations converging in the form norm and unitary flow commuting with spectral functions; the same calculus preserves the form norm.

[F3] On a bounded smooth truncation, [math:thm-sobolev-gauss-green-formula-on-c-one-domains](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sobolev-gauss-green-formula-on-c-one-domains) gives coordinate integration by parts for $H^1$ factors, and [math:thm-sharp-trace-theorem-for-w-one-p](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sharp-trace-theorem-for-w-one-p) gives their bounded $L^2$ boundary traces. [Ambient smooth restrictions are dense on bounded $C^k$ domains](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-smooth-up-to-the-boundary-density-on-smooth-domains) supplies approximation in $H^2$ by smooth restrictions. The normal is the graph normal of [math:def-bounded-c-one-domain-boundary-charts-and-outward-normal](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-c-one-domain-boundary-charts-and-outward-normal).

[F4] [math:lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration) gives a finite smooth ambient partition near the compact boundary. The time and norm conventions are those of [def-se-foundation-time-function-spaces](item-def-se-foundation-time-function-spaces); the boundary product uses surface measure and Lebesgue time measure.

[F5] Put $S=\partial\Omega$.
