The surface integral of [math:def-surface-integral-on-a-compact-c-one-hypersurface](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-surface-integral-on-a-compact-c-one-hypersurface) defines a finite Borel measure $\sigma=dS$ on $S$ by [math:lem-surface-integral-is-independent-of-c-one-boundary-charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-surface-integral-is-independent-of-c-one-boundary-charts). With Lebesgue measure $\lambda=dt$ on $(0,T)$, set $P=\overline{\lambda\times\sigma}$ as in [math:def-completed-product-measure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-completed-product-measure). Both factors are finite, hence sigma-finite. Here $H=L^2(S,\sigma;\mathbb C)$ and $K=L^2((0,T)\times S,P;\mathbb C)$ are scalar almost-everywhere quotient spaces; the boundary spacetime notation in the Statement means $K$.

[F6] [math:thm-tonelli-and-fubini-for-completed-product-measures](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-tonelli-and-fubini-for-completed-product-measures) identifies the $P$ integral of a nonnegative measurable function with its iterated integrals, with section measurability outside null sets for the completed factors. [math:thm-complex-holder-minkowski-and-the-quotient-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-complex-holder-minkowski-and-the-quotient-norm) supplies complex quotient norms, the triangle inequality and Cauchy–Schwarz on these measure spaces. [Complex $L^p$ completeness and almost-everywhere subsequences](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-complex-lp-completeness-and-almost-everywhere-subsequences) gives completeness and almost-everywhere convergent subsequences of norm-convergent sequences on every measure space, including $H$ and $K$. The assumed AC supplies their countable-choice hypotheses and the countable selections of representatives below.

[F7] The compact time interval and norm-continuity convention are those of [def-se-foundation-time-function-spaces](item-def-se-foundation-time-function-spaces). [math:thm-heine-cantor-metric](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-heine-cantor-metric) gives uniform continuity for a continuous map from this compact interval into the norm metric of $H$. Applied to real and imaginary parts, [math:thm-sequential-suprema-infima-limsup-liminf-and-pointwise-limits-are-measurable](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sequential-suprema-infima-limsup-liminf-and-pointwise-limits-are-measurable) gives measurable convergence sets and measurable limits; [math:thm-sections-of-product-measurable-functions-are-measurable](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sections-of-product-measurable-functions-are-measurable) gives measurable sections of functions measurable for the uncompleted product sigma-algebra.

### Proof

[F1] [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization) gives $A=I-\Delta_D\ge I$, $D(A)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$ and $D(A^{1/2})=H^1_0(\Omega)$ with $\|A^{1/2}u\|_2=\|u\|_{H^1}$. Sobolev spaces and the closure defining $H^1_0$ are as in [def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation](item-def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation).

[F2] The mathematical self-adjoint flow calculus of [physics:lem-nrqm-autonomous-dynamics-flow-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/physics/item/lem-nrqm-autonomous-dynamics-flow-calculus), the spectral theorem [math:thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators), and the form-domain identity and core of [math:lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-spectral-form-domain-and-core-of-a-semibounded-operator) apply under [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice). They give spectral truncations converging in the form norm and unitary flow commuting with spectral functions; the same calculus preserves the form norm.

[F3] On a bounded smooth truncation, [math:thm-sobolev-gauss-green-formula-on-c-one-domains](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sobolev-gauss-green-formula-on-c-one-domains) gives coordinate integration by parts for $H^1$ factors, and [math:thm-sharp-trace-theorem-for-w-one-p](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sharp-trace-theorem-for-w-one-p) gives their bounded $L^2$ boundary traces. [Ambient smooth restrictions are dense on bounded $C^k$ domains](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-smooth-up-to-the-boundary-density-on-smooth-domains) supplies approximation in $H^2$ by smooth restrictions. The normal is the graph normal of [math:def-bounded-c-one-domain-boundary-charts-and-outward-normal](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-c-one-domain-boundary-charts-and-outward-normal).

[F4] [math:lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration) gives a finite smooth ambient partition near the compact boundary. The time and norm conventions are those of [def-se-foundation-time-function-spaces](item-def-se-foundation-time-function-spaces); the boundary product uses surface measure and Lebesgue time measure.



**Given:** The domain, time and initial datum in the Statement.

**Goal:** Construct the normal trace, prove its estimate, and exhibit the multiplier boundary term.

1.1 In each smooth boundary graph chart the outward unit normal is the normalized smooth graph normal, extended off the graph by making it independent of the normal chart coordinate. A finite ambient partition subordinate to these charts, multiplied by a smooth cutoff supported in the prescribed neighborhood and equal to one near the compact boundary, gives a real smooth compactly supported field $Q$ with $Q=\nu$ on $\partial\Omega$. Choose a large ball whose sphere is disjoint from both the obstacle and the support of $Q$; its intersection $D$ with $\Omega$ is a bounded smooth domain. [given, F3, F4, construct]

1.2 Let $E$ be the spectral measure of $A$ and set $v_{0,N}=E([1,N])v_0$, $v_N(t)=e^{it\Delta_D}v_{0,N}$. Spectral truncation gives $v_{0,N}\to v_0$ in the form norm. Each $v_N$ is continuously differentiable in time with values in $D(A)$ equipped with its graph norm, since its spectral support is bounded; in particular $v_N\in C^1([0,T];H^2(\Omega))$ and $\partial_t v_N=i\Delta v_N$. Its $H^1$ norm is constant and equals $\|v_{0,N}\|_{H^1}$. The first-derivative traces therefore depend continuously on time in boundary $L^2$. [F1, F2, F3]

1.3 We construct a product representative for any $h\in C([0,T];H)$. By uniform continuity choose, for each integer $m\ge1$, a finite interval partition and an $H$-valued step map $p_m$, taking values of $h$ at partition endpoints, with $\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|p_m(t)-h(t)\|_H\le2^{-m}$. AC selects measurable complex representatives of the countably many sampled $H$ classes. Using these representatives on the corresponding time intervals gives a function $q_m(t,x)$ measurable for the uncompleted product sigma-algebra. Each $q_m$ belongs to $K$, and Tonelli gives $\|q_m-q_l\|_K^2=\int_0^T\|p_m(t)-p_l(t)\|_H^2dt\le T(2^{-m}+2^{-l})^2$. Thus completeness gives a limit in $K$, and the subsequence assertion in [F6] gives indices $m_k$ for which these measurable representatives $q_{m_k}$ converge $P$-almost everywhere to a representative of that limit. Predetermined representatives are allowed here: their countably many null disagreement sets with the representatives supplied by [F6] can be removed. [F5, F6, F7, construct]

2.1 If $u\in H^2(D)$ has zero trace on the obstacle boundary, the traces of its first derivatives satisfy $T\nabla u=\nu\,\partial_\nu u$ there, where $\partial_\nu u=\nu\cdot T\nabla u\in L^2$. Indeed, approximate $u$ in $H^2$ by smooth restrictions. In a graph chart the tangential derivative of each approximating boundary restriction is the corresponding tangential combination of first derivatives. The trace theorem passes both sides to the limit in distributions, and the limiting boundary restriction is zero; hence all tangential components vanish. The trace theorem also makes this normal derivative the limit of the classical normal derivatives of those smooth restrictions. The closure definition of $H^1_0(\Omega)$ and local trace continuity give zero obstacle-boundary trace for every $u\in H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$. [F1, F3, step 1.1]

2.2 For one such solution write $w=v_N$, $M(t)=\operatorname{Im}\int_\Omega\overline w\,Q\cdot\nabla w\,dx$ and $B(t)=\int_{\partial\Omega}(Q\cdot\nu)|\partial_\nu w|^2\,dS$. Differentiation in $H^2$, followed by coordinate Sobolev integration by parts on $D$, gives $M'(t)=-2\operatorname{Re}\int_D\Delta\overline w\,Q\cdot\nabla w\,dx-\operatorname{Re}\int_D(\operatorname{div}Q)\overline w\,\Delta w\,dx$. The boundary term from moving $Q\cdot\nabla$ off $\Delta w$ vanishes because $w$ has zero trace where $Q$ meets the boundary; $Q$ vanishes near the artificial sphere. [F3, step 1.1, step 1.2]

2.3 Let $E$ be the set where $q_{m_k}$ has a finite complex limit. The convergence sets and the finiteness conditions for its real and imaginary parts are measurable for the uncompleted product sigma-algebra by [F7]. Define $g$ as that limit on $E$ and zero elsewhere. It is measurable for that same sigma-algebra, represents the limit in $K$, and has Borel-measurable sections on $S$. Tonelli applied to $\mathbf1_{E^c}$ shows that, for almost every $t$, $q_{m_k}(t,\cdot)\to g(t,\cdot)$ $\sigma$-almost everywhere. For each such $t$, the classes of these sections are $p_{m_k}(t)\to h(t)$ in $H$; the subsequence assertion in [F6], now on $S$, gives a further almost-everywhere convergent subsequence with limit class $h(t)$. Uniqueness of a pointwise limit therefore makes $g(t,\cdot)$ a representative of $h(t)$. A final application of Tonelli gives $\|g\|_K^2=\int_0^T\|h(t)\|_H^2dt$. Any two such product representatives agree in $K$, since their difference has zero section norm almost everywhere and Tonelli gives zero product norm. This also gives the norm identity for differences of two continuous curves. Apply the construction to $h_N(t)=\nu\cdot T\nabla v_N(t)$: the bounded derivative trace maps and multiplication by the smooth bounded normal make it a continuous $H$-valued curve by step 1.2. Denote its product representative by $g_N$ and identify it with $\partial_\nu v_N$ in $K$. [F3, F5, F6, F7, step 1.2, step 1.3]

3.1 Integrate the two displayed integrals by parts. On the obstacle boundary, step 2.1 gives $T\nabla w=\nu\partial_\nu w$; consequently the boundary contribution from the first integral is $-2B(t)+B(t)=-B(t)$, and the second integral has no boundary contribution involving $w$. Thus $M'(t)=-B(t)+2\operatorname{Re}\int_D\sum_{j,k}(\partial_jQ_k)(\partial_j\overline w)(\partial_kw)\,dx+\operatorname{Re}\int_D\overline w\,\nabla(\operatorname{div}Q)\cdot\nabla w\,dx$. The cancellation of the two volume terms containing $(\operatorname{div}Q)|\nabla w|^2$ follows directly from the product rule. All integrations are legitimate for the indicated $H^1$ factors, and no boundary term occurs at the artificial sphere. [F3, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 2.2, algebra]

4.1 Integrating step 3.1 in time and dividing by two displays the asserted multiplier boundary term. Cauchy–Schwarz and boundedness of $Q$, $DQ$ and $D^2Q$ bound $|M(t)|$ and the volume integrals by $C_Q\|w(t)\|_{H^1}^2$. Since $Q=\nu$ on the boundary, $B(t)=\|\partial_\nu w(t)\|_H^2$. Step 2.3 and Tonelli identify $\int_0^T B(t)dt=\|g_N\|_K^2$, so $\|\partial_\nu v_N\|_{L^2((0,T)\times\partial\Omega)}\le C_{\Omega,T}\|v_{0,N}\|_{H^1}$. The same estimate applies to differences of spectral approximants. [F6, step 1.1, step 1.2, step 2.3, step 3.1]

5.1 The difference estimate and form-norm convergence make the normal traces Cauchy in the complete boundary spacetime $L^2$ space. Here the space is $K$ from [F5], its completeness is supplied by [F6], and step 2.3 identifies the difference norms with the time integrals estimated in step 4.1. Their limit defines $\partial_\nu v$ and obeys the stated bound. The same difference estimate proves independence of the particular form-norm convergent spectral truncation sequence; it also shows consistency with the $H^2$ normal trace for initial data in $D(A)$, because spectral truncation then converges in the graph norm and the boundary trace is continuous in $H^2$. No geometrical sign condition was used. [F1, F2, F3, F5, F6, step 1.2, step 2.3, step 4.1] ∎


## Lifted spectral measure and isometric Bochner scale interpolation

[Open item](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-bochner-lifted-spectral-measure) · level 4


### Statement

Assume AC, let $A\ge I$ be self-adjoint on a nonzero complex Hilbert space $H$, and let $I_\tau=(0,\tau)$ with $0<\tau<\infty$ and Lebesgue measure. For a Hilbert space $X$, $L^2(I_\tau;X)$ denotes almost-everywhere classes of strongly measurable functions, meaning almost-everywhere pointwise limits of measurable simple functions, satisfying $\int_{I_\tau}\|u(t)\|_X^2dt<\infty$, with norm $(\int_{I_\tau}\|u(t)\|_X^2dt)^{1/2}$. On $\mathscr H=L^2(I_\tau;H)$, the formula $(\mathscr E(C)u)(t)=E_A(C)u(t)$ defines a regular spectral projection-valued measure; let $\mathscr A=\int\lambda\,d\mathscr E(\lambda)\ge I$. Its completed spectral scale is canonically and isometrically $\mathscr H_{\mathscr A}^s=L^2(I_\tau;H_A^s)$ for every real $s$. In particular,
$$[L^2(I_\tau;H_A^a),L^2(I_\tau;H_A^b)]_\theta=L^2(I_\tau;H_A^{(1-\theta)a+\theta b})$$
isometrically for all real $a,b$ and $0<\theta<1$. 

For the endpoint operator assertion, let $(X_0,X_1)$ be any compatible complex Banach couple: the endpoints embed continuously and complex-linearly into a common Hausdorff complex topological vector space. Give $X_0+X_1$ the norm $\inf_{x=x_0+x_1}(\|x_0\|_{X_0}+\|x_1\|_{X_1})$, and use the admissible complex strip interpolation norm. Thus admissible functions are bounded continuous sum-valued maps on $0\le\operatorname{Re}z\le1$, holomorphic in its interior, with continuous endpoint-valued boundary maps tending to zero at imaginary infinity; the function norm is the maximum of the two boundary supremum norms, and the interpolation norm is its infimum at fixed value $F(\theta)$.

Let $b_0,b_1\in\mathbb R$, set $Y_j=L^2(I_\tau;H_A^{b_j})$, and suppose complex-linear maps $L_j:X_j\to Y_j$ have finite bounds $M_j\ge0$. Assume consistency: $L_0x=L_1x$ for $x\in X_0\cap X_1$, with equality in the common space $L^2(I_\tau;H_A^{\min(b_0,b_1)})$. They then define one map $L:X_0+X_1\to Y_0+Y_1$ by $L(x_0+x_1)=L_0x_0+L_1x_1$. For $b_\theta=(1-\theta)b_0+\theta b_1$ and $0<\theta<1$,
$$\|Lx\|_{L^2(I_\tau;H_A^{b_\theta})}\le M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta\|x\|_{[X_0,X_1]_\theta}\qquad(x\in[X_0,X_1]_\theta).$$
The zero-bound convention is $0^t=0$ for $t>0$. In particular, for $B\ge I$ self-adjoint on a nonzero complex Hilbert space $K$ and real $a_0,a_1$, the source endpoints may be $X_j=K_B^{a_j}$ or $X_j=L^2(I_\sigma;K_B^{a_j})$, where $I_\sigma=(0,\sigma)$ and $0<\sigma<\infty$. Their interpolation spaces are respectively $K_B^{a_\theta}$ and $L^2(I_\sigma;K_B^{a_\theta})$, with exact norms and $a_\theta=(1-\theta)a_0+\theta a_1$.

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] Completed spectral scale levels and their unitary maps are compatible on the spectral cutoff core [lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-scale-identifications](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-scale-identifications).

[F2] The exact spectral interpolation identity and the strip operator argument apply to positive self-adjoint operators on any complex Hilbert spaces [lem-author-03489e9088b7-exact-scale-operator-interpolation](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-exact-scale-operator-interpolation).

[F3] The converse spectral theorem constructs a self-adjoint operator from a regular real PVM [math:thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators).

[F4] The PVM norm integral and monotone spectral approximation determine fractional domains and powers [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus).

[F5] Bochner Lp completeness on a finite interval holds for an arbitrary real Banach value space under countable choice; apply it to the underlying real space of H [math:thm-pe12-bochner-lp-completeness](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-pe12-bochner-lp-completeness).

[F6] AC supplies the choice assumptions of the spectral and Bochner completeness inputs [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

[F7] A PVM has orthogonal projection values, empty-set and whole-space normalization, intersection multiplication and strong countable additivity; regularity means regularity of every scalar measure [math:def-projection-valued-measure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-projection-valued-measure).

[F8] Strong measurability is the almost-everywhere simple-approximation convention of [math:def-strongly-measurable-banach-valued-function](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-strongly-measurable-banach-valued-function). Realification preserves the norm and metric by [math:lem-complex-norm-realification-is-a-real-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-complex-norm-realification-is-a-real-norm). Scalar complex $L^2$ norms and the integral Cauchy–Schwarz inequality are supplied by [math:thm-complex-holder-minkowski-and-the-quotient-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-complex-holder-minkowski-and-the-quotient-norm), and the inner-product Cauchy–Schwarz inequality by [math:thm-cauchy-schwarz-for-real-and-complex-inner-product-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-cauchy-schwarz-for-real-and-complex-inner-product-spaces).

[F9] Scalar dominated convergence on an arbitrary measure space is [math:thm-dominated-convergence](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-dominated-convergence). Nonnegative measurable functions admit increasing measurable simple approximations by [math:thm-nonnegative-measurable-functions-admit-increasing-simple-approximations](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-nonnegative-measurable-functions-admit-increasing-simple-approximations), and their increasing integrals converge by [math:thm-monotone-convergence-for-the-integral](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-monotone-convergence-for-the-integral). These apply to Lebesgue time measure as well as to scalar spectral measures; the dominated limits below have integrable squared-norm majorants.

[F10] Compatible complex Banach couples, their sum norms and the admissible strip interpolation convention are defined in [lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-analytic-strip-profiles](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-analytic-strip-profiles). Only these definitions are used for the arbitrary source couple; its interpolation identity is not assumed to be spectral.

### Proof

**Given:** The data and AC in the statement.

**Goal:** All asserted identifications and bounds, with the stated exact norms.

1.1 Bounded linear maps preserve strong measurability by application to simple approximants. The usual integral pairing $\langle u,v\rangle=\int\langle u(t),v(t)\rangle_Hdt$ is defined by the inner-product and scalar integral Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities in [F8], is positive definite on the almost-everywhere quotient, and has the displayed norm. Completeness follows by applying the real Bochner completeness theorem to the underlying real Banach space of $H$; the norm, measurable simple functions and Cauchy sequences are unchanged by this realification, as specified in [F8]. Hence $\mathscr H$ is a complex Hilbert space, without any separability assumption on $H$. For each Borel $C$, $\mathscr E(C)$ is a bounded orthogonal projection, as is checked pointwise. The identities $\mathscr E(\varnothing)=0$, $\mathscr E(\mathbb R)=I$ and $\mathscr E(C\cap D)=\mathscr E(C)\mathscr E(D)$ also hold pointwise, by the corresponding identities for $E_A$. For disjoint $C_n$, the finite partial sums converge pointwise strongly to $E_A(\bigcup C_n)u(t)$; their squared differences are dominated by $\|u(t)\|^2$. Scalar dominated convergence in [F9] proves strong countable additivity on $\mathscr H$. [F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, F8, F9]

2.1 The scalar spectral measure is $\nu_u(C)=\|\mathscr E(C)u\|^2=\int_{I_\tau}\|E_A(C)u(t)\|^2dt$. It is regular: approximate $u$ in $L^2$ by finite-valued measurable simple functions $v$. Such approximation follows by truncating the norm of $u$, taking its defining simple approximants, clipping them to a fixed ball, and using scalar dominated convergence from [F9], then letting that ball increase. For simple $v$ on disjoint time sets, $\nu_v$ is a finite positive linear combination of the regular scalar measures of $E_A$, so regular. For a finite disjoint Borel partition $(C_k)$, Cauchy–Schwarz from [F8] for the orthogonal projections gives $\sum_k|\nu_u(C_k)-\nu_v(C_k)|\le(\|u\|+\|v\|)\|u-v\|$. Thus $\nu_v\to\nu_u$ in total variation. Given a Borel set and an error tolerance, first take such a $v$ with small variation error and then compact inner and open outer approximants for $\nu_v$; their errors for $\nu_u$ are also small. This proves regularity of each $\nu_u$ and hence of the PVM (polarization also gives regular cross measures). The converse spectral theorem now gives $\mathscr A$ as asserted. [F3, F4, F7, F8, F9, step 1.1]

3.1 For every nonnegative Borel $w$, the identity $\int w\,d\nu_u=\int_{I_\tau}(\int w\,d(E_A)_{u(t)})dt$ follows first for indicators from step 2.1, then for nonnegative simple functions by finite addition, then for arbitrary $w$ by the increasing simple approximations and scalar monotone convergence in [F9] on both sides. In particular for $s\ge0$ the exact norm formula with $w(\lambda)=\lambda^s$ shows that $D(\mathscr A^{s/2})$ consists precisely of $u(t)\in D(A^{s/2})$ almost everywhere with $\int\|A^{s/2}u(t)\|^2dt<\infty$, and gives equality of the norms. The pointwise power is strongly measurable because the bounded spectral truncations are strongly measurable and converge in $H$ pointwise on this domain; conversely the inverse $A^{-s/2}:H\to H_A^s$ is bounded, so the domain functions are strongly measurable also as $H_A^s$-valued functions. Truncated multipliers prove that $\mathscr A^{s/2}u(t)=A^{s/2}u(t)$ on this domain. [F1, F4, F8, F9, step 1.1, step 2.1]

4.1 For any real $s$, the core isometry $u\mapsto\mathscr A^{s/2}u$ extends onto $\mathscr H$ by the cutoff argument. The pointwise map $u(t)\mapsto J_su(t)$ is also an onto isometry $L^2(I_\tau;H_A^s)\to\mathscr H$, since $J_s$ and its inverse are bounded maps between their indicated Hilbert spaces and preserve strong measurability. On the ranges of $\mathscr E([1,N])$, these two maps agree by the bounded multiplier identities and step 3.1; these cutoff ranges lie in $D(\mathscr A^\infty)$ and are dense at every completed level. In the Bochner space they are dense because $E_A([1,N])$ converges strongly and contractively at level $s$, followed by scalar dominated convergence from [F9] in time with majorant $\|u(t)\|_s^2$. This proves the canonical isometric completion identity for all real $s$, consistently in common smaller-index spaces. Applying exact spectral interpolation to $\mathscr A$ proves the displayed identity. [F1, F2, F4, F8, F9, step 1.1, step 3.1]

5.1 Consistency makes $L$ well defined: two decompositions of $x$ differ by an element of $X_0\cap X_1$, on which the endpoint maps agree. For any decomposition $x=x_0+x_1$, the target sum norm satisfies $\|Lx\|_{Y_0+Y_1}\le M_0\|x_0\|_{X_0}+M_1\|x_1\|_{X_1}$. Infimizing gives $\|Lx\|_{Y_0+Y_1}\le\max(M_0,M_1)\|x\|_{X_0+X_1}$. Hence, for an admissible source function $F$, composition $LF$ is bounded and continuous into the target sum. Its interior difference quotients converge to $LF'(z)$ by this boundedness, so it is holomorphic. On boundary $j$, the restriction is $L_jF(j+it)$; boundedness of $L_j$ preserves endpoint continuity and decay, with supremum norm at most $M_j\|F\|_{\mathcal F}$. This argument uses only the arbitrary compatible source couple, without requiring it to be a spectral couple. [given, F10, step 4.1]

6.1 Fix $x\in[X_0,X_1]_\theta$ and an admissible $F$ with $F(\theta)=x$. For $\delta>0$ put $m_j=M_j+\delta>0$ and $G(z)=(m_0/m_1)^{z-\theta}LF(z)$, using the real logarithm of the positive ratio. This scalar factor is holomorphic and bounded on the closed strip, with boundary modulus $(m_0/m_1)^{j-\theta}$, so $G$ is admissible in the target and $G(\theta)=Lx$. Its boundary supremum at $j=0$ is at most $M_0(m_0/m_1)^{-\theta}\|F\|_{\mathcal F}\le m_0^{1-\theta}m_1^\theta\|F\|_{\mathcal F}$, and at $j=1$ it is at most $M_1(m_0/m_1)^{1-\theta}\|F\|_{\mathcal F}\le m_0^{1-\theta}m_1^\theta\|F\|_{\mathcal F}$. Taking the infimum over $F$ and then $\delta\downarrow0$ proves the geometric-mean bound, including either zero endpoint bound. Step 4.1 identifies the target interpolation norm exactly with $L^2(I_\tau;H_A^{b_\theta})$. For the two stated source specializations, [F2] gives the spectral-source identity, and the construction in steps 1.1–4.1 applied to $B,K,I_\sigma$ gives the Bochner-source identity; both preserve the exact norm. [given, F2, F10, step 4.1, step 5.1] ∎



## Positive spectral Hilbert scale: duality and interpolation

[Open item](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract) · level 5



### Statement

Assume AC and let $A\ge I$ be self-adjoint on a nonzero complex Hilbert space $H$. Complete $D(A^\infty)=\bigcap_{n\ge1}D(A^n)$ in $\|A^{s/2}u\|_H$ to obtain $H_A^s$ for every real $s$. For $s\ge0$ this is $D(A^{s/2})$; the pivot pairing identifies $H_A^{-s}$ isometrically with the antidual of $H_A^s$, where the antidual consists of bounded conjugate-linear scalar functionals and the inner product is linear in the first variable. The map $A^{-r/2}:H_A^s\to H_A^{s+r}$ is isometric and onto for all real $r,s$. For $0<\theta<1$,
$$[H_A^a,H_A^b]_\theta=H_A^{(1-\theta)a+\theta b}$$
isometrically, using bounded continuous sum-valued strip functions holomorphic inside with endpoint-continuous boundary values vanishing at imaginary infinity, and the infimum of the maximum endpoint supremum norm.

Let $B\ge I$ be self-adjoint on another nonzero complex Hilbert space $K$. If a consistent complex-linear map is bounded $H_A^{a_j}\to K_B^{b_j}$, $j=0,1$, with bounds $M_j$, then it is bounded between the interpolated indices with norm at most $M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta$. This also holds for target $L^2(0,\tau;K_B^b)$, $0<\tau<\infty$; in particular interpolation of these target spaces is isometric to $L^2(0,\tau;K_B^{(1-\theta)b_0+\theta b_1})$.

The unitary group $e^{it(I-A)}$ extends consistently to every scale level and commutes with all scale powers. For a real form realization on $H=L^2(\Omega;\mathbb C)$, complex conjugation $Ju=\overline u$ is an isometric real-linear involution at every level. Precisely, let $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb R^n$ be nonempty and open, $n\ge1$, and let $Q\subseteq H$ be a dense complex linear subspace invariant under $J$. Let $q:Q\times Q\to\mathbb C$ be sesquilinear, linear in its first variable, with $q(u,v)=\overline{q(v,u)}$, $q(u,u)\ge\|u\|_2^2$, and $Q$ complete for $(q(u,u)+\|u\|_2^2)^{1/2}$. Suppose $q(Ju,Jv)=\overline{q(u,v)}$. Here $A$ is the unique self-adjoint operator represented by $q$: its domain consists exactly of $u\in Q$ for which some $f\in H$ satisfies $q(u,v)=\langle f,v\rangle_2$ for every $v\in Q$, and then $Au=f$. In particular $A\ge I$.

The real-coefficient Dirichlet energy forms intended here are defined on $Q=H^1_0(\Omega;\mathbb C)$, the $H^1$ closure of $C_c^\infty(\Omega;\mathbb C)$, by
$$q(u,v)=\int_\Omega\left(\sum_{i,j=1}^n a^{ij}(x)D_ju\,\overline{D_iv}+c(x)u\overline v\right)\,dx.$$
The coefficients $a^{ij}$ are real measurable essentially bounded, $a^{ij}=a^{ji}$ almost everywhere, and for some $\kappa>0$ satisfy $\sum_{i,j}a^{ij}(x)\xi_j\xi_i\ge\kappa|\xi|^2$ for all $\xi\in\mathbb R^n$ almost everywhere; $c$ is real essentially bounded with $c\ge1$ almost everywhere. These forms satisfy the preceding hypotheses, including conjugation invariance, on every such open set. Taking $a^{ij}=\delta_{ij}$ and $c=1$ gives the shifted Dirichlet energy for $I-\Delta_D$, including the exterior-domain realization.

Here the Dirichlet Laplacian is defined by $\Delta_D=-L$ on $D(\Delta_D)=D(L)$, where $L\ge0$ is the unique self-adjoint operator represented by the unshifted form $q_0(u,v)=\sum_{j=1}^n\int_\Omega D_ju\,\overline{D_jv}\,dx$ on $Q=H^1_0(\Omega;\mathbb C)$. Explicitly, $D(L)$ consists of those $u\in Q$ for which some $f\in L^2(\Omega;\mathbb C)$ satisfies $q_0(u,v)=\langle f,v\rangle_2$ for every $v\in Q$, and then $Lu=f$. For $a^{ij}=\delta_{ij}$ and $c=1$, the represented shifted operator satisfies $D(A)=D(L)$ and $Au=u+Lu$, so $A=I-\Delta_D$.

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] The cutoff-core lemma constructs the compatible completed scales, their onto unitary identifications and all scale powers [lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-scale-identifications](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-spectral-cutoff-scale-identifications).

[F2] Exact complex interpolation of scales and its endpoint operator bound are proved by analytic profiles and cutoff dual tests [lem-author-03489e9088b7-exact-scale-operator-interpolation](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-exact-scale-operator-interpolation).

[F3] The pointwise lifted PVM gives the exact L2-in-time scale identity, without replacing equality of norms by equivalence [lem-author-03489e9088b7-bochner-lifted-spectral-measure](item-lem-author-03489e9088b7-bochner-lifted-spectral-measure).

[F4] Under countable choice, Hilbert-space Riesz representation is onto and isometric; use it for the pivot antidual after conjugating the functional [math:thm-riesz-representation-for-hilbert-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-riesz-representation-for-hilbert-space).

[F5] This physics-namespace supplier has domain mathematics and mathematical premises: for a self-adjoint generator its spectral exponential is a strongly continuous unitary group and commutes with its bounded spectral calculus [physics:lem-nrqm-autonomous-dynamics-flow-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/physics/item/lem-nrqm-autonomous-dynamics-flow-calculus).

[F6] Uniqueness of the regular spectral PVM for a self-adjoint operator identifies conjugated spectral projections [math:thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-spectral-theorem-for-unbounded-self-adjoint-operators).

[F7] The Borel functional calculus supplies the exact domains and norm identities for real powers, products and bounded unitary multipliers [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus).

[F8] Full AC is assumed exactly for the spectral suppliers and hence for their transitive choice requirements, including countable choice for Riesz and Bochner completeness [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

[F9] Closed semibounded symmetric forms use the first-variable-linear pairing and the positive associated-operator convention [physics:def-nrqm-coulomb-closed-semibounded-forms](https://alphabetamath.cc/physics/item/def-nrqm-coulomb-closed-semibounded-forms). Under AC, a densely defined symmetric form bounded below and complete in its shifted form norm has a unique self-adjoint representing operator, with exactly the domain characterization stated above [physics:thm-nrqmx-closed-form-representation](https://alphabetamath.cc/physics/item/thm-nrqmx-closed-form-representation). Both suppliers have domain mathematics.

[F10] Complex weak Sobolev derivatives use the bilinear test identity, and $H^1_0$ is the $H^1$ closure of the test functions [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm) [math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure). Under countable choice $H^1$ and its closed subspace $H^1_0$ are Hilbert spaces with squared norm $\|u\|_2^2+\sum_j\|D_ju\|_2^2$ [math:lem-w-one-two-is-a-hilbert-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-w-one-two-is-a-hilbert-space). On every open set the complex test functions, hence $H^1_0$, are dense in complex $L^2$ [math:lem-smooth-compactly-supported-functions-are-dense-in-ltwo-of-an-open-set](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-smooth-compactly-supported-functions-are-dense-in-ltwo-of-an-open-set).

### Proof

**Given:** The data and AC in the statement.

**Goal:** All asserted identifications and bounds, with the stated exact norms.

1.1 The cutoff-core construction identifies $H_A^s$ with the completed spectral domain by the onto map $J_su=A^{s/2}u$, gives the claimed positive-index domain equality, and extends $A^{-r/2}$ as an onto isometry. For $s\ge0$ and core vectors $u,v$, the product rule gives $\langle u,v\rangle_H=\langle A^{-s/2}u,A^{s/2}v\rangle_H$. Thus define the pivot pairing for all $u\in H_A^{-s},v\in H_A^s$ by $\langle u,v\rangle_{-s,s}=\langle J_{-s}u,J_sv\rangle_H$. It extends the pivot pairing on the dense core, is linear in $u$ and conjugate-linear in $v$, and has norm bound $\|u\|_{-s}\|v\|_s$. Its functional norm equals $\|u\|_{-s}$ by taking $J_sv$ to be the normalized $J_{-s}u$ when nonzero. Conversely, for a bounded conjugate-linear functional $\ell$ on $H_A^s$, Riesz applied to $x\mapsto\overline{\ell(J_s^{-1}x)}$ gives $y\in H$ with $\ell(J_s^{-1}x)=\langle y,x\rangle$. Taking $u=J_{-s}^{-1}y$ proves onto antidual identification and the exact norm identity. This is the pivot antidual, with no identification with an unrestricted distribution space. [F1, F4, F7, F8]

1.2 The exact scale interpolation lemma applies to the compatible embeddings from the first fact for arbitrary real $a,b$, including reversed or equal endpoints. Its proof constructs admissible spectral profiles for the upper bound and tests arbitrary profiles against spectral-cutoff dual vectors for the lower bound, so the equality here is isometric in the stated strip convention. Apply its operator transport argument to the consistent endpoint map and the two scale couples to obtain $M_0^{1-\theta}M_1^\theta$, including zero endpoint bounds. For the time-dependent target use the lifted-PVM lemma on $L^2(0,\tau;K)$, which identifies all completed scale levels with the indicated Bochner spaces with exactly their integral norms. The same strip composition proof then gives the asserted time-target bound. [F1, F2, F3]

1.3 The Borel calculus makes $I-A$ self-adjoint. The mathematical flow supplier, with generator $I-A$, gives the strongly continuous unitary group $U(t)=e^{it(I-A)}$ on $H$. The pushforward of $E_A$ by $\lambda\mapsto1-\lambda$ represents $I-A$, as one checks first on real simple multipliers and then by truncation; spectral uniqueness therefore gives $U(t)=\int e^{it(1-\lambda)}\,dE_A(\lambda)$. Its multipliers have modulus one and commute with the spectral powers of $A$ on their domains: truncate $A$ to $E_NH$, use bounded multiplication there, and pass to the exact domain norm using spectral convergence. In particular $U(t)$ preserves the core and $\|U(t)u\|_s=\|u\|_s$. Extension and the inverse $U(-t)$ make it unitary at every level. On the dense core the group law, consistency of the embeddings, and commutation with each $A^{-r/2}$ hold; bounded extension between the indicated levels gives them everywhere. Also $J_sU(t)=U(t)J_s$ on the core and hence on the whole scale, so strong continuity follows from strong continuity on $H$. [F1, F5, F6, F7, F8]

1.4 First check the specified Dirichlet energies. The bounded coefficients and the inequality $2|zw|\le|z|^2+|w|^2$ show that every integrand is integrable for $u,v\in H^1_0$, so $q$ is well defined on Sobolev classes; it is sesquilinear, and symmetry follows by conjugating and exchanging $i,j$. Write $u=u_1+iu_2$ with real weak Sobolev components. The real symmetric matrix gives $\sum_{i,j}a^{ij}D_ju\,\overline{D_iu}=\sum_{i,j}a^{ij}(D_ju_1D_iu_1+D_ju_2D_iu_2)$ because its imaginary cross terms cancel. Ellipticity therefore gives $q[u]\ge\kappa\|\nabla u\|_2^2+\|u\|_2^2$, where $q[u]=q(u,u)$ and $\|\nabla u\|_2^2=\sum_j\|D_ju\|_2^2$. With $L=\max_{i,j}\|a^{ij}\|_\infty$, the reverse bound is $q[u]\le nL\|\nabla u\|_2^2+\|c\|_\infty\|u\|_2^2$. Consequently $q[u]+\|u\|_2^2$ is bounded below by $\min(\kappa,2)\|u\|_{H^1}^2$ and above by $\max(nL,\|c\|_\infty+1)\|u\|_{H^1}^2$. Completeness of $H^1_0$ and these two inequalities prove closedness in the required form norm. Its density in $L^2$ is [F10]. For a weak derivative, conjugate its bilinear test identity with test function $\overline\varphi$ to obtain $D_j(Ju)=J(D_ju)$; this also shows $\|Ju\|_{H^1}=\|u\|_{H^1}$. Conjugating test-function approximants proves $JQ=Q$. Conjugating the energy integrand, using the real coefficients, proves $q(Ju,Jv)=\overline{q(u,v)}$. Thus [F9] constructs the unique representing self-adjoint $A$, with the stated domain characterization; on $D(A)$, $\langle Au,u\rangle=q[u]\ge\|u\|_2^2$, hence $A\ge I$. This verifies every form hypothesis without requiring a bounded domain or a boundary trace theorem. [F8, F9, F10, given, algebra]

2.1 For the specialization $a^{ij}=\delta_{ij}$ and $c=1$, consider the unshifted form $q_0$ in the statement on the same $Q=H^1_0(\Omega;\mathbb C)$. It is well defined, symmetric and nonnegative, and $q_0[u]+\|u\|_2^2=\|u\|_{H^1}^2$. By [F10], $Q$ is dense in $L^2$ and complete in this form norm. Thus [F9], with lower bound zero and the assumed AC, gives its unique nonnegative self-adjoint representing operator $L$ and exactly the stated characterization of $D(L)$ and $Lu$. This defines $\Delta_D=-L$ on $D(L)$. The shifted form is $q(u,v)=q_0(u,v)+\langle u,v\rangle_2$. If $u\in D(L)$, then $q(u,v)=\langle Lu+u,v\rangle_2$ for all $v\in Q$, so the domain characterization for $A$ gives $u\in D(A)$ and $Au=Lu+u$. Conversely, if $u\in D(A)$, then $q_0(u,v)=\langle Au-u,v\rangle_2$ for every $v\in Q$, giving $u\in D(L)$ and $Lu=Au-u$. Therefore $D(A)=D(L)$ and $A=I+L=I-\Delta_D$, as operators with this common domain. The argument applies to every nonempty open $\Omega$, hence also to the exterior domains in the statement. [F8, F9, F10, step 1.4, given, algebra]

3.1 Now take any real form realization in the statement, including those just constructed. The map $J$ is a conjugate-linear isometric involution on $L^2(\Omega)$. By [F9], $u\in D(A)$ with $Au=f$ exactly when $u\in Q$ and $q(u,v)=\langle f,v\rangle$ for every $v\in Q$. For such $u$, the real-form identity and $JQ=Q$ give $q(Ju,v)=\overline{q(u,Jv)}=\langle JAu,v\rangle$, so $Ju\in D(A)$ and $AJu=JAu$; applying $J$ twice proves $JD(A)=D(A)$. The regular PVM $E'(C)=JE_A(C)J$ is complex-linear projection-valued: the two conjugate-linear factors cancel, orthogonality and countable additivity are preserved by the isometry, and its scalar measures obey $\langle E'(C)x,x\rangle=\langle E_A(C)Jx,Jx\rangle$, so regularity is preserved. For real simple spectral multipliers, conjugation transports their spectral integrals by $J$; truncation gives the same assertion for the identity multiplier with its exact square-integrability domain. Hence $E'$ represents $JAJ=A$. Spectral uniqueness gives $E'=E_A$. Applying the same real-multiplier and truncation argument to every real power shows that $J$ commutes with that power on its domain and preserves the core. Thus $\|Ju\|_s=\|A^{s/2}Ju\|_2=\|JA^{s/2}u\|_2=\|u\|_s$ on the core. The unique continuous extension to each completion is real-linear and isometric, and has square the identity by density. Together with the preceding steps this proves all the assertions. [F1, F6, F7, F8, F9, step 1.1, step 1.2, step 1.3, step 1.4, step 2.1] ∎

### Source notes

This is a locally authored proof composed from the three direct spectral-scale helpers and the declared library inputs. The interpolation and Bochner identities retain their exact norms. The form representation theorem is reused with its actual mathematical hypotheses; the stated real Dirichlet energies and their conjugation invariance are checked locally. The physics-namespace inputs have domain mathematics and contribute no physical premise. This proposal claims neither independent review nor an original-article defect.


## Independent exterior fractional Sobolev embedding

[Open item](item-thm-se-exterior-fractional-sobolev-embedding) · level 5


### Statement

Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $\Omega\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a smooth exterior domain, meaning the complement of a compact set with smooth boundary, and let $0<a<1$, $1<q<\infty$ and $aq>n$. With the complex-interpolation convention $\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)=[L^q(\Omega),W^{1,q}(\Omega)]_a$ of [lem-se-interpolation-sobolev-model](item-lem-se-interpolation-sobolev-model), there is a constant $C$ such that $\|u\|_{L^\infty(\Omega)}\le C\|u\|_{\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)}$ for every $u\in\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)$.

In particular, the hypotheses hold when $n=2$, $p>2$, $a=1-1/p$ and $q=2p/(p-2)$.

### External input

For $0<a<1$, $1<q<\infty$ and $aq>n$, the cited whole-space theorem continuously embeds $H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)$ into bounded Hölder-continuous functions. Here $H^{a,q}$ denotes the whole-space Bessel-potential space, equivalently $[L^q(\mathbb R^n),W^{1,q}(\mathbb R^n)]_a$ with equivalent norms. This input makes no exterior-domain assertion.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and notation of the Statement.

[F1] The Axiom of Choice is assumed as in [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and in the interpolation-model supplier.

[F2] By [lem-se-interpolation-sobolev-model](item-lem-se-interpolation-sobolev-model), under these hypotheses there is a bounded extension $E:\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)\to H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)$ such that $(Eu)|_\Omega=u$ almost everywhere. Its whole-space Bessel and complex-interpolation norms are equivalent.

[F3] The independent whole-space input in the External input section provides a constant $C_0$ such that $\|F\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)}\le C_0\|F\|_{H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)}$ for every $F\in H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)$.

[F4] For $D=\Omega$ or $D=\mathbb R^n$, the norm $\|v\|_{L^\infty(D)}$ is the essential supremum of the real-valued measurable function $|v|$ with respect to Lebesgue measure on $D$, using [math:def-essential-supremum-with-respect-to-a-measure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-essential-supremum-with-respect-to-a-measure). Thus it is the infimum of the bounds $M\in[0,\infty]$ satisfying $|v|\le M$ almost everywhere on $D$.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and notation of the Statement.

[F1] The Axiom of Choice is assumed as in [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice) and in the interpolation-model supplier.

[F2] By [lem-se-interpolation-sobolev-model](item-lem-se-interpolation-sobolev-model), under these hypotheses there is a bounded extension $E:\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)\to H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)$ such that $(Eu)|_\Omega=u$ almost everywhere. Its whole-space Bessel and complex-interpolation norms are equivalent.

[F3] The independent whole-space input in the External input section provides a constant $C_0$ such that $\|F\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)}\le C_0\|F\|_{H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)}$ for every $F\in H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)$.



**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish the asserted bounded inclusion and verify the stated parameter example.

1.1 For $u\in\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)$, set $F=Eu$. The extension in [F2] gives $F|_\Omega=u$ almost everywhere and $\|F\|_{H^{a,q}(\mathbb R^n)}\le C_E\|u\|_{\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)}$ for a constant $C_E$ independent of $u$. [F1, F2, given]

2.1 If $M$ is a finite almost-everywhere bound for $|F|$ on $\mathbb R^n$, its exceptional null set restricts to a Lebesgue-null set in $\Omega$. Since $F|_\Omega=u$ almost everywhere by step 1.1, adjoining the null set where this equality fails shows $|u|\le M$ almost everywhere on $\Omega$. Thus every such bound for $F$ is a bound for $u$. Taking infima in the definition in [F4] gives $\|u\|_{L^\infty(\Omega)}\le\|F\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)}$; finite bounds exist by [F3]. Consequently the whole-space bound in [F3] and step 1.1 give $\|u\|_{L^\infty(\Omega)}\le\|F\|_{L^\infty(\mathbb R^n)}\le C_0C_E\|u\|_{\mathcal W^{a,q}(\Omega)}$. This proves the continuous inclusion. [F3, F4, step 1.1]

3.1 If $p>2$, then $0<1-1/p<1$ and $1<2p/(p-2)<\infty$. Moreover $aq=2(p-1)/(p-2)=2+2/(p-2)>2=n$, so the same inclusion applies to the stated parameters. [step 2.1, algebra, given] ∎

### Source notes

The whole-space embedding is an imported premise; the exterior restriction argument above is local. The published integer-order transfer result does not itself cover this fractional interpolation model, and the published fractional inequality inspected concerns the subcritical Slobodeckij setting.


## Dirichlet spectral Sobolev scale and duality

[Open item](item-def-se-dirichlet-scale) · level 6


### Definition

Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $n\ge2$, let $\Theta\subset\mathbb R^n$ be nonempty and compact with smooth boundary, and let its exterior $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$ be connected. Work in the complex Hilbert space $L^2(\Omega)$, with inner product $\langle u,v\rangle_2=\int_\Omega u\overline v\,dx$, linear in the first argument. Let $\Delta_D$ be the Dirichlet Laplacian with domain $H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$, and put $A=I-\Delta_D$.

For $\sigma\in\mathbb R$, let $D(A^\infty)=\bigcap_{k\ge1}D(A^k)$ and define $H_D^\sigma(\Omega)$ as its completion in the norm $\|u\|_{H_D^\sigma}=\|A^{\sigma/2}u\|_2$, where the powers are defined by the spectral calculus. For $\sigma\ge0$, this completion is identified with $D(A^{\sigma/2})$, equipped with that norm. In particular $H_D^0(\Omega)=L^2(\Omega)$, $H_D^1(\Omega)=H^1_0(\Omega)$ and $H_D^2(\Omega)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$, as spaces with their respective indicated norms.

For $s\ge0$, identify $H_D^{-s}(\Omega)$ isometrically with the antidual of $H_D^s(\Omega)$: the antidual is the space of bounded conjugate-linear scalar functionals, with its operator norm. The pairing $\langle f,g\rangle_{-s,s}$ is linear in $f$ and conjugate-linear in $g$ and extends $\int_\Omega f\overline g\,dx$ from pairs in $D(A^\infty)$. The integral is therefore a pivot notation for this extension; arbitrary negative-order elements need not be functions for which the integral is defined.

Write $R=A^{-1}$. It extends to an isometric isomorphism $R:H_D^\sigma(\Omega)\to H_D^{\sigma+2}(\Omega)$ for every real $\sigma$. The group $e^{it\Delta_D}$ extends consistently to a unitary group on every $H_D^\sigma(\Omega)$ and commutes there with $R$.

### Definition references

[def-se-geometry](item-def-se-geometry)

[def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation](item-def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation)

[def-se-dirichlet-laplacian](item-def-se-dirichlet-laplacian)

[lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization)

[lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract)

[math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

### Justification

The geometry is fixed by [def-se-geometry](item-def-se-geometry), and the weak Sobolev spaces and the closure defining $H^1_0(\Omega)$ are fixed by [def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation](item-def-se-foundation-integer-sobolev-notation). Under Choice, [def-se-dirichlet-laplacian](item-def-se-dirichlet-laplacian) supplies the displayed Dirichlet realization and its self-adjoint shift $A\ge I$ on the nonzero complex Hilbert space $L^2(\Omega)$. The powers and their domains are defined by [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus), with the Choice assumption of that supplier recorded by [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

The positive spectral-scale result [lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract) applies to this $A$: it supplies the normed completion, the positive-domain identification, and the isometric pivot-antidual identification. In particular, it gives a definite norm on the common core and a complete space at each real index. Its pivot pairing extends the $L^2$ inner product by continuity, so its notation does not assert that a general negative-order element is an integrable function.

The realization theorem [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization) gives $D(A^{1/2})=H^1_0(\Omega)$ with $\|A^{1/2}u\|_2^2=\|u\|_2^2+\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ and $D(A)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$. The latter space carries the scale norm $\|Au\|_2$, which that theorem states is equivalent to its $H^2$ norm. The same spectral-scale result supplies the stated extension of $A^{-1}$, the unitary evolution and their commutation.

### Source notes

The article is cited for the scale, pivot pairing and notation on PDF page 8. The mathematical justifications are the independent suppliers listed above; the article is not used as proof of its own definition or its properties.

## Endpoint multiplier bounds extended to negative spectral orders

[Open item](item-lem-se-spectral-cutoff-duality) · level 6


### Statement

Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$, $n\ge2$, where $\Theta$ is compact with smooth boundary and $\Omega$ is open. Let $A=I-\Delta_D\ge I$ be the shifted Dirichlet Laplacian and let $H_D^s$ be the associated Hilbert scale, with pivot $L^2(\Omega)$ and norm $\|A^{s/2}u\|_2$ on its spectral core. For every complex-valued $a\in W^{2,\infty}(\Omega)$ and every $-2\le s\le2$, multiplication by $a$ extends to a bounded complex-linear operator on $H_D^s$, with norm at most $C_{s,\Omega}\|a\|_{W^{2,\infty}}$. Here $W^{k,p}(\Omega;\mathbb C)$ is the space of almost-everywhere classes with weak derivatives through order $k$ in $L^p$, and $H^k(\Omega)=W^{k,2}(\Omega;\mathbb C)$ for $k=0,1,2$, with $\|u\|_{H^k}^2=\sum_{|\alpha|\le k}\|D^\alpha u\|_2^2$. In particular $W^{2,\infty}$ has norm $\|a\|_{W^{2,\infty}}=\max_{|\alpha|\le2}\|D^\alpha a\|_\infty$.

For $s>0$, the negative-order extension is defined by $\langle af,u\rangle_{-s,s}=\langle f,\overline a u\rangle_{-s,s}$ for $f\in H_D^{-s}$ and $u\in H_D^s$, where the pivot pairing is linear in its first argument. It agrees with ordinary multiplication on $L^2$. Thus the result applies in particular to smooth cutoffs with bounded derivatives through order two and to any potentials belonging to $W^{2,\infty}(\Omega)$. Complex conjugation is an isometry on each of these scale spaces.

### Facts & Assumptions

[F1] The exterior Dirichlet realization gives $D(A)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$ with equivalent graph and $H^2$ norms, and $D(A^{1/2})=H^1_0(\Omega)$ with the energy norm [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization). Here $H^1_0(\Omega)$ is the $H^1$ closure of $C_c^\infty(\Omega)$.

[F2] The positive spectral-scale contract identifies positive levels with domains of powers, identifies negative levels with pivot antiduals, gives bounded-operator interpolation on the scale, and makes conjugation an isometry for the real Dirichlet form [lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract).

[F3] The weak Leibniz rule gives the product formula through any finite order when the multiplier is smooth and the indicated weak derivatives exist [math:lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor).

[F4] The Axiom of Choice is assumed for the spectral and realization suppliers and implies the Countable Choice hypotheses of the Sobolev, product, mollification and extension suppliers. It also permits the approximating sequence used below [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

[F5] Integer-order Sobolev spaces are spaces of almost-everywhere classes characterized by the weak test identities; their norms are derivative sums for finite $p$ and the maximum of essential derivative bounds for $p=\infty$ [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm). We use complex scalars, $H^k=W^{k,2}$ and the norm convention in the Statement.

[F6] For an open set $G$, $b\in W^{k,p}(G;\mathbb C)$, $1\le p\le\infty$, and a nonnegative smooth unit-mass kernel $\rho$ supported in the unit ball, the interior mollification $b_\varepsilon=\rho_\varepsilon*E_0b$ is smooth on $G_\varepsilon=\{x\in G:\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathbb R^n\setminus G)>\varepsilon\}$ and $D^\alpha b_\varepsilon=\rho_\varepsilon*E_0(D^\alpha b)$ there for $|\alpha|\le k$ [math:lem-mollification-commutes-with-weak-derivatives-in-the-interior](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-mollification-commutes-with-weak-derivatives-in-the-interior). Here $\rho_\varepsilon(x)=\varepsilon^{-n}\rho(x/\varepsilon)$, $E_0$ denotes extension by zero, and $G_\varepsilon=\mathbb R^n$ if $G=\mathbb R^n$.

[F7] For the same mollification, if $b\in W^{k,p}(G;\mathbb C)$ and $1\le p<\infty$, then $b_\varepsilon\to b$ in $W^{k,p}(U;\mathbb C)$ for every open $U$ compactly contained in $G$ [math:thm-local-smooth-approximation-in-wkp](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-local-smooth-approximation-in-wkp). We apply this with $p=2$, on bounded interior sets for the coefficient and on $\mathbb R^n$ for compactly supported $H^1$ functions; no $W^{k,\infty}$ norm convergence is used.

[F8] If $v\in W^{k,p}(G;\mathbb C)$ vanishes outside a compact subset of the open set $G$, then $E_0v\in W^{k,p}(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$, $D^\alpha E_0v=E_0D^\alpha v$ for $|\alpha|\le k$, and its Sobolev norm is preserved [math:lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp).

[F9] Complex Holder bounds $\left|\int fg\right|\le\|f\|_2\|g\|_2$, and complex Minkowski bounds finite sums in $L^2$ [math:thm-complex-holder-minkowski-and-the-quotient-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-complex-holder-minkowski-and-the-quotient-norm). These apply with Lebesgue measure on every open set used below.

[F10] A compact subset of an open Euclidean set has a smooth compactly supported cutoff valued in $[0,1]$ and equal to one near that subset [math:lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization). Applied to $\{0\}\subset B_1(0)$ and normalized by its positive finite integral, this supplies a nonnegative $\rho\in C_c^\infty(B_1(0))$ with integral one.

### Proof

**Given:** The domain, scale, choice assumption and multiplier in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove the multiplier bounds at all orders from $-2$ to $2$ and identify the negative-order action.

1.1 Fix $k\in\{1,2\}$, $u\in H^k(\Omega)$ and $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(\Omega)$. Choose bounded open sets $U,V$ with $\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset U$, $\overline U\subset V$ and $\overline V\subset\Omega$, both closures compact, and choose the kernel $\rho$ of [F10]. Let $a_\varepsilon=\rho_\varepsilon*E_0a$. For small $\varepsilon$, [F6] gives smoothness on $U$ and $|D^\beta a_\varepsilon(x)|\le\|D^\beta a\|_\infty$ there for $|\beta|\le k$, since the convolution is an average with a nonnegative unit-mass kernel. Because $V$ is bounded, $a|_V\in W^{k,2}(V)$: each derivative has finite $L^2(V)$ norm bounded by $|V|^{1/2}\|D^\beta a\|_\infty$. For $\varepsilon<\operatorname{dist}(\overline U,\mathbb R^n\setminus V)$ its mollification agrees on $U$ with $a_\varepsilon$. Thus [F7] gives $D^\beta a_\varepsilon\to D^\beta a$ in $L^2(U)$ for every $|\beta|\le k$, including the highest order. [F4, F5, F6, F7, F10, given]

2.1 Apply [F3] on $U$ to $a_\varepsilon$ and $u|_U$. In the weak test identity for $|\alpha|\le k$, pass to the limit against $\varphi$: the left-hand error is bounded by $\|a_\varepsilon-a\|_{L^2(U)}\|uD^\alpha\varphi\|_{L^2(U)}$, and the error in each right-hand product term is bounded by $\|D^\beta a_\varepsilon-D^\beta a\|_{L^2(U)}\|D^{\alpha-\beta}u\,\varphi\|_{L^2(U)}$, both tending to zero by step 1.1 and [F9]. Since the test function was arbitrary, the weak formula is $D^\alpha(au)=\sum_{\beta\le\alpha}\binom{\alpha}{\beta}D^\beta a\,D^{\alpha-\beta}u$ on $\Omega$. Every summand is globally in $L^2$, with norm at most $\|D^\beta a\|_\infty\|D^{\alpha-\beta}u\|_2$. Finite-sum estimates and [F5] therefore give $au\in H^k(\Omega)$ and $\|au\|_{H^k}\le C_{n,k}\|a\|_{W^{k,\infty}}\|u\|_{H^k}$. For $k=0$ the bound is the pointwise $L^\infty$ estimate. [F3, F5, F9, step 1.1, given]

3.1 If $u\in H^1_0(\Omega)$, choose $u_j\in C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ converging to $u$ in $H^1$. Step 2.1 gives $au_j\to au$ in $H^1$. For fixed $j$, $v=au_j$ belongs to $H^1$ and vanishes outside $K_j=\operatorname{supp}u_j$, a compact subset of $\Omega$. By [F8], $E_0v\in H^1(\mathbb R^n)$. Its mollification is smooth by [F6] and vanishes when $\operatorname{dist}(x,K_j)>\varepsilon$, directly from the support of $\rho_\varepsilon$ in its defining integral. Hence for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$ it has compact support in $\Omega$, with all these supports in one bounded ball $B$. By [F7] on $\mathbb R^n$ it converges to $E_0v$ in $H^1(B)$, and therefore globally in $H^1(\mathbb R^n)$, since the differences and their weak derivatives vanish outside $B$. Restriction to $\Omega$ proves $au_j\in H^1_0(\Omega)$; closure then gives $au\in H^1_0(\Omega)$. The case $v=0$ uses the zero approximants. The same proof applies to $\overline a$. Combined with step 2.1 this preserves $H^2\cap H^1_0$ as well. [F1, F4, F5, F6, F7, F8, step 2.1, given]

4.1 By the domain and norm identifications, steps 2.1 and 3.1 give multiplier bounds on $H_D^0=L^2$, $H_D^1=H^1_0$ and $H_D^2=H^2\cap H^1_0$, each bounded by a constant times $\|a\|_{W^{2,\infty}}$. The endpoint operators are consistent because they are all ordinary pointwise multiplication. Interpolation between orders $0$ and $1$, and between orders $1$ and $2$, yields the asserted bound for every $0\le s\le2$. The same bounds hold for multiplication by $\overline a$. [F1, F2, step 2.1, step 3.1]

5.1 For $0<s\le2$, the antidual identification makes $u\mapsto\langle f,\overline a u\rangle_{-s,s}$ a bounded conjugate-linear functional on $H_D^s$, with norm at most $C_{s,\Omega}\|a\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\|f\|_{H_D^{-s}}$. It therefore determines a unique $af\in H_D^{-s}$ with that bound. If $f\in L^2$, then $\langle f,\overline a u\rangle_{L^2}=\langle af,u\rangle_{L^2}$ for $u\in H_D^s$, so this definition agrees with ordinary multiplication. The real Dirichlet form also gives the stated conjugation isometry on the scale. The cutoff and potential cases follow by substituting the stated regularity into the bound. [F2, step 4.1, given] ∎

### Source notes

The proof uses local coefficient mollification and the test-function definition of the zero-boundary space; it does not require a trace product theorem or a higher-power Dirichlet boundary condition. No assertion is made here for orders beyond two.


## Whole-space interpolation of one extension–restriction operator

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation) · level 6


### Statement

Assume AC and use complex scalars throughout. Let $n\ge1$ and let $D\subseteq\mathbb R^n$ be open, and suppose one linear $E:L^2(D)\to L^2(\mathbb R^n)$ is bounded, restricts to a bounded $W^{2,2}(D)\to W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n)$ map, and satisfies $r_DE=I$. Define $T=E r_D$ on all of $L^2(\mathbb R^n)$. Then the same $T$ is bounded on the Fourier spaces $H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ for every $0\le s\le2$. For the restriction quotient
$$H^s(D)=r_D H^s(\mathbb R^n),\qquad \|u\|_{H^s(D)}=\inf_{r_DF=u}\|F\|_{H^s(\mathbb R^n)},$$
the existing $L^2$ map $E$ restricts to a bounded extension $H^s(D)\to H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ and $r_DE=I$ there. These quotient norms are definite and complete, and the extension value is independent of the choice of $F$. Whole-space $H^s$ here has the exact norm $\|\langle\xi\rangle^s\widehat F\|_2$, with $\langle\xi\rangle=(1+|\xi|^2)^{1/2}$.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) gives the weighted Fourier distribution convention and exact norm; [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) gives only norm equivalence with weak $W^{2,2}$ at the integer endpoint.

[F3] [math:ex-unbounded-multiplication-operator-and-its-domain](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-unbounded-multiplication-operator-and-its-domain) constructs the self-adjoint real multiplication operator with its exact domain and Borel calculus on $L^2$; [math:thm-plancherel](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-plancherel) transports it unitarily to physical space.

[F4] [lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract) gives isometric interpolation of positive self-adjoint Hilbert scales and the common-operator geometric-mean bound, with the stated decaying strip convention.

[F5] [math:thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-quotient-of-banach-by-closed-subspace-is-banach) makes the quotient by a closed kernel Banach; AC supplies its countable-choice requirement.

[F6] For an open set $U\subseteq\mathbb R^n$, $W^{2,2}(U)$ means $W^{2,2}(U;\mathbb C)$ from [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm): almost-everywhere classes with complex weak derivatives through order two in $L^2$, equipped with the square root of the sum of their squared $L^2$ norms. Under AC, restriction from $W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$ to $W^{2,2}(D;\mathbb C)$ is a contraction by [math:lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces), including the empty-set case.

[F7] [physics:lem-nrqm-oscillator-unitary-transport-of-spectral-pvms](https://alphabetamath.cc/physics/item/lem-nrqm-oscillator-unitary-transport-of-spectral-pvms) has domain mathematics and, under AC and its countable instance, transports a self-adjoint operator and its spectral PVM along a bijective complex-linear isometry. [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus) gives the squared-integrability domains, exact norm identities and bounded-truncation limits for Borel multipliers. Together these apply to the inverse Plancherel unitary and the real multiplier $1+|\xi|^2$; no physical premise is used.

### Proof

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement under the stated hypotheses.

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

1.1 Use the self-adjoint multiplication operator $M_{1+|\xi|^2}$ and the Plancherel unitary to define $B=\mathcal F_2^{-1}M_{1+|\xi|^2}\mathcal F_2$ with domain $\mathcal F_2^{-1}D(M_{1+|\xi|^2})$. The transport lemma in [F7], with $U=\mathcal F_2^{-1}$, makes $B$ self-adjoint with spectral PVM $P_B(C)=\mathcal F_2^{-1}P_M(C)\mathcal F_2$. Its scalar spectral measure at $F$ is the multiplier's scalar spectral measure at $\mathcal F_2F$. The multiplication model and the calculus domain and norm identities therefore give $D(B^{s/2})=\{F\in L^2:\langle\xi\rangle^s\mathcal F_2F\in L^2\}$ and $\|B^{s/2}F\|_2=\|\langle\xi\rangle^s\mathcal F_2F\|_2$ for $s\ge0$. The multiplier is at least one, so $B\ge I$; the calculus is transported first for simple bounded multipliers and then by the bounded-truncation limits in [F7]. By [F2] and Plancherel these domains are exactly the named $H^s$ with their Fourier norms. The whole-space $L^2$ Hilbert space is nonzero for $n\ge1$, so [F4] applies and gives $[L^2(\mathbb R^n),H^2(\mathbb R^n)]_\theta=H^{2\theta}(\mathbb R^n)$ isometrically in these norms. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F7, given]

2.1 The operator $T$ is defined first on $L^2$ as $Er_D$. Its $L^2$ bound follows from restriction and the assumed $E$ bound. If $F\in H^2(\mathbb R^n)$, [F2] identifies it with a complex weak $W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n)$ class with equivalent norm. Apply [F6] with $\Omega=\mathbb R^n$, $U=D$, $k=2$ and $p=2$: $r_DF\in W^{2,2}(D)$ and $\|r_DF\|_{W^{2,2}(D)}\le\|F\|_{W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n)}\le C\|F\|_{H^2}$. The assumed endpoint bound for the same $E$ and [F2] now give $TF\in W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n)=H^2(\mathbb R^n)$ with $\|TF\|_{H^2}\le C\|F\|_{H^2}$. Thus the two endpoint restrictions are the same complex-linear map. Apply common-operator spectral interpolation with endpoints $0,2$ to obtain $\|TF\|_{H^s}\le M_0^{1-s/2}M_2^{s/2}\|F\|_{H^s}$ for $0<s<2$, retaining the already established bounds at $s=0,2$. No domain interpolation assertion is used in obtaining this whole-space bound. [F1, F2, F4, F6, step 1.1, given]

2.2 For $s\ge0$ the weighted norm controls $L^2$, so $r_D:H^s(\mathbb R^n)\to L^2(D)$ is continuous. Its kernel is closed, and its quotient is Banach with the displayed infimum norm by the quotient theorem. All restriction classes are $L^2$ classes, so this also defines their restrictions as distributions and makes the norm definite: $\|u\|_{L^2(D)}\le\|u\|_{H^s(D)}$. [F2, F5, step 1.1]

3.1 If $u=r_DF$ for $F\in H^s$, then $Eu=Er_DF=TF$ as an $L^2$ class, and the whole-space bound makes it an $H^s$ class. If $F$ is replaced by another extension, their restrictions agree in $L^2(D)$, so the value of the already defined $E$ is unchanged. Hence $\|Eu\|_{H^s}\le C_s\|F\|_{H^s}$ for every extension $F$. Infimizing gives $\|Eu\|_{H^s}\le C_s\|u\|_{H^s(D)}$, while $r_DEu=u$ follows from its $L^2$ identity. This proves the quotient estimate without choosing a fractional extension operator or assuming a fractional-domain identification. [F2, F4, step 2.1, step 2.2, algebra] ∎


### Source notes

The whole-space norm uses the repository bracket weight, not an unannounced replacement by the Laplacian weight with 4π². Positive Fourier order gives actual L² representatives, which is what makes the common extension independent of an ambient extension representative. No exterior fractional theorem is imported.


## Localized smooth coordinate changes and cutoffs on Fourier $H^r$

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators) · level 6


### Statement

Assume AC, let $d\ge1$, and use complex scalars for all function spaces. Let $\Phi:U\to V$ be a smooth diffeomorphism between open subsets of $\mathbb R^d$ and $\chi\in C_c^\infty(U)$. Define one operator on whole-space $L^2$ classes by $Kf=\chi(f\circ\Phi)$ on $U$ and zero outside $U$. Then $K:H^r(\mathbb R^d)\to H^r(\mathbb R^d)$ is bounded for $0\le r\le2$, with bounds depending on the fixed compact chart and cutoff. The same holds in the inverse chart direction and for fixed smooth multipliers with bounded derivatives through order two. These are the bracket-weighted Fourier spaces, and compactly localized coordinate representatives are extended by zero only outside the chart edges.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [$C^k$ boundary flattening preserves local $W^{k,p}$](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-c-k-boundary-flattening-preserves-wkp-locally) gives the weak chain rule and composition bound through order two on fixed compact patches, with $k=2$ and $p=2$. [math:lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor) controls cutoff products, and [math:lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp) extends the compact ambient-chart product.

[F3] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) uses $\|\langle\xi\rangle^r\widehat f\|_2$ and the real-order completion of Schwartz space. [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies its order-two endpoint with weak derivatives up to norm equivalence. [lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract) supplies exact common-operator interpolation for the positive Fourier Hilbert scale.

[F4] [math:ex-unbounded-multiplication-operator-and-its-domain](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-unbounded-multiplication-operator-and-its-domain) gives the self-adjoint multiplication operator $M_{1+|\xi|^2}$, its PVM and exact multiplier calculus on complex Euclidean $L^2$. [math:thm-plancherel](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-plancherel) gives the onto complex-linear Fourier isometry $\mathcal F_2$.

[F5] Smooth Euclidean maps and diffeomorphisms have the meaning of [math:def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms). Complex $L^2$ classes, smooth complex test functions and componentwise derivatives use [Complex $L^p$ classes and Euclidean test-function conventions](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-complex-lp-and-euclidean-test-function-conventions). The open-set convention $C_c^\infty(U;\mathbb C)$ and its support compactly contained in $U$, equivalently smooth compactly supported zero extension to $\mathbb R^d$, are supplied by [math:def-test-function-space-d-of-an-open-set](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-test-function-space-d-of-an-open-set).

[F6] Under the countable-choice consequence of AC, [A $C^1$ diffeomorphism satisfies the change-of-variables formula for $L^1$ functions](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-c-one-change-of-variables-for-l-one-functions) gives substitution for complex $L^1$ functions under a $C^1$ diffeomorphism of open Euclidean sets. [math:lem-c-one-diffeomorphisms-map-lebesgue-null-sets-to-null-sets](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-c-one-diffeomorphisms-map-lebesgue-null-sets-to-null-sets) sends null sets to null sets; apply it to $\Phi^{-1}$ to obtain representative independence of pullback by $\Phi$.

[F7] Under AC and Countable Choice, [physics:lem-nrqm-oscillator-unitary-transport-of-spectral-pvms](https://alphabetamath.cc/physics/item/lem-nrqm-oscillator-unitary-transport-of-spectral-pvms) transports a self-adjoint operator and its regular spectral PVM along a bijective complex-linear isometry. Its actual domain is mathematics. [math:thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-unbounded-borel-functional-calculus) supplies the square-integrability domains, norm identities and spectral truncation limits for the transported real powers. No physical premise is used.

[F8] [math:lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces) makes restriction $W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^d;\mathbb C)\to W^{2,2}(V_0;\mathbb C)$ a contraction for every open $V_0$. Its AC, positive-dimension and complex-scalar hypotheses hold here.

[F9] Under Countable Choice, [math:thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms) identifies the distributional Fourier transform of an $L^2$ class with its Plancherel transform in the same negative-sign $2\pi$ normalization. Combine this with the weighted Fourier characterization in [F3] at nonnegative orders.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement with the stated spaces and bounds.

1.1 If $U=\varnothing$, then $V=\varnothing$ because $\Phi$ is a bijection. The chart operator and its inverse-chart analogue are then identically zero on every $H^r$, with operator norm zero. For the remaining chart argument assume $U\ne\varnothing$. By [F5], $\operatorname{supp}\chi$ is a compact subset of $U$. Choose fixed nonempty open patches $U_0\Subset U$ and $V_0\Subset V$ with $\operatorname{supp}\chi\subset U_0$ and $\Phi(\overline{U_0})\subset V_0$. Put $\Psi=\Phi^{-1}$. On these compact patches the derivatives of $\Phi$ and $\Psi$ through order two are bounded, and the absolute Jacobians are bounded above and away from zero. For $f\in L^2(\mathbb R^d;\mathbb C)$, the function $g(y)=|\chi(\Psi(y))|^2|f(y)|^2|\det D\Psi(y)|$ on $\Phi(U_0)$ belongs to $L^1$ by the compact Jacobian bound. Apply substitution to $g$ under $\Phi|_{U_0}$ and use $|\det D\Psi(\Phi(x))||\det D\Phi(x)|=1$ to obtain $\|Kf\|_2^2=\int_{\Phi(U_0)}|\chi(\Psi(y))|^2|f(y)|^2|\det D\Psi(y)|\,dy\le C_0\|\chi\|_\infty^2\|f\|_2^2$, where $C_0=\sup_{\Phi(\overline{U_0})}|\det D\Psi|<\infty$. If two input representatives differ on a null set $N$, their pullbacks differ only on $\Psi(N\cap V)$, which is null by the null-set supplier applied to $\Psi$. Thus the formula defines one complex-linear bounded operator on $L^2$ classes. [F1, F5, F6, given]

2.1 For $f\in H^2(\mathbb R^d)$, the integer Fourier characterization identifies $f$ with its $W^{2,2}$ class with equivalent norms. Apply [F8] with ambient open set $\mathbb R^d$, restriction set $V_0$, $k=p=2$ and complex scalars: $\|f|_{V_0}\|_{W^{2,2}(V_0)}\le\|f\|_{W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^d)}$. The weak chain rule bounds $f\circ\Phi$ in $W^{2,2}(U_0)$: its first derivatives use first input derivatives, and its second derivatives use first and second input derivatives with bounded chart coefficients. Leibniz bounds $\chi(f\circ\Phi)$, whose support is compact in $U_0$. Compact-support zero extension across only the ambient chart edges gives $\|Kf\|_{W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^d)}\le C\|f\|_{W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^d)}$ for the same operator as in step 1.1. [F2, F3, F8, step 1.1]

3.1 The integer Fourier characterization turns the preceding bound into a bound on $H^2(\mathbb R^d)$ for the same $L^2$ operator. On the nonzero frequency Hilbert space $L^2(\mathbb R^d_\xi;\mathbb C)$ let $M=M_{1+|\xi|^2}$. The multiplier is real and at least one, and Lebesgue measure is sigma-finite. Apply [F7] to $U=\mathcal F_2^{-1}$ and $M$: the operator $B=\mathcal F_2^{-1}M\mathcal F_2$ on $D(B)=\mathcal F_2^{-1}D(M)$ is self-adjoint, with PVM $E_B(C)=\mathcal F_2^{-1}E_M(C)\mathcal F_2$. The scalar spectral measures are consequently transported by $\mathcal F_2$, so the multiplier calculus and square-integrability formula give $B\ge I$, $D(B^{r/2})=\{f\in L^2:\langle\xi\rangle^r\mathcal F_2f\in L^2\}$ and $\|B^{r/2}f\|_2=\|\langle\xi\rangle^r\mathcal F_2f\|_2$ for $0\le r\le2$. The powers follow first for bounded truncated multipliers and then by their exact domain-norm limits. By [F9] and [F3], these domains are the bracket-weighted $H^r$ spaces with exactly their Fourier norms. The positive scale contract identifies its completed positive levels with these domains. Apply its common-operator interpolation to $K$ between levels zero and two to obtain the stated bounds for $0<r<2$; retain the endpoint bounds already proved. The inverse chart satisfies the same hypotheses on fixed compact inverse patches, so the identical argument applies there. [F1, F3, F4, F7, F9, step 1.1, step 2.1]

4.1 For a smooth multiplier with bounded derivatives through order two, the $L^2$ bound and global weak Leibniz bound give the same endpoint interpolation argument directly, without compact support. In all cases the extension to a positive-order space is the original $L^2$ formula restricted to that space, so local coordinate changes and cutoffs remain consistent under restriction and composition. This proves the claimed common-model localization. [F2, F3, F4, step 3.1] ∎


## Restriction-quotient interpolation from a common endpoint retraction

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction) · level 7


### Statement

Assume AC and let $D,E$ satisfy [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation). Then $W^{2,2}(D)=H^2(D)$, the restriction-quotient space, with equivalent norms, and for $0<s<2$,
$$[L^2(D),H^2(D)]_{s/2}=H^s(D)$$
canonically with equivalent norms. Restriction $r_D$ and this one $E$ are bounded endpoint and interpolated maps and satisfy $r_DE=I$ throughout. The same assertion holds if the second domain endpoint is equipped with its equivalent weak $W^{2,2}$ norm. Both conventions include $s=0,2$ by the specified endpoints, without adding boundary conditions.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation) supplies the common endpoint extension hypotheses, the bounded restriction-quotient extension, its independence of representatives and quotient completeness.

[F3] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined) defines the decaying compatible-couple strip class, proves its Banach norm and the action of a common bounded endpoint operator. Only its already-authored strip/retraction interface is used; no new external source is imported here.

[F4] [math:thm-sobolev-spaces-are-banach-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sobolev-spaces-are-banach-spaces) supplies the complex weak endpoint Banach spaces on $D$, and [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies the whole-space $H^2$ endpoint with weak derivatives, retaining equivalence of norms.

[F5] [lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract) gives isometric complex interpolation of the scales of a positive self-adjoint operator on a nonzero complex Hilbert space, under AC and with the same decaying strip convention as [F3].

[F6] [math:ex-unbounded-multiplication-operator-and-its-domain](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-unbounded-multiplication-operator-and-its-domain) gives self-adjointness and the exact multiplier calculus for a real measurable multiplier on a sigma-finite measure space. [math:thm-plancherel](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-plancherel) gives the surjective complex-linear Fourier isometry on complex Euclidean $L^2$ under Countable Choice.

[F7] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies the real-order Bessel-potential space with weighted Fourier distributions and its exact bracket norm.
