[math:thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms) identifies the distributional Fourier transform of an $L^2$ class with its Plancherel transform, in the same negative-sign $2\pi$ normalization. Their Countable Choice hypotheses follow from AC.

[F8] [math:lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces) gives contractive weak Sobolev restriction under AC. Apply it with $\Omega=\mathbb R^n$, $U=D$, $k=2$, $p=2$ and $\mathbb K=\mathbb C$: $r_D:W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)\to W^{2,2}(D;\mathbb C)$ is a contraction, including $D=\varnothing$. The open-set and scalar hypotheses are those of [F2], and AC is [F1].

### Proof

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement under the stated hypotheses.

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

1.1 For a whole-space $H^2$ extension $F$ of $u$, [F4] identifies $F$ with its complex weak $W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n)$ class. The contraction in [F8] then gives $\|u\|_{W^{2,2}(D)}\le\|F\|_{W^{2,2}(\mathbb R^n)}\le C\|F\|_{H^2}$. Conversely the assumed endpoint extension sends every weak $u\in W^{2,2}(D)$ to $Eu\in H^2(\mathbb R^n)$ with norm at most $C\|u\|_{W^{2,2}(D)}$. Taking infima proves $W^{2,2}(D)=H^2(D)$ and the norm equivalence. The order-zero quotient is exactly $L^2(D)$ by zero extension. Both domain endpoints inject continuously into $L^2(D)$, so form a compatible Banach couple. [F1, F2, F4, F8, given]

1.2 On the nonzero complex Hilbert space $K=L^2(\mathbb R^n_\xi)$, let $M=M_{1+|\xi|^2}$. Lebesgue measure is sigma-finite and this real multiplier is finite and at least one, so [F6] gives a self-adjoint $M\ge I$ with $D(M^{t/2})=\{g\in K:\langle\xi\rangle^t g\in K\}$ and norm $\|\langle\xi\rangle^t g\|_2$ for $t\ge0$. The Fourier identifications in [F6] and [F7] make $\mathcal F_2$ an onto isometry from $H^t(\mathbb R^n)$ to this domain for $0\le t\le2$, using actual $L^2$ representatives at these nonnegative orders. At $t=0,2$ these Fourier maps and their inverse are single consistent maps on the endpoint sums. Thus [F3] transports them to inverse contractions on the interpolation spaces. Apply [F5] to $M$ with scale indices $0,2$: its interpolation space is $D(M^\theta)$ with exactly the weighted norm of order $2\theta$. Transporting back by the same Fourier maps yields $[L^2(\mathbb R^n),H^2(\mathbb R^n)]_\theta=H^{2\theta}(\mathbb R^n)$ isometrically in the bracket norms for $0<\theta<1$. This is an application of the spectral supplier's stated interpolation contract, rather than an extra conclusion attributed to [F2]. [F1, F3, F5, F6, F7, given]

2.1 Put $X_\theta=[L^2(D),H^2(D)]_\theta$ and $Y_\theta=[L^2(\mathbb R^n),H^2(\mathbb R^n)]_\theta=H^{2\theta}(\mathbb R^n)$. The whole-space identity is supplied by step 1.2. At the two endpoints $r_D$ and $E$ are bounded common maps and $r_DE=I$. Applying them to a strip function preserves bounded sum-continuity, norm-holomorphy, endpoint continuity and decay by boundedness and linearity; hence the strip-map theorem gives bounded $r_D:Y_\theta\to X_\theta$ and $E:X_\theta\to Y_\theta$ with the same identity. In particular $X_\theta=r_DY_\theta$ as sets. This conclusion uses actual common maps, not unrelated endpoint extensions. [F2, F3, step 1.1, step 1.2]

3.1 If $u=r_DF$ with $F\in Y_\theta$, the interpolated restriction bound gives $\|u\|_{X_\theta}\le C\|F\|_{H^{2\theta}}$, so $\|u\|_{X_\theta}\le C\|u\|_{H^{2\theta}(D)}$ after infimizing. In the other direction, $Eu$ is an extension with $\|Eu\|_{H^{2\theta}}\le C\|u\|_{X_\theta}$; therefore $\|u\|_{H^{2\theta}(D)}\le C\|u\|_{X_\theta}$. This proves equality with equivalent norms and the retraction at every intermediate order. Changing the second endpoint to its equivalent weak norm changes the strip norm only by bounded identity maps in both directions, so gives the same space with equivalent norms. At $s=0,2$ these are the specified endpoint statements. No zero trace is present in either endpoint or quotient construction. [F2, F3, F4, step 2.1, algebra] ∎


## Atlas-independent hypersurface Sobolev norms and $H^{3/2}$ tangential derivatives

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-hypersurface-atlas-and-derivatives) · level 7


### Statement

Assume AC. Let $S\subset\mathbb R^n$, $n\ge2$, be a compact smooth embedded hypersurface without boundary. Equip $S$ with the smooth structure induced by restricting ambient slice charts. Choose a finite smooth atlas $\kappa_i:U_i\subset S\to V_i\subset\mathbb R^{n-1}$ compatible with this induced structure and a subordinate smooth partition $\chi_i$ with supports compactly inside $U_i$. For $f\in L^2(S)$ let $f_i$ be $(\chi_i f)\circ\kappa_i^{-1}$ extended by zero outside $V_i$, and set
$$\|f\|_{H^r(S),\mathcal A}^2=\sum_i\|f_i\|_{H^r(\mathbb R^{n-1})}^2\quad(0\le r\le2).$$
The space where this norm is finite is Banach, and different finite smooth atlases compatible with the induced structure and their subordinate partitions give the same space with equivalent norms. At $r=3/2$ the square of this norm is equivalent to
$$\sum_i\left(\|f_i\|_{H^{1/2}}^2+\sum_{a=1}^{n-1}\|\partial_a f_i\|_{H^{1/2}}^2\right).$$
Thus $H^{3/2}(S)$ is described by the lower-order norm and first weak tangential derivatives of localized chart representatives in $H^{1/2}$. This description is invariant under changes of charts and uses no global tangent frame.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [math:lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration) supplies finite smooth partitions on the compact hypersurface. [math:def-surface-integral-on-a-compact-c-one-hypersurface](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-surface-integral-on-a-compact-c-one-hypersurface) defines the chart integral, and [math:lem-surface-integral-is-independent-of-c-one-boundary-charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-surface-integral-is-independent-of-c-one-boundary-charts) proves that it is a finite Borel surface measure independent of the charts and partition, with continuous strictly positive regular chart densities. Its countable-choice hypothesis follows from AC. On each compact smooth patch the density is bounded above and away from zero, and all fixed transition derivatives are bounded.

[F3] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators) bounds compactly supported overlap pullbacks and smooth cutoff multipliers on every Fourier $H^r$, $0\le r\le2$.

[F4] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies the complete weighted Fourier spaces and their dense Schwartz core. [math:lem-weak-derivatives-are-polynomial-fourier-multipliers](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-derivatives-are-polynomial-fourier-multipliers) gives $\widehat{\partial_a g}=2\pi i\xi_a\widehat g$ distributionally.

[F5] [math:def-codimension-and-hypersurface](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-codimension-and-hypersurface) specifies the embedded dimension $n-1$ of $S$. [math:lem-slice-chart-restrictions-form-a-smooth-atlas](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-slice-chart-restrictions-form-a-smooth-atlas) supplies the induced atlas with the subspace topology, and compatibility of atlases is as in [math:def-smooth-atlas](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-smooth-atlas). [math:prop-the-inclusion-of-an-embedded-submanifold-is-a-smooth-embedding](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/prop-the-inclusion-of-an-embedded-submanifold-is-a-smooth-embedding) makes the inclusion a smooth immersion in this structure. Consequently the inverse coordinate maps of compatible charts are regular smooth parametrizations into $\mathbb R^n$; their coordinate derivatives are velocities of curves in $S$, hence tangent to $S$. Transitions between any two chosen compatible atlases are smooth diffeomorphisms.

[F6] [math:thm-complex-lp-completeness-and-almost-everywhere-subsequences](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-complex-lp-completeness-and-almost-everywhere-subsequences) gives completeness of complex $L^2$ on every measure space under Countable Choice. Apply it to the finite Borel surface measure on $S$ supplied by [F2]; AC supplies Countable Choice. Thus the surface space $L^2(S;\mathbb C)$ is complete, independently of the completeness of the coordinate Fourier spaces.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement with the stated spaces and bounds.

1.1 The coordinate functions $f_i$ are well-defined on almost-everywhere classes because the compatible charts are regular parametrizations, smooth coordinate changes preserve null sets, and the chart-independent surface measure has positive chart densities. At order zero their squared $L^2$ norms sum to a quantity comparable to $\|f\|_{L^2(S)}^2$: the upper bound follows from the finite bounded densities and cutoffs, while $f=\sum_i\chi_i f$ gives the lower bound by the finite-sum inequality and change of variables. Thus the stated norm is definite for every $r\ge0$ and controls $L^2(S)$. [F2, F4, F5, given]

2.1 For a second compatible atlas and subordinate partition $(\widetilde\chi_j,\widetilde\kappa_j)$, write each localized new representative as the finite sum of the representations of $\widetilde\chi_j\chi_i f$. Each summand is obtained from $f_i$ by an overlap transition and a smooth cutoff supported inside that overlap. Such overlap cutoffs exist after a finite refinement of the compact support intersections; extend them smoothly with compact margins inside the relevant chart. The localized-chart lemma bounds each summand in $H^r$. The finite triangle inequality gives $\|f\|_{\widetilde{\mathcal A},r}\le C\|f\|_{\mathcal A,r}$, and reversing the atlases gives the other inequality. If $f_j$ is Cauchy in the atlas norm, the order-zero comparison in step 1.1 makes it Cauchy in $L^2(S;\mathbb C)$, and [F6] supplies a limit $f$ there; each localized representative converges also in complete $H^r$, whose continuous injection into $L^2$ identifies its limit with the localized representative of $f$. Hence $f$ belongs to the space and the atlas norm convergence holds, proving completeness. [F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, step 1.1]

2.2 For a whole-space $g\in H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^{n-1})$, the Fourier derivative identity shows that $g$ and all $\partial_a g$ are in $H^{1/2}$ exactly when $g\in H^{3/2}$. Indeed the sum of their squared norms is $\int\langle\xi\rangle(1+4\pi^2|\xi|^2)|\widehat g|^2$, whose weight is bounded above and below by positive constants times $\langle\xi\rangle^3$. Apply this to every $f_i$ and sum, obtaining the displayed characterization in both directions, including distributional derivative membership rather than presuming it. [F4, step 1.1, algebra]

3.1 In any compatible chart the coordinate vector fields are tangent to $S$, so these first derivatives are local tangential weak derivatives. On an overlap the chain rule gives a smooth transition Jacobian multiplying the coordinate derivatives; partition Leibniz terms add smooth coefficients multiplying the undifferentiated representative. To justify this identity for the present functions, approximate each localized representative by Schwartz functions in $H^{3/2}$, localize with a fixed larger chart cutoff, and pass to the limit using bounded overlap pullbacks on $H^{3/2}$ and bounded coefficient multipliers on $H^{1/2}$. Differentiation $H^{3/2}\to H^{1/2}$ is continuous by the preceding Fourier estimate. The limiting distributional identity follows, so the local derivative description is intrinsic without a global frame. [F2, F3, F4, F5, step 2.1, step 2.2] ∎


## Fourier trace on a hyperplane and one-sided H¹ uniqueness

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-flat-fourier-trace) · level 7


### Statement

Assume AC and $n\ge2$, and write $H_\pm=\{(x^{\prime},t):\pm t>0\}$. For every $1\le s\le2$ there is a bounded linear trace $\gamma:H^s(\mathbb R^n)\to H^{s-1/2}(\mathbb R^{n-1})$ agreeing with $F(x',0)$ for Schwartz $F$. All these maps are restrictions of the same $H^1$ trace, as $L^2$-valued maps. Moreover
$$\|\gamma F\|_{L^2}^2\le2\|F\|_{L^2(H_+)}\|\partial_nF\|_{L^2(H_+)}$$
for $F\in H^1(\mathbb R^n)$, and likewise from the negative half-space. Consequently an $H^s$ function zero on one half-space has zero trace.

For the local assertion let $U\subset\mathbb R^n$ be open. Here $f\in H^s_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)$ means $f\in L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)$ and, for every $\chi\in C_c^\infty(U)$, the function $G_\chi$ equal to $\chi f$ on $U$ and zero outside $U$ belongs to $H^s(\mathbb R^n)$. Let $P\subset\mathbb R^{n-1}$ be open with $\overline P\times\{0\}$ a compact subset of $U$. For any $\chi\in C_c^\infty(U)$ equal to one near $\overline P\times\{0\}$, define $\gamma_P f=(\gamma G_\chi)|_P$. This is an $L^2(P)$ class independent of $\chi$, compatible under restriction to smaller such patches, and equal to $(\gamma f)|_P$ when $f$ is a whole-space $H^s$ function. If $f=0$ almost everywhere on $U\cap H_+$, or on $U\cap H_-$, then $\gamma_Pf=0$. Thus the assertion applies to local data on a neighborhood of a flat boundary patch, with no choice of a whole-space extension of those data.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); its countable instance supplies the hypotheses of the Fourier and completion interfaces and the choices of Schwartz approximation sequences used below.

[F2] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies real-order $H^s$ with the weighted Fourier distribution space, with exact norm and dense Schwartz core; its completion definition supplies this density for all orders here, without a dimension restriction.

[F3] [math:thm-fourier-inversion-on-schwartz-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-inversion-on-schwartz-space) gives absolutely convergent inversion. [math:thm-plancherel](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-plancherel) gives the $L^2$ norm identity; [math:thm-tonelli-and-fubini-for-completed-product-measures](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-tonelli-and-fubini-for-completed-product-measures) and [math:thm-holder-inequality-for-integrals](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-holder-inequality-for-integrals) justify the Schwartz integrations and weighted Cauchy–Schwarz estimate.

[F4] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators) bounds fixed smooth cutoff multipliers on $H^r(\mathbb R^d)$ for $0\le r\le2$, in both the ambient and tangential dimensions. [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies $H^1$ with its weak derivative space, with equivalent norms.

[F5] [math:lem-fundamental-theorem-and-integration-by-parts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-fundamental-theorem-and-integration-by-parts) gives $\int_a^b h'(t)\,dt=h(b)-h(a)$ for real $C^1$ functions on an open interval containing the finite endpoints. Apply this to the real smooth function $h(t)=|F(x^{\prime},t)|^2$ before taking an infinite-endpoint limit.

[F6] [math:lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization) supplies a compactly supported smooth cutoff equal to one near any compact subset of an open Euclidean set. This applies to $\overline P\times\{0\}$ in $U$ and in any smaller open neighborhood of that compact set.

[F7] [math:def-schwartz-space-and-its-seminorms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-schwartz-space-and-its-seminorms) defines actual smooth Schwartz functions by the bounds $\sup_x|x^\alpha\partial^\beta F(x)|<\infty$ for every pair of multi-indices. [math:thm-fourier-transform-maps-schwartz-space-continuously-to-itself](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-transform-maps-schwartz-space-continuously-to-itself) preserves this class for the negative-sign $2\pi$ transform under countable choice, supplied by the assumed AC.

[F8] [math:lem-schwartz-functions-and-all-derivatives-are-integrable](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-schwartz-functions-and-all-derivatives-are-integrable) gives $L^p$ integrability of every weighted Schwartz derivative for $1\le p\le\infty$ under countable choice. [math:thm-dominated-convergence](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-dominated-convergence) passes limits through integrals of measurable complex functions dominated by one integrable majorant; below the majorants in the normal variable are constant multiples of $(1+\tau^2)^{-1}$.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement with the stated spaces and bounds.

1.1 Let $F\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R^n)$ and put $h(x^{\prime})=F(x^{\prime},0)$. Its tangential Schwartz seminorms are bounded by those of $F$, so $h\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R^{n-1})$. By F7, $\widehat F\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R^n)$. Define $g(\xi^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb R}\widehat F(\xi^{\prime},\tau)\,d\tau$. For every tangential multi-index pair $\alpha,\beta$, the Schwartz seminorms with normal powers zero and two give $|(\xi^{\prime})^\alpha\partial_{\xi^{\prime}}^\beta\widehat F(\xi^{\prime},\tau)|\le C_{\alpha\beta}(1+\tau^2)^{-1}$. The finite-interval fundamental theorem bounds coordinate difference quotients by the same kind of majorant for one higher derivative. Dominated convergence, applied along arbitrary sequences of increments tending to zero, therefore permits differentiation under the normal integral and proves continuity of every derivative. Integrating the displayed bound shows that every Schwartz seminorm of $g$ is finite, so $g\in\mathcal S(\mathbb R^{n-1})$. Inversion for $F$ at $t=0$ and Fubini, justified by $\widehat F\in L^1$, give $h(x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb R^{n-1}}g(\xi^{\prime})e^{2\pi i x^{\prime}\cdot\xi^{\prime}}\,d\xi^{\prime}$. Thus $\widehat g(y)=h(-y)$; inversion for $g$ and the substitution $y\mapsto-y$ yield $g=\widehat h$. This establishes $\widehat{F(\cdot,0)}(\xi^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb R}\widehat F(\xi^{\prime},\tau)\,d\tau$ without interchanging an undamped double Fourier transform. [F1, F3, F5, F7, F8, given]

1.2 For Schwartz $F$ and fixed $x^{\prime}$, apply the finite-interval fundamental theorem to $h(t)=|F(x^{\prime},t)|^2$ on $[0,R]$: $|F(x^{\prime},0)|^2=|F(x^{\prime},R)|^2-2\operatorname{Re}\int_0^R(\partial_nF)\overline F\,dt$. Schwartz decay sends the boundary term to zero as $R\to\infty$ and makes the normal integral absolutely convergent. The product $(\partial_nF)\overline F$ is absolutely integrable on $H_+$ by the $p=q=2$ case of Holder applied to its absolute values. Fubini therefore gives $\|F(\cdot,0)\|_2^2=-2\operatorname{Re}\int_{H_+}(\partial_nF)\overline F\le2\|F\|_{L^2(H_+)}\|\partial_nF\|_{L^2(H_+)}$. Integrating instead from $-R$ to zero and taking $R\to\infty$ gives the negative-side estimate by the same absolute-integrability argument. [F3, F5, F7, F8, given]

2.1 Using the restriction identity of step 1.1, set $a=1+|\xi^{\prime}|^2$. Weighted Cauchy–Schwarz bounds the squared modulus of this integral by $\int(a+\tau^2)^s|\widehat F|^2d\tau$ times $\int(a+\tau^2)^{-s}d\tau=C_s a^{1/2-s}$, where $C_s=\int(1+t^2)^{-s}dt<\infty$ since $s>1/2$. Multiplying by $a^{s-1/2}$ and integrating in $\xi^{\prime}$ gives $\|F(\cdot,0)\|_{H^{s-1/2}}^2\le C_s\|F\|_{H^s}^2$. This uses the bracket norm and the negative-sign $2\pi$ transform, so no hidden normalization factor enters the trace integral. [F2, F3, step 1.1, given, algebra]

3.1 Approximate $F\in H^s$ by Schwartz functions in the completion norm. The bound makes their boundary restrictions Cauchy in complete $H^{s-1/2}$, with a limit independent of the approximating sequence. This defines the claimed bounded linear trace. For $s\ge1$ the same sequence converges also in $H^1$, and the target embeds into $L^2$ by its Fourier weight. The $s$-trace and the order-one trace therefore have the same $L^2$ limit, proving their compatibility. [F2, F3, step 2.1]

4.1 For $F\in H^1$, select Schwartz approximants in $H^1$. Their restrictions and normal derivatives converge in $L^2$ on either half-space by the weak-derivative norm equivalence; their traces converge in $L^2$ by step 3.1. Passing to the limits in the two inequalities proves the displayed bound on both sides for every $H^1$ input. If $F$ vanishes on one half-space, the corresponding factor $\|F\|_{L^2(H_\pm)}$ is zero, so its trace is zero. Compatibility from step 3.1 proves the same conclusion for $H^s$ inputs. [F1, F2, F4, step 3.1, step 1.2]

4.2 For $F\in H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ and $\eta\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$, put $\eta_0(x^{\prime})=\eta(x^{\prime},0)$. On Schwartz functions the identity $\gamma(\eta F)=\eta_0\gamma F$ is the classical restriction identity. Multiplication by $\eta$ is bounded on $H^s$ and by $\eta_0$ on $H^{s-1/2}$, since both exponents lie in $[0,2]$. Thus Schwartz approximation and the trace bound pass this identity to all $F\in H^s$, in particular in $L^2$. Consequently, if $F=0$ on an open neighborhood $V$ of $\overline P\times\{0\}$, choose $\eta\in C_c^\infty(V)$ equal to one near that compact set. Then $\eta F=0$ and the identity shows $(\gamma F)|_P=0$. This also shows that two whole-space inputs agreeing near the patch have the same trace on it. [F1, F2, F4, F6, step 3.1]

5.1 For local data $f$ in the Statement, F6 gives an admissible $\chi$, and the local hypothesis gives $G_\chi\in H^s$, so $(\gamma G_\chi)|_P$ is an $L^2(P)$ class by step 3.1. If $\chi_1,\chi_2$ are two such cutoffs, then $G_{\chi_1}-G_{\chi_2}\in H^s$ and is zero on an open neighborhood of $\overline P\times\{0\}$, because both cutoffs equal one there. Step 4.2 makes their traces equal on $P$, proving independence. If a smaller patch is used, the cutoff for the larger patch is also admissible for the smaller one, which proves compatibility. A whole-space $f\in H^s$ satisfies the local hypothesis by F4; the difference $G_\chi-f$ vanishes near the patch, so step 4.2 proves agreement with its global trace. [F4, F6, step 3.1, step 4.2]

6.1 If the local datum vanishes almost everywhere on $U\cap H_+$, then every $G_\chi$ is zero on all of $H_+$, since it also vanishes outside $U$. Step 4.1 gives $\gamma G_\chi=0$, and hence $\gamma_Pf=0$. The negative side is identical using the negative-side bound in that step. The same argument applies after shrinking $U$ to any one-sided vanishing neighborhood of the patch. This proves the stated local uniqueness without weakening the global trace bound or the range of exponents. [given, step 4.1, step 5.1] ∎


## A common exterior extension and explicit collar–tail retraction

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-exterior-collar-tail-retraction) · level 8


### Statement

Assume AC. Let $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$ have smooth compact boundary and compact complement. There is one $E_\Omega:L^2(\Omega)\to L^2(\mathbb R^n)$, bounded also on the weak $W^{2,2}$ endpoint, with $r_\Omega E_\Omega=I$. It is given by a finite smooth collar construction (boundary reflections and a compactly supported interior identity localization) plus an identity free tail. Consequently the restriction-quotient $H^s(\Omega)$ satisfies $[L^2(\Omega),H^2(\Omega)]_{s/2}=H^s(\Omega)$ with equivalent norms for $0\le s\le2$.

More explicitly choose a large $L$ with $\Theta\Subset B_{L/4}$, $\eta\in C_c^\infty(B_{L/2})$ equal to $1$ near $\Theta$, $a\in C_c^\infty(B_L)$ equal to $1$ near $\operatorname{supp}\eta$, and $\beta\in C_c^\infty(\{\eta=1\}^{\circ})$ equal to $1$ near $\Theta$; put $b=1-\beta$ and $G=\Omega\cap B_L$. Let $Z u$ be the zero extension of $(1-\eta)u$ to $\mathbb R^n$. If $V_a v$ denotes $av$ on $G$ and zero on $\Omega\setminus G$, then the same maps
$$J u=((\eta u)|_G,Zu),\qquad P(v,w)=V_a v+(bw)|_\Omega=(aE_Gv+bw)|_\Omega$$
are bounded $H^s(\Omega)\to H^s(G)\oplus H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ and back for every $0\le s\le2$, and $PJ=I$. In particular
$$\|u\|_{H^s(\Omega)}\asymp\bigl(\|\eta u\|_{H^s(G)}^2+\|Zu\|_{H^s(\mathbb R^n)}^2\bigr)^{1/2}.$$
These are ordinary restriction Sobolev spaces; no boundary condition is imposed by these maps. In addition, at the integer endpoint $P$ maps $(H^2(G)\cap H^1_0(G))\oplus H^2(\mathbb R^n)$ into $H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$; this conditional property asserts no fractional Dirichlet-domain identification.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-bounded-common-endpoint-extension](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-bounded-common-endpoint-extension) gives one $E_G$ on the bounded smooth collar $G$, bounded at $L^2$ and weak $W^{2,2}$ endpoints, with restriction identity. We use specifically its Proof steps 1.1–4.1: the nested boundary charts may be chosen arbitrarily small before selecting a finite cover; the partition has an interior term $\rho_0$ compactly supported in $G$ and boundary terms $\rho_i$; the extension is the zero extension of $\rho_0v$ plus finitely many chart/reflection terms $E_iv$, each supported in its chosen larger ambient chart by the cutoff $\psi_i$. The same construction gives both endpoint bounds. The localization of these choices for the present $G$ is specified in step 2.1 below.

[F3] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation) gives fractional quotient bounds for any common endpoint extension and [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction) then identifies its domain interpolation norms.

[F4] [math:lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization) supplies the separating cutoffs. [math:lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor) controls every weak derivative through order two, and [math:lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-compact-support-zero-extension-in-wkp) supplies compact-support zero extension; for the unbounded tail and for extension across only the artificial sphere, the compact-test argument is given below.

[F5] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined) applies the same endpoint maps to admissible strip functions. Finite Hilbert-sum endpoint norms may be used; their interpolated norm is equivalent to the sum of component interpolated norms by coordinate injections, coordinate projections and paired strip functions.

[F6] For any open $D$, write $H^1_0(D)=W^{1,2}_0(D)$ for the closure of $C_c^\infty(D)$ in the weak $W^{1,2}(D)$ norm, as defined by [math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure). The supplier [Compactly supported smooth functions are dense in $W^{k,p}(\mathbb R^n)$](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-compactly-supported-smooth-functions-are-dense-in-wkp-of-rn) gives $C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ approximation in weak $W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)$, using only $k=1$, $p=2$ and complex scalars.

[F7] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies whole-space Fourier $H^1(\mathbb R^n)$ with weak $W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)$ under the regular-distribution embedding, with equivalent norms. [math:lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-bounded-restriction-and-cutoff-localisation-in-sobolev-spaces) gives contractive weak $W^{1,2}$ restriction to every open subset. AC supplies the Countable Choice hypotheses of these inputs. The boundary-closure argument below stays in weak first-order norms and does not assume a first-order quotient-norm comparison on $G$.

### Proof

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement under the stated hypotheses.

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

1.1 The compact obstacle lies strictly inside a large ball, so the stated nested cutoffs exist; choose $\beta$ supported in the open plateau of $\eta$. Thus $a\eta=\eta$ and $b(1-\eta)=1-\eta$. For a weak $W^{2,2}(\Omega)$ function, $Zu$ and the zero extensions of $D^\alpha((1-\eta)u)$, $|\alpha|\le2$, have $L^2$ norms bounded by $C\|u\|_{W^{2,2}}$. They are its actual global weak derivatives: every test $\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ is localized away from $\Theta$ by the factor $1-\eta$ and its derivatives, so the weak product identity on $\Omega$ gives the global identity after integration. Equivalently partition its compact support into the open region where the tail is zero and the region inside $\Omega$, on which the original local identities hold. This argument is valid even though the tail itself is unbounded. Hence $Z$ is one common bounded $L^2/W^{2,2}$ map. [F4, given]

2.1 Fix the extension in F2 using the following chart choices, all independent of the input. The boundary of $G$ is the disjoint union of the obstacle boundary and $\partial B_L$. Since $a=\eta=1$ on a neighborhood of the compact obstacle, choose all larger doubled obstacle charts compactly inside that neighborhood and inside $B_L$. Since $\operatorname{supp}a\Subset B_L$ and $\operatorname{supp}\eta\Subset B_{L/2}$, choose all larger doubled artificial-sphere charts in the open complement of $\operatorname{supp}a\cup\overline B_{L/2}\cup\Theta$. At every boundary point these are open neighborhoods, so the nested chart construction of F2 fits inside them; compactness then gives finitely many inner charts covering each boundary component. Complete this cover by the interior set and choose $\rho_0,\rho_i,\psi_i$ exactly as in that construction, retaining its reflection margins. Write $I_\Theta,I_L$ for the two finite sets of boundary-chart indices and $E_0$ for zero extension from $G$. Thus $E_Gv=E_0(\rho_0v)+\sum_{i\in I_\Theta}E_iv+\sum_{i\in I_L}E_iv$. For $i\in I_L$, $\operatorname{supp}E_iv\subseteq\operatorname{supp}\psi_i$ is disjoint from $\operatorname{supp}a$, hence $aE_iv=0$ for every $L^2$ input. For $i\in I_\Theta$, $a=\eta=1$ on the whole larger chart, hence $aE_iv=E_iv$. These are support identities for the actual common endpoint construction, not properties inferred for an arbitrary right inverse. [F1, F2, F4, step 1.1, construct]

2.2 At order zero the displayed $J$ and $P$ are bounded by cutoff multiplication and restriction. At the second weak-derivative endpoint, $J_1$ is bounded by restriction and Leibniz, and $J_2=Z$ is bounded by the first step. To justify $V_a$, work on $\Omega$: near the artificial sphere the function $av$ and its derivatives vanish because $a$ has support strictly inside $B_L$. On $G$ they have the weak Leibniz derivatives. Every compact test in $\Omega$ can be partitioned between these two regions, proving the partial zero-extension identity and its norm bound. This crosses only the artificial sphere, never the obstacle boundary. The term $bw|_\Omega$ is bounded at order two since $b$ has bounded derivatives through order two. Consequently $J$ and $P$ are common endpoint maps between the ordinary domain couple and the finite product of the collar and whole-space couples. [F2, F4, step 1.1]

3.1 Define $E_\Omega u=aE_G((\eta u)|_G)+Zu$ for $u\in L^2(\Omega)$. Every factor is fixed and linear, and the collar lemma and weak Leibniz bounds give both endpoint estimates for this very formula. On $G$ its first term restricts to $a\eta u=\eta u$; on $\Omega\setminus G$ it is zero because $a$ is supported inside $B_L$. Adding $Zu$ therefore gives $r_\Omega E_\Omega u=u$. For $v=(\eta u)|_G$, step 2.1 gives the exact decomposition $aE_Gv=E_0(a\rho_0v)+\sum_{i\in I_\Theta}E_iv$. The first term is the compactly supported interior identity localization: $a\rho_0v$ is supported compactly in $G$, so its zero extension is legitimate at both endpoints. Each remaining term is an obstacle-chart reflection, with no artificial-sphere contribution. Together with $Zu$ these terms establish the claimed finite collar construction and identity free tail; in particular outside $B_L$ the extension is exactly $u$. Apply the whole-space $Er$ and quotient/retraction lemmas to this common extension. They prove the exterior fractional quotient bounds and the asserted interpolation identity without presupposing an exterior fractional-domain theorem. If the complement is empty, take $E_\Omega=I$ and the collar cutoffs zero. [F2, F3, F4, step 1.1, step 2.1, algebra]

3.2 First record the weak first-order localization bounds. For $f\in W^{1,2}(G)$ the weak product rule gives $\partial_i(af)=a\partial_i f+(\partial_i a)f$ on $G$. Since $a$ vanishes near the artificial sphere, the compact-test argument of step 2.2, now through order one, shows that $V_af\in W^{1,2}(\Omega)$ with these derivatives on $G$ and zero derivatives outside $G$. Integrating these expressions gives $\|V_af\|_{W^{1,2}(\Omega)}\le C_a\|f\|_{W^{1,2}(G)}$. For $h\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)$, all derivatives of $b$ through order one are bounded, so the same weak product rule and contractive restriction give $\|(bh)|_\Omega\|_{W^{1,2}(\Omega)}\le C_b\|h\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)}$. These estimates require no extension across the obstacle. [F4, F7, step 2.2]

4.1 For $0<s<2$ apply the strip-map theorem to these same $J,P$. The domain interpolation couples are the quotient $H^s$ spaces by the preceding step and the quotient lemma. For the product target, each coordinate projection and injection is bounded at both endpoints, hence after interpolation. Conversely, given admissible component strip functions with prescribed values, their ordered pair is an admissible product strip function, and its boundary Hilbert norm is at most the sum of the two component boundary norms. Infimizing proves the product identification with equivalent Hilbert-sum norms. These observations give the asserted $J,P$ bounds on every intermediate order, consistent with their original $L^2$ formulas. The endpoint bounds are already established. [F3, F5, step 3.1, step 2.2]

5.1 For any $L^2$ input, $PJ u=a\eta u+b(1-\eta)u=u$ by the cutoff identities. This remains the identity on every $H^s$ restriction class because all maps are the same restrictions of the $L^2$ maps. The bound for $J$ is one of the inequalities in the claimed norm comparison, while $u=PJu$ and the bound for $P$ give the other. The definition imposes no trace at either boundary: the first collar component is an arbitrary ordinary Sobolev function and the auxiliary cutoffs merely localize. This completes the ordinary exterior localization/retraction. [F3, F4, F5, step 1.1, step 4.1, algebra]

6.1 If $v\in H^2(G)\cap H^1_0(G)$, its closure membership gives $v_j\in C_c^\infty(G)$ converging to $v$ in weak $W^{1,2}(G)$. Their $a$-localized partial zero extensions belong to $C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ and converge in weak $W^{1,2}(\Omega)$ to $V_av$ by step 3.2. For $w\in H^2(\mathbb R^n)$, the Fourier-weight inequality $\langle\xi\rangle\le\langle\xi\rangle^2$ gives $w\in H^1(\mathbb R^n)$, and F7 therefore gives $w\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)$. Choose $w_j\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ converging to $w$ in weak $W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)$ by F6. Then $(bw_j)|_\Omega\in C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ because $b$ vanishes on a neighborhood of the obstacle, and step 3.2 gives convergence to $(bw)|_\Omega$ in weak $W^{1,2}(\Omega)$. The sums of these approximants converge to $P(v,w)$ in that norm, proving $P(v,w)\in H^1_0(\Omega)$ by the closure definition. Its $H^2$ membership follows from the already established endpoint bound, proving the stated conditional property. The alternate displayed formula for $P$ holds because $E_Gv=v$ on $G$ and $a$ vanishes outside $B_L$, so it is the same map rather than a second extension. [F2, F3, F4, F6, F7, step 2.2, step 3.2] ∎



## Simultaneous Sobolev extension and localization

[Open item](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction) · level 9


### Statement

Assume AC. For a bounded smooth domain $G$ there is one linear extension $E_G$, agreeing with the input on $G$, bounded $H^s(G)\to H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ for every $0\le s\le2$, with output supported in a fixed compact neighborhood. Here $H^s(G)$ is the restriction of the Bessel-potential space with quotient norm. In this range $[L^2(G),H^2(G)]_{s/2}=H^s(G)$ with equivalent norms. Finite smooth boundary charts, cutoffs and restriction give the same order-0-to-2 Sobolev localization/retraction for a smooth exterior domain (a bounded collar plus a free-space tail).

Here the whole-space $H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ uses the weighted Fourier distribution convention, $H^s(G)$ is its restriction quotient, and a retraction/coretraction pair consists of bounded linear maps $J,P$ with $PJ=I$. Complex interpolation is over complex scalars. For a compatible complex Banach couple $(X_0,X_1)$, give $X_0+X_1$ the sum norm $\|x\|_{X_0+X_1}=\inf_{x=x_0+x_1}(\|x_0\|_{X_0}+\|x_1\|_{X_1})$. On $S=\{z\in\mathbb C:0\le\operatorname{Re}z\le1\}$, the bounded-strip class $\mathcal F_b(X_0,X_1)$ consists of bounded, sum-norm continuous functions $f:S\to X_0+X_1$ that are norm-holomorphic in the interior and whose boundary maps $t\mapsto f(j+it)$ are bounded and continuous in the $X_j$ norm, $j=0,1$. Give this class the boundary norm $\|f\|_{\mathcal F_b}=\max_{j=0,1}\sup_{t\in\mathbb R}\|f(j+it)\|_{X_j}$. For $0<\theta<1$, $[X_0,X_1]_\theta$ consists of the values $f(\theta)$, with norm $\inf_{f(\theta)=u}\|f\|_{\mathcal F_b}$. At $\theta=0,1$, the brackets denote the respective endpoint spaces with their given norms, rather than endpoint evaluation quotients. In this Hilbert setting the claimed Sobolev identifications include equivalence of norms.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-bounded-common-endpoint-extension](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-bounded-common-endpoint-extension) constructs one $E_G$ on $L^2(G)$ and weak $W^{2,2}(G)$ with $r_GE_G=I$ and output in a fixed compact subset of a chosen neighborhood.

[F3] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-whole-space-er-interpolation) interpolates the one whole-space map $E_Gr_G$ and infimizes over ambient extensions; [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction) gives the domain interpolation/quotient identity with equivalent norms, including equivalence of the integer weak endpoint.

[F4] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-exterior-collar-tail-retraction](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-exterior-collar-tail-retraction) constructs one exterior endpoint extension and the explicit compatible $J,P$ maps with $PJ=I$ and two-sided collar/tail norm equivalence through order two.

[F5] [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined) supplies the compatible complex Banach-couple sum and the decaying strip class $\mathcal F_d(X_0,X_1)$: it has exactly the regularity and boundedness conditions of $\mathcal F_b$ in the Statement and additionally $\|f(j+it)\|_{X_j}\to0$ as $|t|\to\infty$, $j=0,1$. Its norm is the same maximum boundary norm. The quotient/interpolation supplier in F3 uses this decaying class. The comparison with the bounded class is proved locally below.

### Proof

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement under the stated hypotheses.

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

1.1 For bounded smooth $G$, take the common compactly supported endpoint extension from the first fact. It is defined already on $L^2(G)$ and is bounded at the second weak-derivative endpoint. The whole-space $Er$ lemma therefore applies with $D=G$: for each $0\le s\le2$ and each ambient $H^s$ extension $F$ of $u$, $E_Gu=(E_Gr_G)F$ and $\|E_Gu\|_{H^s}\le C_s\|F\|_{H^s}$. The value is independent of $F$ because it is the original $L^2$ map applied to $u$. Infimizing gives the bounded quotient extension for every order. Its support remains in the same fixed compact set because the formula on positive-order inputs is unchanged. [F2, F3, given]

1.2 For any compatible complex Banach couple, denote the evaluation norms from $\mathcal F_b$ and $\mathcal F_d$ by $N_b$ and $N_d$. Fix $0<\theta<1$, $f\in\mathcal F_b$ and $\varepsilon>0$, and put $f_\varepsilon(z)=\exp(\varepsilon(z-\theta)^2)f(z)$ and $c_\theta=\max\{\theta^2,(1-\theta)^2\}$. For $z=x+it\in S$, the scalar factor has modulus $\exp(\varepsilon((x-\theta)^2-t^2))\le\exp(\varepsilon c_\theta)\exp(-\varepsilon t^2)$. Multiplication by this entire scalar function preserves sum-norm continuity and norm-holomorphy: the latter follows directly from the product difference quotient. The displayed bound preserves boundedness on $S$ and makes each continuous $X_j$ boundary map decay in its endpoint norm. Hence $f_\varepsilon\in\mathcal F_d$, $f_\varepsilon(\theta)=f(\theta)$, and $\|f_\varepsilon\|_{\mathcal F_d}\le e^{\varepsilon c_\theta}\|f\|_{\mathcal F_b}$. Thus the two classes have identical sets of values at $\theta$. The inclusion $\mathcal F_d\subseteq\mathcal F_b$ gives $N_b(u)\le N_d(u)$, while the damped representatives give $N_d(u)\le e^{\varepsilon c_\theta}N_b(u)$ after infimizing over all $f$ representing $u$. Letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ proves $N_d(u)=N_b(u)$. No convergence of $f_\varepsilon$ in either strip norm is needed. [F5, given, construct, algebra]

2.1 For $0<s<2$, apply the quotient/interpolation lemma with $\theta=s/2$. Its decaying-strip interpolation space is $H^s(G)$ with equivalent norms; step 1.2 identifies that evaluation space isometrically with the bounded-strip convention of the Statement. At $s=0,2$ the brackets are the specified endpoint spaces, and the endpoint identifications are supplied directly by F2 and F3, without an endpoint strip-evaluation argument. Quotient completeness, definiteness and independence of the chosen ambient extension are supplied by the whole-space $Er$ lemma in F3. Consequently the domain interpolation identity and the common extension have precisely the asserted restriction-quotient meaning. [F2, F3, F5, step 1.1, step 1.2]

3.1 For the smooth exterior domain, use the final helper’s bounded collar $G=\Omega\cap B_L$, its finite-reflection collar extension and its independently justified identity tail. That helper proves the common $L^2/W^{2,2}$ endpoint bounds and then applies the same whole-space and quotient arguments; it also gives explicit maps $J,P$ with $PJ=I$ and equivalent collar/tail norms for each $0\le s\le2$. Thus both the bounded compact-output assertion and the exterior localization/retraction in the Statement hold in exactly the restriction-quotient Bessel convention. [F3, F4, step 1.1, step 2.1] ∎

### Source notes

Five local intermediate items supply the second-order reflection, common compact bounded-domain extension, whole-space Er interpolation, quotient/interpolation retraction, and exterior collar/free-tail construction. The exact existing half-space weak matching and authored spectral interpolation interfaces are used; no Rychkov theorem or unread external extension theorem is cited. This is an authoring candidate, not an independent proof or scaffold review.


## Exterior spectral domains and the critical trace index

[Open item](item-lem-se-exterior-fractional-identification) · level 10


### Statement

Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $n\ge2$, let $\Theta\subset\mathbb R^n$ be nonempty and compact with smooth boundary, and let $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$ be connected. Let $A=I-\Delta_D$ and $H_D^s(\Omega)=D(A^{s/2})$ with norm $\|A^{s/2}u\|_2$, as in [def-se-dirichlet-scale](item-def-se-dirichlet-scale). Let $H^s(\Omega)$ be the restriction of the whole-space Bessel-potential $H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ with quotient norm, and let $d(x)=\operatorname{dist}(x,\partial\Omega)$. For $s>1/2$, $\gamma_0u$ denotes the boundary trace obtained by localization to a bounded smooth collar, as in the proof. Then, with equivalent norms, for $0\le s\le2$:

$$H_D^s(\Omega)=\begin{cases}H^s(\Omega),&0\le s<1/2,\\ \{u\in H^{1/2}(\Omega):d^{-1/2}u\in L^2(\Omega)\},&s=1/2,\\ \{u\in H^s(\Omega):\gamma_0u=0\},&1/2<s\le2.\end{cases}$$

At $s=1/2$ the norm on the displayed space is $\|u\|_{H^{1/2}(\Omega)}+\|d^{-1/2}u\|_2$; at other indices it is the inherited $H^s$ norm. In particular, for $1\le s\le2$ the last space is $H^s(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$, where $H^1_0$ is the $H^1$-closure of $C_c^\infty(\Omega)$. At $s=3/2$ no normal-derivative condition is imposed.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization) gives $A\ge I$, $D(A)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$, and equivalence of the graph and $H^2$ norms. [lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract) identifies $D(A^{s/2})$ with $[L^2(\Omega),D(A)]_{s/2}$ for $0<s<2$. The scale notation is fixed by [def-se-dirichlet-scale](item-def-se-dirichlet-scale).

[F2] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-exterior-collar-tail-retraction](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-exterior-collar-tail-retraction) supplies nested cutoffs, the maps $J,P$ below, their ordinary $L^2$ and $H^2$ endpoint bounds, $PJ=I$, and the mapping of the bounded Dirichlet $H^2$ endpoint under $P$ into the exterior Dirichlet endpoint. [lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction) supplies the corresponding ordinary fractional localization norm equivalence.

[F3] [thm-se-bounded-fractional-dirichlet-input](item-thm-se-bounded-fractional-dirichlet-input) supplies the fractional Dirichlet interpolation spaces, traces and critical distance-weight norm on a bounded smooth open set. [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction) gives $[L^2(\mathbb R^n),H^2(\mathbb R^n)]_{s/2}=H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ with equivalent norms. [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined) transports common endpoint maps through complex interpolation.

[F4] On a bounded smooth domain, the kernel of the $H^1$ trace is the test-function closure [math:thm-kernel-of-the-trace-is-w-one-p-zero](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-kernel-of-the-trace-is-w-one-p-zero). Smooth multiplication obeys the weak product rule [math:lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor), and $C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ is dense in $H^1(\mathbb R^n)$ [Compactly supported smooth functions are dense in $W^{k,p}(\mathbb R^n)$](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-compactly-supported-smooth-functions-are-dense-in-wkp-of-rn). Full Choice, including the countable instances used by these suppliers, is assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] [lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization](item-lem-se-exterior-dirichlet-realization) gives $A\ge I$, $D(A)=H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$, and equivalence of the graph and $H^2$ norms. [lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract](item-lem-se-positive-spectral-scale-contract) identifies $D(A^{s/2})$ with $[L^2(\Omega),D(A)]_{s/2}$ for $0<s<2$. The scale notation is fixed by [def-se-dirichlet-scale](item-def-se-dirichlet-scale).

[F2] [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-exterior-collar-tail-retraction](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-exterior-collar-tail-retraction) supplies nested cutoffs, the maps $J,P$ below, their ordinary $L^2$ and $H^2$ endpoint bounds, $PJ=I$, and the mapping of the bounded Dirichlet $H^2$ endpoint under $P$ into the exterior Dirichlet endpoint. [lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction) supplies the corresponding ordinary fractional localization norm equivalence.

[F3] [thm-se-bounded-fractional-dirichlet-input](item-thm-se-bounded-fractional-dirichlet-input) supplies the fractional Dirichlet interpolation spaces, traces and critical distance-weight norm on a bounded smooth open set. [lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction](item-lem-se-author-891dd21c27d6-quotient-interpolation-retraction) gives $[L^2(\mathbb R^n),H^2(\mathbb R^n)]_{s/2}=H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ with equivalent norms. [lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined](item-lem-se-author-ccbb64dfef2c-strip-model-well-defined) transports common endpoint maps through complex interpolation.

[F4] On a bounded smooth domain, the kernel of the $H^1$ trace is the test-function closure [math:thm-kernel-of-the-trace-is-w-one-p-zero](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-kernel-of-the-trace-is-w-one-p-zero). Smooth multiplication obeys the weak product rule [math:lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-weak-leibniz-rule-with-a-smooth-factor), and $C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n)$ is dense in $H^1(\mathbb R^n)$ [Compactly supported smooth functions are dense in $W^{k,p}(\mathbb R^n)$](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-compactly-supported-smooth-functions-are-dense-in-wkp-of-rn). Full Choice, including the countable instances used by these suppliers, is assumed [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice).



**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove the three space identifications and norm equivalences, including the critical index and the $H^1_0$ reformulation.

1.1 Choose $L$ and smooth cutoffs $\eta,a,\beta$ as in the collar–tail supplier: $\Theta\Subset B_{L/4}$, $\eta=1$ near $\Theta$ and is supported in $B_{L/2}$, $a=1$ near $\operatorname{supp}\eta$ and is supported in $B_L$, and $\beta=1$ near $\Theta$ with support in the interior of the plateau $\{\eta=1\}$. Put $b=1-\beta$, $G=\Omega\cap B_L$, and let $Zu$ be the zero extension to $\mathbb R^n$ of $(1-\eta)u$. Define $Ju=((\eta u)|_G,Zu)$ and $P(v,w)=V_av+(bw)|_\Omega$, where $V_av$ is $av$ on $G$ and zero on $\Omega\setminus G$. The supplier proves both maps bounded on the ordinary $L^2$ and $H^2$ endpoint couples, and $PJ=I$. In particular the unbounded tail zero extension is justified there by compactly supported tests, and $V_a$ crosses only the artificial sphere, where $a$ vanishes. [F2, given]

2.1 If $u\in H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$, choose $u_j\in C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ converging to $u$ in $H^1(\Omega)$. The functions $(\eta u_j)|_G$ lie in $C_c^\infty(G)$ and converge to $(\eta u)|_G$ in $H^1(G)$ by the product rule. Thus the first component of $Ju$ lies in $H^2(G)\cap H^1_0(G)$; the second lies in $H^2(\mathbb R^n)$. Conversely, the collar–tail supplier proves that $P$ sends the product of those two endpoint spaces into $H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)$. Hence the same $J,P$ form a bounded retraction pair for the Dirichlet endpoint couples as well as for $L^2$. [F2, F4, step 1.1]

3.1 Let $0<s<2$. Apply the strip-map theorem to those two common endpoint maps. Coordinate projections and injections give one direction of the finite-product interpolation norm comparison; pairing admissible component strip functions gives the other. Consequently $J$ and $P$ give a retraction between $[L^2(\Omega),H^2(\Omega)\cap H^1_0(\Omega)]_{s/2}$ and the product of the bounded Dirichlet interpolation space on $G$ with $H^s(\mathbb R^n)$, with equivalent norms. By the spectral scale this exterior interpolation space is $H_D^s(\Omega)$. Thus $u\in H_D^s$ exactly when its collar component belongs to the bounded Dirichlet interpolation space and $Zu\in H^s(\mathbb R^n)$; its scale norm is equivalent to the sum of those component norms. [F1, F3, step 2.1]

4.1 The ordinary collar–tail retraction says that $u\in H^s(\Omega)$ exactly when $(\eta u)|_G\in H^s(G)$ and $Zu\in H^s(\mathbb R^n)$, with equivalent norms. The bounded-domain input therefore gives the claimed result for $0<s<1/2$. For $1/2<s<2$, define $\gamma_0u$ as the trace of $(\eta u)|_G$ on the obstacle boundary. Its trace on the artificial sphere is zero because $\eta$ vanishes in a neighborhood of that sphere. Since $\eta=1$ near the obstacle, the obstacle trace is independent of the chosen such cutoff: the difference has support away from that boundary and has zero localized trace. The bounded-domain input now gives precisely $\gamma_0u=0$ and no further condition, including at $s=3/2$. [F2, F3, step 3.1]

4.2 At $s=1/2$, the bounded input requires $d_G^{-1/2}(\eta u)|_G\in L^2(G)$, where $d_G=\operatorname{dist}(\cdot,\partial G)$. In a fixed neighborhood of the obstacle, $d_G=d$ after choosing the neighborhood sufficiently small; away from that neighborhood, $d$ is bounded below, while $\eta$ vanishes near the artificial sphere and $d_G$ is bounded below on the remaining relevant support. Also $d^{-1}$ is bounded on the exterior tail. Splitting the integrals into these regions shows that the bounded collar weighted norm plus the tail $H^{1/2}$ norm is equivalent to $\|u\|_{H^{1/2}(\Omega)}+\|d^{-1/2}u\|_2$. This proves the critical assertion without identifying it with the $H^{1/2}$-closure of test functions. [F2, F3, step 3.1]

5.1 At $s=0$, $H_D^0=L^2=H^0$; at $s=2$, the realization theorem gives $H_D^2=H^2\cap H^1_0$ with equivalent norms. For $u\in H^1(\Omega)$, zero obstacle trace implies $(\eta u)|_G\in H^1_0(G)$ by the bounded trace-kernel theorem, since its artificial-sphere trace is zero. Approximate that collar component by compactly supported smooth functions in $G$ and approximate $Zu\in H^1(\mathbb R^n)$ by compactly supported smooth whole-space functions. Applying $P$ to these approximants yields compactly supported smooth functions in $\Omega$ converging to $u$, because $a$ is supported inside $B_L$ and $b$ vanishes near the obstacle. Conversely, restriction and smooth multiplication carry any $H^1_0(\Omega)$ approximating sequence to a test-function approximating sequence for the collar component, so its trace vanishes. Thus $\ker\gamma_0=H^1_0(\Omega)$ in $H^1(\Omega)$. The $H^s$-to-$H^1$ inclusion for $s\ge1$ follows from the restriction quotient and the whole-space Fourier weights. Combining this with the previous steps proves the stated $1\le s\le2$ reformulation and all endpoint claims. [F1, F2, F3, F4, step 1.1, step 4.1, step 4.2] ∎

### Source notes

The bounded-domain fractional identification is the explicitly imported input recorded in [thm-se-bounded-fractional-dirichlet-input](item-thm-se-bounded-fractional-dirichlet-input). The exterior localization and application above are local arguments; the article under study is not used as a prerequisite source.


## Fractional compactness on bounded collar domains

[Open item](item-lem-se-local-fractional-rellich) · level 10


### Statement

Assume the Axiom of Choice. Let $G\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a bounded smooth domain, $n\ge1$. Define $H^{1/2}(G)$ as the restrictions to $G$ of $H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^n)$, with the infimum norm over all extensions. Then the inclusion $H^{1/2}(G)\hookrightarrow L^2(G)$ is compact: it sends bounded sets to sets with compact closure.

Let $H^2_0(G)$ be the closure of $C_c^\infty(G)$ in the weak-derivative $W^{2,2}(G)$ norm. With complex scalars, write $(H^2_0(G))'$ for the continuous conjugate-linear functionals on $H^2_0(G)$. The pairing $f\mapsto[v\mapsto\int_G f\overline v\,dx]$ is a continuous injective linear map from $L^2(G)$ into $(H^2_0(G))'$. Thus the triple $H^{1/2}(G)\hookrightarrow L^2(G)\hookrightarrow(H^2_0(G))'$ has compact first and continuous injective second inclusion. For the convention in which the dual consists of linear functionals, conjugating this pairing gives the corresponding continuous injective conjugate-linear map.

### Statement references

[lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction)

[math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces)

[math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure)

[math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm)

[math:lem-complex-lp-completeness-density-and-inner-product](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-complex-lp-completeness-density-and-inner-product)

[math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[math:ex-zero-order-bessel-completion-is-ltwo](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-zero-order-bessel-completion-is-ltwo)

[math:thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms)

[math:lem-japanese-bracket-powers-preserve-schwartz-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-japanese-bracket-powers-preserve-schwartz-space)

[math:thm-tempered-distributions-embed-continuously-in-distributions](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-tempered-distributions-embed-continuously-in-distributions)

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses, spaces and pairing in the Statement.

[F1] The Axiom of Choice supplies the countable and dependent choice hypotheses of the cited Fourier and compactness results. [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice)

[F2] For bounded smooth $G$, there is a bounded linear extension $E:H^{1/2}(G)\to H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^n)$, agreeing with its input on $G$, whose outputs have support in one fixed compact set. The domain space uses the restriction-quotient convention. [lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction)

[F3] Use the negative-sign $2\pi$ distributional Fourier transform $\mathcal F$ and $\langle\xi\rangle=(1+|\xi|^2)^{1/2}$. For $s\in\{0,1/2\}$, the canonical embedding $\iota_s$ identifies $H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ bijectively with the tempered distributions $T$ such that $\langle\xi\rangle^s\mathcal FT=u_g$ for an $L^2$ density $g$, and the $H^s$ norm is $\|g\|_2$. Here $u_g$ is the regular distribution with bilinear test pairing $\varphi\mapsto\int g\varphi$. The Plancherel extension $\mathcal F_2$ is a complex-linear $L^2$ isometry. [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) [math:thm-plancherel](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-plancherel)

[F4] For an $L^1$ function, translation $\tau_hU(x)=U(x-h)$ multiplies its Fourier transform by $e^{-2\pi i h\cdot\xi}$. [math:thm-fourier-translation-modulation-dilation-and-reflection-laws](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-translation-modulation-dilation-and-reflection-laws)

[F5] A uniformly bounded $L^2(\mathbb R^n)$ family with uniformly vanishing tails and uniformly small translation differences has compact closure in $L^2(\mathbb R^n)$. Uniform support in a bounded set supplies the tail condition. [math:thm-frechet-kolmogorov-compactness-criterion-in-lp](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-frechet-kolmogorov-compactness-criterion-in-lp) [math:lem-bounded-support-makes-frechet-kolmogorov-tail-control-automatic](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-bounded-support-makes-frechet-kolmogorov-tail-control-automatic)

[F6] The $W^{2,2}$ norm dominates the $L^2$ norm, and $H^2_0(G)$ contains every smooth compactly supported test function. The $L^2$ pairing satisfies Cauchy–Schwarz. [math:def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-sobolev-space-wkp-and-its-norm) [math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure) [math:lem-complex-lp-completeness-density-and-inner-product](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-complex-lp-completeness-density-and-inner-product)

[F7] A locally integrable function on an open set that pairs to zero with every smooth compactly supported test function vanishes almost everywhere. [math:lem-fundamental-lemma-of-the-calculus-of-variations](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-fundamental-lemma-of-the-calculus-of-variations)

[F8] There is a surjective linear isometry $I_0:H^0(\mathbb R^n)\to L^2(\mathbb R^n)$ with $\iota_0(V)=u_{I_0V}$. For $f\in L^2$, $\mathcal Fu_f=u_{\mathcal F_2f}$; for $f\in L^1$, $\mathcal Fu_f=u_{\widehat f}$ with $\widehat f$ the integral transform. All these distributional equalities use the same bilinear pairing and negative-sign $2\pi$ convention. [math:ex-zero-order-bessel-completion-is-ltwo](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/ex-zero-order-bessel-completion-is-ltwo) [math:thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-transform-agrees-with-l-one-and-plancherel-transforms)

[F9] Multiplication by $\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}$ and $\langle\xi\rangle^{-1/2}$ gives mutually inverse operators on $\mathcal S'$; by transposition, multiplying a regular distribution by either weight gives the regular distribution of the corresponding product whenever that product is used below. [math:lem-japanese-bracket-powers-preserve-schwartz-space](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-japanese-bracket-powers-preserve-schwartz-space)

[F10] Restriction to compactly supported tests is an injective map $\rho:\mathcal S'(\mathbb R^n)\to\mathcal D'(\mathbb R^n)$. For a regular tempered distribution $u_f$, its restriction acts by the same integral $\varphi\mapsto\int f\varphi$; further restriction to an open set uses tests supported in that set. [math:thm-tempered-distributions-embed-continuously-in-distributions](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-tempered-distributions-embed-continuously-in-distributions)

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Prove compactness of the first inclusion and continuity and injectivity of the pairing into the conjugate dual.

1.1 Let $U\in H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^n)$, put $T=\iota_{1/2}U$, and choose its unique density $g$ from [F3], so $\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}\mathcal FT=u_g$ and $\|g\|_2=\|U\|_{H^{1/2}}$. By [F9], $\mathcal FT=u_q$, where $q=\langle\xi\rangle^{-1/2}g\in L^2$ and $\|q\|_2\le\|g\|_2$. The $s=0$ case of [F3] therefore supplies $V\in H^0$ with $\iota_0V=T$. By [F8], $f=I_0V\in L^2$ satisfies $T=u_f$. The same fact gives $u_{\mathcal F_2f}=\mathcal FT=u_q$. Restricting this equality to compact tests by [F10] and applying [F7] to the locally integrable difference gives $\mathcal F_2f=q$ almost everywhere. Thus $\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}\mathcal F_2f=g$ almost everywhere and $\|f\|_2=\|q\|_2\le\|U\|_{H^{1/2}}$, with $\|\langle\xi\rangle^{1/2}\mathcal F_2f\|_2=\|U\|_{H^{1/2}}$. This identification is linear and injective because the distribution embeddings and $I_0$ are linear and injective. [F1, F3, F7, F8, F9, F10, given]

1.2 For $f\in L^2(G)$ and $v\in H^2_0(G)$, [F6] gives $|\int_G f\overline v|\le\|f\|_2\|v\|_2\le\|f\|_2\|v\|_{W^{2,2}}$. Thus the displayed pairing is a well-defined continuous conjugate-linear functional of $v$, and its operator norm is at most $\|f\|_2$. It is linear in $f$. [F6, given]

2.1 Under [F10], the distributional restriction of $T=u_f$ to $G$ acts on every test in $C_c^\infty(G)$ by integration against $f|_G$. Two whole-space extensions of the same restriction class therefore give $L^2(G)$ functions whose difference pairs to zero with all these tests; [F7] makes them equal almost everywhere. This defines the claimed map $H^{1/2}(G)\to L^2(G)$. If its value is zero, its restricted distribution is zero, so its restriction class is zero, proving injectivity. For every whole-space extension $U$ of a class $u$, step 1.1 gives $\|u\|_{L^2(G)}\le\|U\|_{H^{1/2}(\mathbb R^n)}$; taking the infimum over extensions proves $\|u\|_{L^2(G)}\le\|u\|_{H^{1/2}(G)}$. Hence this inclusion is well-defined, linear and continuous in exactly the restriction-quotient convention of the Statement. [F2, F7, F10, step 1.1, given]

3.1 Let $B$ be bounded in $H^{1/2}(G)$ and identify $\mathcal F=E(B)$ with its $L^2$ classes by step 1.1. By [F2], $\sup_{U\in\mathcal F}\|U\|_{H^{1/2}}<\infty$, hence $\sup_{U\in\mathcal F}\|U\|_2<\infty$. The distributions of these extensions are supported in one fixed compact set $K$. By [F10] they pair to zero with all compact tests in $\mathbb R^n\setminus K$, and [F7] makes their $L^2$ representatives vanish almost everywhere there. Thus their $L^2$ tails vanish uniformly by [F5]. Cauchy–Schwarz on $K$ gives $\|U\|_1\le |K|^{1/2}\|U\|_2$, so each member also belongs to $L^1$. [F2, F5, F6, F7, F10, step 1.1, step 2.1, given]

4.1 For any $V\in L^1\cap L^2$, the two Fourier agreement identities in [F8] give $u_{\widehat V}=u_{\mathcal F_2V}$; by [F10] and [F7], $\widehat V=\mathcal F_2V$ almost everywhere. If $U\in\mathcal F$, both $U$ and $\tau_hU$ belong to $L^1\cap L^2$, since substituting $x-h$ for $x$ preserves both norms. Applying this agreement and [F4], and then the Plancherel identity [F3], gives $\|\tau_hU-U\|_2^2=\int |e^{-2\pi i h\cdot\xi}-1|^2|\mathcal F_2U(\xi)|^2\,d\xi$. The elementary estimate $|e^{-it}-1|^2\le\min\{t^2,4\}\le2|t|$ gives $|e^{-2\pi i h\cdot\xi}-1|^2\le4\pi|h|\langle\xi\rangle$. By step 1.1, $\|\tau_hU-U\|_2^2\le4\pi|h|\|U\|_{H^{1/2}}^2$, uniformly for $U\in\mathcal F$. [F3, F4, F7, F8, F10, step 1.1, step 3.1]

5.1 The boundedness, tail and translation hypotheses of [F5] now hold for $\mathcal F$, so its closure is compact in $L^2(\mathbb R^n)$. Restriction to $G$ is an $L^2$ contraction, and by [F2] and step 2.1 it restricts $Eu$ to the same $L^2(G)$ class as $u$ for every $u\in B$. Thus $B$ lies in the compact image of the closure of $\mathcal F$ under restriction, and its closure in $L^2(G)$ is compact. This proves compactness of the first inclusion. [F1, F2, F5, step 2.1, step 3.1, step 4.1]

6.1 If the functional in step 1.2 is zero, it vanishes on $C_c^\infty(G)$ by [F6]. Complex conjugation preserves the test space, so $\int_G f\varphi=0$ for every test function $\varphi$. Since $f\in L^2(G)$ is locally integrable by Cauchy–Schwarz on compact subsets, [F7] yields $f=0$ almost everywhere. Hence the second map is injective; together with steps 5.1 and 1.2 this proves the stated triple. Conjugating the scalar value of each functional gives the stated continuous injective conjugate-linear map into the scalar-linear dual, with the same norm bound. [F6, F7, step 5.1, step 1.2] ∎

### Remarks

The compactness assertion is local to a bounded domain; it does not assert compactness of the corresponding embedding on an unbounded exterior domain. No smoothing estimate is asserted.


## Canonical higher traces on a bounded boundary or compact interior hypersurface

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-smooth-collar-trace-assembly) · level 10


### Statement

Assume AC. Let $G\subset\mathbb R^n$, $n\ge2$, be smooth bounded, let $S$ be a smooth boundary component, and let $1\le s\le2$. There is a bounded linear trace $\gamma_S:H^s(G)\to H^{s-1/2}(S)$, with the restriction-quotient domain norm and finite-atlas boundary norm. It agrees as an $L^2(S)$ function with the existing first-order Sobolev trace and is independent of the chosen whole-space extension and charts. Smooth cutoffs commute with it and it is local near $S$.

For the interior assertion, let $U\subset\mathbb R^n$ be open and let $S\subset U$ be a compact smooth embedded hypersurface without boundary, with the induced smooth structure. Use the same whole-space Bessel-potential convention and define
$$H^s(U)=\{F|_U:F\in H^s(\mathbb R^n)\},\qquad \|u\|_{H^s(U)}=\inf_{F|_U=u}\|F\|_{H^s(\mathbb R^n)}.$$
There is a bounded linear trace $\gamma_{S,U}:H^s(U)\to H^{s-1/2}(S)$. For nonempty $S$ one can choose a bounded smooth open neighborhood $D$ with $S\subset D$ and $\overline D\subset U$, restrict $u$ to $D$, and assemble this trace there. Restriction satisfies $\|u|_D\|_{H^s(D)}\le\|u\|_{H^s(U)}$. The resulting trace agrees with smooth restriction and is independent of the chosen neighborhood, whole-space extension, charts and local side; it commutes with smooth cutoffs and is local near $S$. For empty $S$ the trace is the zero map. The constants may depend on the fixed neighborhood, its finite atlas and $s$; no uniform assertion for uncontrolled noncompact geometry is made.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction) gives one bounded $E_G:H^s(G)\to H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ for every $0\le s\le2$, agreeing with the input on $G$.

[F3] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators) bounds localized pullbacks on the ambient $H^s$ scale, and [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-hypersurface-atlas-and-derivatives](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-hypersurface-atlas-and-derivatives) gives complete atlas-independent $H^{s-1/2}(S)$ norms and their continuous inclusion into surface $L^2$.

[F4] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-flat-fourier-trace](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-flat-fourier-trace) gives the bounded flat trace and shows its $L^2$ value vanishes when the input is zero on a one-sided neighborhood after localization.

[F5] [math:thm-sharp-trace-theorem-for-w-one-p](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-sharp-trace-theorem-for-w-one-p) supplies the bounded $H^1(G)$ trace whose target norm contains $L^2(S)$, and [math:lem-trace-commutes-with-smooth-boundary-cutoffs-and-charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-trace-commutes-with-smooth-boundary-cutoffs-and-charts) supplies its agreement with classical smooth restrictions, chart transport, cutoff commutation and locality. No identification of its Slobodeckij target with a Bessel target is assumed.

[F6] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) defines whole-space $H^s$ as a completion with dense Schwartz core, and [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) gives convergence of these approximants in the weak-derivative $W^{1,2}$ norm when $s\ge1$.

[F7] [Bounded $C^k$ domains and boundary charts](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-bounded-c-k-domain-and-boundary-charts) specifies the one-sided smooth graph charts used for $G$ (with smooth graph functions), without requiring connectedness. [math:lem-smooth-euclidean-hypersurface-graph-and-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-smooth-euclidean-hypersurface-graph-and-localization) supplies smooth graph charts and local smooth unit normals for embedded hypersurfaces. [math:thm-heine-borel-rn](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-heine-borel-rn) identifies closed bounded Euclidean sets as compact. [math:lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-finite-ambient-partitions-for-euclidean-boundary-integration) supplies nonnegative ambient smooth cutoffs compactly supported in a finite open cover of a compact set, with sum one near that set.

[F8] Under countable choice, [math:thm-euclidean-tubular-neighbourhood-theorem](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-euclidean-tubular-neighbourhood-theorem) gives a positive smooth radius $\delta$ on an embedded smooth $S$ for which $E(p,v)=p+v$ is a diffeomorphism from $\{(p,v)\in N^\perp S:|v|<\delta(p)\}$ onto an open neighborhood of $S$. Here [math:def-normal-addition-map-for-a-euclidean-submanifold](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-normal-addition-map-for-a-euclidean-submanifold) defines $N^\perp S=\{(p,v):p\in S,\ v\perp T_pS\}$ with its smooth normal-bundle structure and the map $E$. AC in F1 supplies the countable-choice hypothesis.

[F9] [math:def-nitsche-restriction-bessel-potential-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-nitsche-restriction-bessel-potential-sobolev-spaces) defines the restriction space and quotient norm for every nonempty open Euclidean set and every nonnegative order, using the canonical $L^2$ realization of the Bessel-potential completion. Its countable-choice assumption follows from F1. We use this convention for the arbitrary open region $U$ as well as for $D$; if $U$ is empty, its restriction space contains only zero.

[F10] [math:def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart) defines embedded submanifolds by ambient slice charts and the subspace topology; [math:def-codimension-and-hypersurface](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-codimension-and-hypersurface) defines a hypersurface as an embedded submanifold of codimension one. [math:lem-slice-chart-restrictions-form-a-smooth-atlas](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-slice-chart-restrictions-form-a-smooth-atlas) supplies the smooth atlas on $S$ obtained by restricting slice charts, with its subspace topology; this is the induced smooth structure used here.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement with the stated spaces and bounds.

1.1 The boundary $\partial G$ is closed and bounded, hence compact. Its component $S$ is closed in $\partial G$ and therefore compact; the smooth boundary charts make it an embedded hypersurface without boundary. Choose finitely many ambient flattening patches covering $S$. F7 supplies ambient $\chi_i$ compactly supported in those patches with $\sum_i\chi_i=1$ near $S$. Shrink or refine the patches so their surface coordinates and the restrictions of $\chi_i$ give a finite atlas and subordinate partition. For any $F\in H^s(\mathbb R^n)$, pull each $\chi_iF$ back to the flattened coordinates and extend by zero only past the artificial chart edges. F3 bounds this whole-space $H^s$ operation; F4 bounds its flat trace in $H^{s-1/2}(\mathbb R^{n-1})$. Approximate $F$ by Schwartz $F_j$ in $H^s$. Their ordinary surface restrictions have these localized coordinate traces and are Cauchy in the complete finite-atlas norm from F3. Their limit defines a bounded linear map $T_S:H^s(\mathbb R^n)\to H^{s-1/2}(S)$ agreeing with smooth restriction. The same construction works for any compact smooth embedded hypersurface without boundary: use the graph charts in F7 in place of the boundary charts. In both cases $\|T_SF\|_{H^{s-1/2}(S)}\le C\|F\|_{H^s(\mathbb R^n)}$. [F3, F4, F6, F7, F10, given]

1.2 For nonempty compact interior $S\subset U$, apply F8. Compactness and positivity of $\delta$ give a uniform $\varepsilon>0$ with $2\varepsilon<\delta(p)$ for every $p\in S$; shrink $\varepsilon$ so that $p+v\in U$ whenever $p\in S$ and $|v|\le2\varepsilon$. The latter follows by covering $S$ by finitely many balls $B(p_j,r_j)$ with $B(p_j,2r_j)\subset U$ and taking $2\varepsilon<\min_j r_j$. Put $K_\varepsilon=\{(p,v)\in N^\perp S:|v|\le\varepsilon\}$ and $D=E(\{|v|<\varepsilon\})$. The set $K_\varepsilon$ is closed and bounded in $\mathbb R^{2n}$: $S$ is compact, and normality is a closed condition in local smooth tangent frames. It is compact by F7. The tube diffeomorphism makes $D$ open, with $\overline D=E(K_\varepsilon)\subset U$ compact and $\partial D=E(\{|v|=\varepsilon\})$. On each graph patch of $S$, a local smooth unit normal $\nu$ gives tube coordinates $(y,t)\mapsto p(y)+t\nu(p(y))$, in which $D$ is $|t|<\varepsilon$ and its boundary is $t=\pm\varepsilon$. Thus the boundary is smoothly embedded and $D$ occupies exactly one side locally. F7 converts these local slices to the one-sided graph convention, so $D$ is a bounded smooth domain of the type required by F2. This construction uses only local normals, not a global choice of side. Compactness supplies the first uniform radius by taking a finite subcover of sets where $\delta$ has a positive lower bound. [F1, F2, F7, F8, F10, given]

2.1 For $u\in H^s(G)$ define $\gamma_Su=T_S(E_Gu)$ using F2. If two whole-space extensions of $u$ are used, their difference vanishes on $G$. In each localized boundary chart the pulled-back difference is zero on the corresponding half-space: inside the chart this is the one-sided domain condition, and outside the chart it is zero by its compact support margin. F4, from either sign of the normal coordinate, makes its flat trace zero in $L^2$. Thus all localized traces agree, proving extension independence and $\|\gamma_Su\|_{H^{s-1/2}(S)}\le C\|u\|_{H^s(G)}$. The same argument applies to extensions agreeing on just a one-sided neighborhood of a boundary patch after further localization. [F2, F3, F4, F7, step 1.1]

2.2 In the stated quotient convention, every whole-space extension $F$ of $u\in H^s(U)$ also extends $u|_D$. Hence $\|u|_D\|_{H^s(D)}\le\|F\|_{H^s}$ for every such $F$, and taking their infimum gives the asserted contractive restriction. The quotient expression is a norm: it controls $\|u\|_{L^2(U)}$ by the nonnegative Fourier weight, is homogeneous, and satisfies the triangle inequality by adding extensions with norms arbitrarily close to their infima. Its kernel consists precisely of zero restrictions. Define $\gamma_{S,U}u=T_S(E_D(u|_D))$ using F2 on the fixed $D$ from step 1.2 and the interior version of $T_S$ in step 1.1. Both operators are linear and bounded, so $\|\gamma_{S,U}u\|_{H^{s-1/2}(S)}\le C_{S,D,s}\|u\|_{H^s(U)}$. [F2, F3, F6, F9, step 1.1, step 1.2, algebra]

3.1 Choose the Schwartz approximants to $E_Gu$ used above. Since $s\ge1$, their $H^s$ convergence implies $H^1$ convergence by the Fourier weights; F6 converts this to norm convergence of the functions and their first weak derivatives. Restriction to $G$ preserves that convergence, as follows directly by testing weak derivatives with compactly supported tests in $G$ and bounding their $L^2(G)$ norms by the ambient norms. The published first-order trace in F5 therefore converges in $L^2(S)$ to its trace of $u$. On these smooth approximants it agrees with the classical restriction, whose new traces converge in $H^{s-1/2}(S)$ and hence in $L^2(S)$ by F3. Uniqueness of the $L^2$ limit proves the claimed agreement without identifying a Slobodeckij norm with a Bessel norm. [F2, F3, F5, F6, step 1.1, step 2.1]

4.1 The atlas lemma makes the boundary target independent of charts up to equivalent norms.
