Two choices of charts give bounded trace maps agreeing on ambient smooth restrictions; the same Schwartz approximation proves that their $L^2$ values agree. For a fixed ambient smooth cutoff, the identity between the trace of its product and the product of its surface value with the trace holds on smooth functions and passes to the limit using the ambient and surface multiplier bounds in F3. If data agree on a neighborhood of $S$, localizing their difference in the patches of step 1.1 makes every flat trace zero by F4. This proves cutoff commutation and locality, consistently with the first-order interface in F5. [F3, F4, F5, F6, step 1.1, step 2.1, step 3.1]

5.1 For any whole-space $F$ with $F|_U=u$, the difference $E_D(u|_D)-F$ is zero on the neighborhood $D$ of $S$. The local flat uniqueness in F4, applied chartwise as in step 4.1, gives $\gamma_{S,U}u=T_SF$. This identity proves independence of $D$ and of extensions, including a change of neighborhood, since all such constructions give $T_SF$. Atlas independence, agreement with classical smooth restriction, locality, and cutoff commutation follow from the same ambient construction and step 4.1; on a local side where a first-order boundary trace is defined, step 3.1 identifies its $L^2$ value with this trace. The two flat one-sided traces also agree because F4 makes both restrictions of the same whole-space trace. If $S$ is empty the zero map has all asserted properties. All bounds use fixed compact charts and, for the collar construction, fixed $D$; thus the stated dependence of constants and the absence of a noncompact uniformity claim are preserved. [F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, step 1.1, step 3.1, step 4.1, step 2.2] ∎



## Partial extension across an artificial sphere and annular cutoff gluing

[Open item](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-artificial-sphere-extension-and-gluing) · level 11


### Statement

Assume AC and let $n\ge2$. Let $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$ be a smooth compact-obstacle exterior, let $\Theta\Subset B_L$, and put $G_0=\Omega\cap B_L$. If $1\le s\le2$, $v\in H^s(G_0)$ and $v=0$ on a neighborhood of the outer sphere inside $G_0$, then the function equal to $v$ on $G_0$ and zero on $\Omega\setminus G_0$ belongs to $H^s(\Omega)$. For each fixed gap and separating cutoff this extension is bounded, with constants allowed to depend on that gap/cutoff. No vanishing near $\partial\Theta$ is required, and no uniform shrinking-gap assertion is made.

For $1\le s\le2$ and $U\in H^s(\Omega)$, define the exterior obstacle trace by
$$\Gamma_\Omega U=\gamma^{G_0}_{\partial\Theta}(U|_{G_0})\in H^{s-1/2}(\partial\Theta),$$
where $\gamma^{G_0}_{\partial\Theta}$ is the bounded-domain boundary trace, applied on each obstacle-boundary component. This defines a bounded linear map independent of the choice of truncation radius $L$ with $\Theta\Subset B_L$. It is local near $\partial\Theta$ and commutes with smooth cutoffs. The notation $\gamma_{\partial\Theta}$ for an exterior input means $\Gamma_\Omega$; for an input on $G_0$ it means $\gamma^{G_0}_{\partial\Theta}$.

Fix $\varepsilon>0$ with $\Theta\subset B_{L-3\varepsilon}$. Fix smooth bounded cutoffs $\zeta,\widetilde\zeta$ on $\mathbb R^n$, with bounded derivatives through order two, such that $\zeta=0$ on $B_{L-2\varepsilon}$, $\zeta=1$ for $|x|\ge L-\varepsilon$, $\widetilde\zeta=0$ on $B_{L-3\varepsilon}$, and $\widetilde\zeta=1$ on $\operatorname{supp}\zeta$. Let $\mathcal A=\{L-3\varepsilon<|x|<L\}$ and let $E_{\mathcal A}$ be the simultaneous bounded annular extension. Then
$$\mathcal G u=\widehat{(1-\zeta)u}+\widetilde\zeta E_{\mathcal A}((\zeta u)|_{\mathcal A})|_\Omega$$
is a bounded linear map $H^s(G_0)\to H^s(\Omega)$ with $(\mathcal G u)|_{G_0}=u$, where the hat extends only across the artificial sphere. Its canonical trace on $\partial\Theta$ equals that of $u$. If this inner trace is zero, then $\mathcal G u\in H^1_0(\Omega)$ as well. The cutoffs and gap are fixed in this gluing assertion.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available.

[F2] [lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction) provides bounded whole-space extensions from the smooth bounded $G_0$ and annulus $\mathcal A$, and from the exterior, for every order zero through two, in the restriction-quotient convention. Its weak endpoint identification is at order two; the first-order whole-space identification used below is supplied separately in F7.

[F3] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-localized-chart-operators) bounds whole-space cutoff multiplication at all these orders. [math:lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-test-function-cutoffs-and-euclidean-localization) constructs the separating cutoffs. Domain cutoff bounds follow by applying a whole-space extension, multiplying there, and restricting; their constants depend on the chosen cutoff.

[F4] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-smooth-collar-trace-assembly](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-smooth-collar-trace-assembly) gives the bounded-domain boundary traces and their locality and cutoff commutation, including equality with the existing $H^1$ trace as an $L^2$ function. In its Proof steps 1.1 and 2.1 these traces are constructed as $\gamma^G_S u=T_S F$ for any whole-space extension $F$ of $u$, with the same ambient surface trace $T_S$ for each fixed compact smooth $S$ and independent of the extension and charts. We apply this bounded-domain construction on truncations of the exterior; no boundary-trace theorem for an unbounded domain is assumed.

[F5] [math:thm-kernel-of-the-trace-is-w-one-p-zero](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-kernel-of-the-trace-is-w-one-p-zero) identifies the zero trace kernel in $W^{1,2}$ with $W^{1,2}_0$ on a bounded smooth domain. [math:def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-wkp-zero-as-a-sobolev-closure) defines this weak-norm closure by compactly supported tests, denoted $H^1_0$ for complex scalars and $p=2$. [Compactly supported smooth functions are dense in $W^{k,p}(\mathbb R^n)$](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/cor-compactly-supported-smooth-functions-are-dense-in-wkp-of-rn) supplies whole-space $W^{1,2}$ approximation by compactly supported smooth functions. F7 converts the whole-space approximations to the Fourier $H^1$ norm.

[F6] [math:lem-zero-extension-from-w-one-p-zero](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/lem-zero-extension-from-w-one-p-zero) gives a linear zero-extension isometry $W^{1,2}_0(G_0;\mathbb C)\to W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$: weak first derivatives are extended by zero and all component norms are preserved. Its Countable Choice hypothesis follows from the standing AC assumption. This applies after the bounded trace-kernel theorem has established zero-boundary membership; it requires no compact support for the limiting input.

[F7] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-integer-order-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) at $k=1$ identifies whole-space Fourier $H^1(\mathbb R^n)$ with $W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$ under the regular-distribution embedding, with equivalent norms. It uses the negative-sign $2\pi$ Fourier normalization and bracket weight of F2. Its hypotheses $n\ge1$, complex scalars and Countable Choice hold here, the last by F1. We use this whole-space equivalence, not an unstated first-order domain identification.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement with the stated spaces and bounds.

1.1 Compactness of the artificial sphere gives $\delta>0$ with $v=0$ for $L-\delta<|x|<L$ inside $G_0$; shrink $\delta$ if necessary to separate this collar from $\Theta$. Choose $\rho\in C_c^\infty(B_L)$ equal to $1$ on $B_{L-\delta}$ and supported in $B_{L-\delta/2}$. If $E_{G_0}$ is the ordinary simultaneous extension, then $r_\Omega(\rho E_{G_0}v)$ equals $v$ on $G_0$ and zero outside $B_L$, so it is exactly the partial extension. Its $H^s(\Omega)$ norm is at most $C_{s,\rho}\|v\|_{H^s(G_0)}$ by the whole-space extension and multiplier bounds. The construction uses values inside $\Theta$ only as an ordinary extension auxiliary and then restricts to $\Omega$; it never asserts zero extension across the obstacle. The dependence on $\rho$ is retained, so no uniform bound for arbitrary shrinking gaps is inferred. [F2, F3, given]

1.2 For a fixed admissible truncation $G_0$, restriction $H^s(\Omega)\to H^s(G_0)$ is contractive: every whole-space extension of $U$ is also one of $U|_{G_0}$, so take the infimum of their norms. F4 on each obstacle-boundary component therefore makes $\Gamma_\Omega$ bounded and linear into $H^{s-1/2}(\partial\Theta)$. To compare two admissible truncations $G_a=\Omega\cap B_{L_a}$ and $G_b=\Omega\cap B_{L_b}$, take one whole-space $F\in H^s(\mathbb R^n)$ with $F|_\Omega=U$, which exists by the restriction-space convention. It extends both $U|_{G_a}$ and $U|_{G_b}$. The ambient construction in F4 computes both bounded-domain obstacle traces as $T_{\partial\Theta}F$, componentwise, so their values agree. This also proves independence of any bounded smooth truncation agreeing with $\Omega$ on a neighborhood of its compact obstacle boundary. Locality and cutoff commutation follow by restricting to a fixed such truncation and applying those properties in F4; F3 makes the cutoff products bounded. The $L^2$ value agrees with the bounded-domain $H^1$ trace there. [F2, F3, F4, given]

2.1 For the gluing formula, $(1-\zeta)u$ has the fixed outer gap $\varepsilon$ and bounded $H^s(G_0)$ norm, so the first term is bounded by the preceding construction. Restriction $H^s(G_0)\to H^s(\mathcal A)$ is contractive in the quotient model, and multiplication by the fixed cutoffs is bounded by extension and whole-space multiplication. The second term is therefore bounded $H^s(G_0)\to H^s(\Omega)$ through $E_{\mathcal A}$. These are fixed linear operators. On $\mathcal A$ its correction restricts to $\widetilde\zeta\zeta u=\zeta u$, while below the annulus $\zeta=0$. Hence the two terms sum to $u$ on all of $G_0$. [F2, F3, step 1.1, algebra]

3.1 By step 2.1, $(\mathcal G u)|_{G_0}=u$, so the definition in step 1.2 gives $\Gamma_\Omega\mathcal G u=\gamma^{G_0}_{\partial\Theta}u$. Equivalently, near $\partial\Theta$ the first term of the gluing formula equals $u$ since $\zeta=0$, and the second term vanishes since $\widetilde\zeta=0$. The local exterior trace just constructed thus preserves the inner trace, with its higher regularity and its bounded-domain $H^1$ value in $L^2$. No zero extension across $\partial\Theta$ is involved. [F3, F4, step 2.1, step 1.2]

4.1 If the inner trace is zero, choose a smooth $\theta$ equal to $1$ near $\Theta$ with support inside $B_{L-3\varepsilon}$ and put $U=\mathcal G u$. The function $w=(\theta U)|_{G_0}=\theta u$ belongs to $H^s(G_0)$ and has zero trace on both boundary components of $G_0$: on the inner one by hypothesis and on the outer one because $\theta$ vanishes nearby. To place $w$ in the weak space, take a whole-space $H^s$ extension $Q$ of $w$. The Fourier weights imply $Q\in H^1(\mathbb R^n)$ since $s\ge1$, and F7 gives $Q\in W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$. Testing its weak derivative identity against the zero extension of any $\psi\in C_c^\infty(G_0)$ gives $D_iw=(D_iQ)|_{G_0}$. Restriction decreases each $L^2$ component norm. More generally the same test calculation on any open $D$, followed by infimizing over all whole-space $H^1$ extensions of $h$, gives $\|h\|_{W^{1,2}(D)}\le C\|h\|_{H^1(D)}$. Thus $w\in W^{1,2}(G_0)$; F4 identifies its zero trace with the first-order trace in F5, and the bounded kernel theorem gives $w\in W^{1,2}_0(G_0;\mathbb C)$. Choose $\varphi_j\in C_c^\infty(G_0;\mathbb C)$ converging to $w$ in $W^{1,2}(G_0)$, using the closure definition and the countable-choice consequence of AC. By F6 their whole-space zero extensions satisfy $\|E_0\varphi_j-E_0w\|_{W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)}=\|\varphi_j-w\|_{W^{1,2}(G_0)}\to0$. F7 converts this to whole-space $H^1$ convergence. Their restrictions to $\Omega$ lie in $C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ and converge in quotient $H^1(\Omega)$ by contractive restriction, hence also in $W^{1,2}(\Omega)$ by the bound just proved. The limit is $\theta U$: it agrees with $w$ on $G_0$ and both functions vanish on $\Omega\setminus G_0$, since $\theta$ is supported inside $B_{L-3\varepsilon}$. Thus the weak-norm closure definition gives $\theta U\in H^1_0(\Omega)$. [F1, F2, F3, F4, F5, F6, F7, step 3.1]

5.1 Take an ordinary whole-space $H^1$ extension $F$ of $U$ and set $W=(1-\theta)F$. F3 makes $W\in H^1(\mathbb R^n)$, and F7 identifies it with a $W^{1,2}$ class. Choose a smooth bounded $b$ with bounded derivatives through order two, vanishing near $\Theta$ and equal to $1$ on $\operatorname{supp}(1-\theta)$, so $bW=W$. By F5 choose $W_j\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^n;\mathbb C)$ converging to $W$ in $W^{1,2}(\mathbb R^n)$, using the countable-choice consequence of F1. F7 gives convergence in whole-space $H^1$; the fixed multiplier bound in F3 and contractive restriction give $(bW_j)|_\Omega\to(1-\theta)U$ in $H^1(\Omega)$. The restriction bound in step 4.1 also gives convergence in $W^{1,2}(\Omega)$. Each $(bW_j)|_\Omega$ lies in $C_c^\infty(\Omega)$ because $b$ vanishes on a fixed neighborhood of the compact obstacle. Hence the weak-norm closure definition gives $(1-\theta)U\in H^1_0(\Omega)$, and adding $\theta U$ proves the final assertion. This argument proves only the claimed integer zero-trace preservation and ordinary $H^s$ gluing; it does not impose any additional higher normal trace condition. [F1, F2, F3, F5, F7, step 4.1] ∎


## Collar traces and extension across an artificial boundary

[Open item](item-lem-se-collar-traces-and-partial-extension) · level 12


### Statement

Assume AC, let $n\ge2$, and let $1\le s\le2$. Throughout, use complex Bessel-potential Sobolev spaces: for $d\ge1$ and $0\le t\le2$, $H^t(\mathbb R^d)$ consists of tempered distributions $U$ whose distributional product $(1+|\xi|^2)^{t/2}\widehat U$ is represented by an $L^2$ function, with norm equal to the $L^2$ norm of that function. The Fourier transform has negative sign and $2\pi$ normalization. For an open set $D\subset\mathbb R^d$, use the restriction space and quotient norm
$$H^t(D)=\{U|_D:U\in H^t(\mathbb R^d)\},\qquad \|u\|_{H^t(D)}=\inf_{U|_D=u}\|U\|_{H^t(\mathbb R^d)}.$$

For a compact smooth embedded hypersurface $S\subset\mathbb R^n$ without boundary, equip $S$ with its induced smooth structure and define $H^r(S)$, $1/2\le r\le3/2$, by a finite compatible smooth atlas $\kappa_i:U_i\to V_i\subset\mathbb R^{n-1}$ and a subordinate smooth partition $\chi_i$ supported compactly inside the chart domains. For $f\in L^2(S)$ let $f_i=(\chi_i f)\circ\kappa_i^{-1}$, extended by zero outside $V_i$, and set $\|f\|_{H^r(S)}^2=\sum_i\|f_i\|_{H^r(\mathbb R^{n-1})}^2$. These norms are independent of the chosen compatible finite atlas and partition up to equivalence. At order $3/2$ this squared norm is equivalent to $\sum_i(\|f_i\|_{H^{1/2}}^2+\sum_{a=1}^{n-1}\|\partial_a f_i\|_{H^{1/2}}^2)$. Thus the space is equivalently described by the lower-order norm and order-$1/2$ first tangential weak derivatives of localized representatives, without choosing a global tangent frame.

Let $G\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a bounded smooth domain and let $S$ be a smooth boundary component. Smooth restriction extends to a bounded linear trace $\gamma_S:H^s(G)\to H^{s-1/2}(S)$, agreeing in $L^2(S)$ with the first-order trace. For the interior version, let $U\subset\mathbb R^n$ be open, let $S\subset U$ be a compact smooth embedded hypersurface without boundary, and fix a bounded smooth collar/neighborhood $W\Subset U$ containing $S$. Restriction to $W$ followed by its interior trace gives a bounded linear map $H^s(U)\to H^{s-1/2}(S)$. The constants may depend on this fixed collar and its finite atlas. The traces are local, commute with smooth cutoffs, and are independent of extensions and local sides.

For the extension assertions, let $\Theta\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a compact smooth obstacle, with connected smooth exterior $\Omega=\mathbb R^n\setminus\Theta$. Write $B_L=\{x\in\mathbb R^n:|x|<L\}$, choose $L>0$ with $\Theta\Subset B_L$, and put $G_0=\Omega\cap B_L$; its artificial boundary is $\partial B_L$. If $v\in H^s(G_0)$ vanishes on a neighborhood of $\partial B_L$ inside $G_0$, define $\widehat v=v$ on $G_0$ and $\widehat v=0$ on $\Omega\setminus G_0$. Then $\widehat v\in H^s(\Omega)$. For each fixed outer gap and separating cutoff this partial zero extension is bounded, with constants allowed to depend on that gap/cutoff. No vanishing near $\partial\Theta$ is required, and no uniform bound for shrinking gaps is asserted. Here the hat on $v$ denotes partial zero extension, whereas the hat on $U$ in the Fourier convention above denotes Fourier transformation.

For bounded gluing, fix $0<\varepsilon<L/3$ with $\Theta\subset B_{L-3\varepsilon}$. Fix smooth cutoffs $\zeta,\widetilde\zeta$ with bounded derivatives, with $\zeta=0$ on $B_{L-2\varepsilon}$, $\zeta=1$ for $|x|\ge L-\varepsilon$, $\widetilde\zeta=0$ on $B_{L-3\varepsilon}$, and $\widetilde\zeta=1$ on $\operatorname{supp}\zeta$. Put $\mathcal A=\{L-3\varepsilon<|x|<L\}$ and fix one simultaneous bounded linear extension $E_{\mathcal A}:H^t(\mathcal A)\to H^t(\mathbb R^n)$, $0\le t\le2$, agreeing with its input on $\mathcal A$. Then
$$\mathcal G u=\widehat{(1-\zeta)u}+\left.\widetilde\zeta E_{\mathcal A}((\zeta u)|_{\mathcal A})\right|_\Omega$$
is a bounded linear map $H^s(G_0)\to H^s(\Omega)$ with $(\mathcal G u)|_{G_0}=u$ and $\gamma_{\partial\Theta}\mathcal G u=\gamma_{\partial\Theta}u$. In particular it preserves zero obstacle trace; it does not zero-extend across the obstacle. All gluing data are fixed.

### Facts & Assumptions

**Given:** The hypotheses and conventions of the Statement.

[F1] Assume the Axiom of Choice [math:def-axiom-of-choice](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/def-axiom-of-choice); in particular its countable and dependent instances are available. The assumption is inherited in applying the norm, trace and extension suppliers below.

[F2] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-hypersurface-atlas-and-derivatives](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-hypersurface-atlas-and-derivatives) defines and proves the finite-atlas Sobolev norm, its independence, completeness and the order-$3/2$ local tangential-derivative description for compact smooth embedded hypersurfaces without boundary and compatible atlases.

[F3] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-smooth-collar-trace-assembly](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-smooth-collar-trace-assembly) proves the asserted $H^s\to H^{s-1/2}$ boundary and compact interior trace bounds for $1\le s\le2$, with restriction-quotient domain spaces, canonical $L^2$ agreement with the first-order trace, locality and cutoff commutation. The interior assertion uses a fixed bounded smooth collar/neighborhood contained in the ambient open region.

[F4] [lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-artificial-sphere-extension-and-gluing](item-lem-se-author-8d28f6f38ee5-artificial-sphere-extension-and-gluing) proves partial zero extension for $v\in H^s(G_0)$ with an outer gap, and proves the displayed fixed-cutoff annular gluing formula, its restriction identity and preservation of the inner obstacle trace under the stated geometry. It never zero-extends a general $H^2$ function across the obstacle from its value trace alone.

[F5] [lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction](item-lem-se-sobolev-extension-retraction) supplies the restriction-quotient Bessel convention and simultaneous bounded extensions for bounded smooth domains, including $G$, $W$, $G_0$ and $\mathcal A$, and supplies the smooth exterior localization. In particular its annular extension can be chosen as $E_{\mathcal A}$ in the Statement.

[F6] [math:thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces](https://alphabetamath.cc/item/thm-fourier-characterisation-of-fractional-hilbert-sobolev-spaces) identifies the whole-space Bessel spaces with weighted Fourier tempered distributions with the stated norm and negative-sign $2\pi$ transform for all real orders and all positive dimensions. Its Countable Choice hypothesis is available under AC.

### Proof

**Given:** The hypotheses and data in the Statement.

**Goal:** Establish every assertion of the Statement with the stated spaces and bounds.

1.1 The Fourier characterization gives precisely the whole-space spaces and norms fixed in the Statement. The restriction-quotient convention and simultaneous bounded extensions on the stated smooth domains are those of the extension/retraction supplier. In particular fix its extension for the smooth bounded annulus $\mathcal A$. AC supplies the choice hypotheses of these interfaces and of the following atlas and trace suppliers. [F1, F5, F6, given]

2.1 Apply the hypersurface atlas lemma to each compact smooth embedded hypersurface in the Statement, with its induced smooth structure and compatible finite atlas. It gives the stated norm, atlas independence and completeness. Its Fourier derivative calculation identifies the $H^{3/2}$ norm with the lower $H^{1/2}$ norm plus first local coordinate derivatives in $H^{1/2}$, and its transition argument makes this tangential description intrinsic without a global tangent frame. [F2, step 1.1, given]

2.2 The given compact-obstacle exterior, $\Theta\Subset B_L$, order $1\le s\le2$ and input $v\in H^s(G_0)$ with an outer vanishing neighborhood satisfy the partial-extension supplier's hypotheses. Its separating cutoff in a fixed outer gap represents $\widehat v$ as an ordinary whole-space extension multiplied by that cutoff and restricted to $\Omega$. Hence $\widehat v\in H^s(\Omega)$ with a bound for each fixed gap/cutoff. This restricts away the obstacle and requires no vanishing of $v$ near $\partial\Theta$. The supplier retains the gap dependence. [F4, F5, step 1.1, given]

3.1 Apply the collar trace theorem to the bounded smooth $G$ and its smooth boundary component. It gives the claimed bounded trace for $1\le s\le2$ with the finite-atlas target. For the interior case the given compact smooth hypersurface and fixed bounded smooth $W\Subset U$ meet the same supplier's interior hypotheses. Restriction $H^s(U)\to H^s(W)$ cannot increase the quotient norm, since each whole-space representative on $U$ is also a representative on $W$. Composing restriction with that fixed interior trace gives the claimed bound, with its allowed collar dependence. The supplier's canonical $L^2$ agreement and locality give consistency with the boundary trace from either local side and independence of extensions and charts, as well as cutoff commutation. [F3, F5, step 1.1, step 2.1, given]

4.1 The fixed $\varepsilon$, cutoffs, annulus and simultaneous $E_{\mathcal A}$ satisfy exactly the gluing hypotheses of the same supplier. Thus its gluing map is the displayed $\mathcal G$, bounded from $H^s(G_0)$ to $H^s(\Omega)$. On the annulus its second term restricts to $\widetilde\zeta\zeta u=\zeta u$, and below the annulus $\zeta=0$, so the sum restricts to $u$ on $G_0$. Near the obstacle, $\zeta=\widetilde\zeta=0$, so the first term equals $u$ there and the correction vanishes. Canonical trace locality therefore gives equality of the obstacle traces, including preservation of zero trace. These are partial extension and gluing across the artificial sphere with the fixed data of the Statement. [F3, F4, step 3.1, step 2.2, given, algebra] ∎

### Source notes

The restriction-quotient convention and simultaneous extensions are supplied by the Sobolev extension/retraction result, the whole-space Fourier model by the Fourier characterization, and the intrinsic hypersurface norm by the atlas lemma. The collar trace and artificial-sphere gluing lemmas supply the stated trace and extension conclusions for the specified geometry. Constants retain their fixed collar, atlas, gap and cutoff dependence.
